## 2026-07-11 — S10 Completion

### A34 — Clean-build CI-breaking defect: `tsc -b` project-reference graph had silently drifted from the real package.json dependency graph in FOUR packages, not the two independent verification named; structural guard added

**Reproduction (concrete, not inferred).** From a genuinely clean state (`pnpm run clean && pnpm run build`), the build failed with exit code 2 and 20 error lines (13 `TS2307` "Cannot find module", the rest cascading `TS7006`/`TS7031`/`TS2345` inference failures downstream of the unresolved modules):

```
packages/server/src/promotion.ts(38,8): error TS2307: Cannot find module '@aart/governance'...
packages/mcp/src/real-context.ts(53,76): error TS2307: Cannot find module '@aart/registry'...
packages/mcp/src/real-context.ts(54,58): error TS2307: Cannot find module '@aart/llm'...
packages/cli/src/*.ts (7 files): error TS2307: Cannot find module '@aart/mcp'...
packages/dashboard/src/stub-deps.ts(39,53): error TS2307: Cannot find module '@aart/server'...
(+ 6 cascading TS7006/TS7031/TS2345 errors in mcp/cli, downstream of the above)
```

**Root cause.** `tsc -b` derives build order from each package's OWN `tsconfig.json` `"references"` array — never from `package.json` `"dependencies"`, and never from the flat, unordered list in root `tsconfig.build.json` (that file is a build-orchestration entry point, not a dependency graph). Two packages had a real `package.json` dependency with no matching `tsconfig.json` reference: `packages/server` (depends on `@aart/governance`, referenced neither) and `packages/mcp` (depends on six workspace packages — `@aart/blocks-core`, `@aart/engine`, `@aart/evidence`, `@aart/governance`, `@aart/llm`, `@aart/registry` — referenced none of them). `packages/cli` (references `@aart/mcp` correctly) and `packages/dashboard` (references `@aart/server` correctly) then failed too, purely as a downstream cascade of `mcp`/`server` themselves never emitting `dist/*.d.ts`.

**Found beyond what independent verification named — the same defect class, two more instances:** `packages/engine/tsconfig.json` and `packages/evidence/tsconfig.json` had **no `"references"` key at all**, despite both packages' `src/` genuinely importing from `@aart/expr`/`@aart/store`/`@aart/types` (`engine`) and `@aart/store`/`@aart/types` (`evidence`) — confirmed by grepping their non-test source, not assumed. Neither surfaced as a `TS2307` in the reproduction above, purely by luck: root `tsconfig.build.json`'s reference list happens to position `types`/`store`/`expr` before `engine`, and before `governance`/`mcp` before `evidence`, so `tsc -b`'s scheduler built their real prerequisites first anyway despite no declared edge requiring it. This is exactly the fragility the independent verification's own framing warned about ("warm dist masks it") generalized one step further: even a clean build can pass or fail on pure incidental list order rather than a correct graph. Both fixed alongside the two named packages (added the matching `"references"` entries; verified each addition against the package's real non-test imports, not copied blind from `dependencies`).

**Fix.** Four `tsconfig.json` files updated (`server`, `mcp`, `engine`, `evidence`) — each now lists a `references` entry for every workspace package its own `src/` actually imports, matching its `package.json` `dependencies`. Verified: `pnpm run clean && pnpm run build` now exits 0 from cold, as does `pnpm run typecheck` (`tsc -b --force`) and `pnpm run typecheck:tests`.

**Structural guard, not just the patch — new `scripts/check-tsconfig-references.mjs` + `pnpm run check:tsconfig-refs`, wired into CI as a BLOCKING step before `Build`.** Asserts, for all 14 packages: (1) forward — every `@aart/*` entry in `package.json` `dependencies` has a matching `tsconfig.json` reference (this is the defect class above; blocking); (2) reverse — every `tsconfig.json` reference corresponds to *some* declared relationship in `package.json` (`dependencies` or `devDependencies`); a reference matching neither is an orphaned/stale reference (blocking); a reference matching a `devDependency` only is printed as a non-blocking informational note, since that's a legitimate, already-established pattern in this repo (see A31: `@aart/llm`'s `devDependency` on `@aart/engine`, test-file-only, added deliberately alongside its own matching `tsconfig.json` reference) — a package used only from an excluded `*.test.ts` file is correctly a `devDependency` and its reference isn't load-bearing for `tsc -b`, so hard-failing on it would be a false positive, not a real defect. Both directions verified against real regressions, not just the passing case: temporarily reintroduced the original `server`→`governance` gap (script caught it, exit 1) and a fully-orphaned reference with no `package.json` entry of either kind (script caught it, exit 1), then restored both files and re-confirmed a clean pass before committing anything.

Tolerates the one piece of JSONC already in this repo (`packages/blocks-core/tsconfig.json`'s `//` comments) via a small string-literal-aware comment stripper — a naive substring strip would have truncated `"$schema": "https://json.schemastore.org/tsconfig"` at its own `//`.

**This defect class has now bitten this repo three ways** before finally surfacing as a real clean-build failure: a warm `dist/` masking a missing reference indefinitely; pnpm's workspace `node_modules` symlinks making a module resolvable on disk regardless of the project-reference graph; and (a related but distinct near-miss, A31) test files going completely unchecked by any `tsc` pass at all until this session's predecessor added `typecheck:tests`. The guard closes the specific graph-mismatch class going forward — a future PR that adds a workspace dependency without the matching `tsconfig.json` reference now fails in one legible line at the `check:tsconfig-refs` CI step, before `Build` ever runs, instead of as a cascading wall of `TS2307`s someone has to reverse-engineer back to its root cause.

### A35 — A33 fixed: `packages/cli` now bundles its entire workspace-internal closure with esbuild; ADR-18's own cross-reference for the mechanism was wrong, corrected here

**ADR-18 mechanism correction.** A33 found that ADR-18 requires `packages/cli` to "bundle the runtime it depends on" but cites the wrong existing mechanism for it — "the existing bundle design (§0.3, ADR-04)" is `aart bundle`, the workflow-deployment artifact (`packages/server/src/bundle/**`: manifest + workflow/block/pack definitions for Docker/K8s/serverless deployment of a *workflow run*). That mechanism has nothing to do with bundling this CLI *package's own npm dependency graph* for publication — different input, different output, different consumer. The actual mechanism, implemented in this entry: an esbuild-based JS bundle step, new, with no prior name in either source document. Treat ADR-18's "§0.3, ADR-04" cross-reference as superseded by this entry for the specific claim "packages/cli bundles the runtime it depends on" — §0.3/ADR-04 remains completely correct and unchanged for its actual subject, workflow-deployment bundles.

**Decision (the shape A33 flagged as needing one, made here):** bundle ALL workspace-internal `@aart/*` code into `dist/bin.js` and `dist/index.js` with esbuild; leave EVERY third-party (non-`@aart/*`) import external — real `dependencies` in the published `package.json`, never inlined. This is narrower than "bundle everything reachable": third-party packages are already independently installable, normal npm packages; inlining them buys nothing and costs real risk — `playwright` is known not to survive bundling (it locates its own driver/browser assets via runtime `require.resolve`, per A33's own note) and `isolated-vm` is a native `.node` addon a JS bundler cannot text-inline at all, full stop. Scoping the bundle to exactly the `@aart/*` closure solves exactly the problem A33 found and nothing else — it also sidesteps a real landmine found while implementing this: `yaml` resolves to two different versions in this workspace (`blocks-core` pins `2.8.1` exactly; `registry` wants `^2.6.1`, resolving to `2.9.0` via a transitive pull — confirmed in `pnpm-lock.yaml`). Inlining both would have silently picked whichever one `esbuild` happened to bundle per call site; leaving `yaml` external instead means both call sites share one real, single installed copy at the consumer's install time — declared as `"yaml": "2.8.1"` in the published `package.json` (satisfies both origin constraints; the isolated-install proof below exercises both call sites for real and both work).

**New `packages/cli/scripts/build-publish.mjs`, wired as `pnpm --filter @team-monet/aart run build:publish` (`tsc -b && node scripts/build-publish.mjs`).** Bundles `src/bin.ts` -> `dist/bin.js` (shebang preserved — esbuild hoists a `#!` first line automatically, verified directly) and `src/index.ts` -> `dist/index.js`, `platform: "node"`, `format: "esm"`, `target: "node22"`. The external list (`@anthropic-ai/sdk`, `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` + subpath wildcard, `ajv`, `isolated-vm`, `js-yaml`, `playwright`, `yaml`, `zod`) was derived by hand from every `package.json` in `packages/*`'s transitive closure, then verified empirically against `esbuild`'s own `metafile` output rather than trusted blind — the script fails the build loudly if the bundle ever actually imports a third-party package `package.json` `"dependencies"` doesn't declare (reproducing A33's bug for a new package), and separately fails if any `@aart/*` specifier survives as an external import (the bundle's entire reason to exist). Both checks exercised as real regressions, not just the passing case, same discipline as A34's own guard script.

`packages/cli/package.json` `"dependencies"` replaced: was the three private packages (`@aart/mcp`, `@aart/store`, `@aart/types`); now the eight real third-party packages above, with exact/range versions matching what each contributing `@aart/*` package already declares. Those three workspace packages didn't disappear from `package.json` — they moved to `"devDependencies"` (still `workspace:*`, still needed for pnpm to symlink them so `tsc -b`/`esbuild` can resolve them from source at *build* time), which is exactly what `devDependencies` means: needed to build this package, never installed as a dependency of a project that merely *consumes* it. Confirmed directly, not assumed: the isolated-install proof below shows zero `@aart/*` packages present in `node_modules` after install (`devDependencies` of an installed package are never fetched by a normal `npm install <package>`) and zero E404s despite the packed tarball's manifest still literally listing `"@aart/mcp": "0.1.0"` etc. under `devDependencies` (harmless — this is the standard, common shape of most published npm packages' manifests, not something needing further suppression).

Also added: `dist/`'s dead-file cleanup (every per-file `.js`/`.js.map` `tsc -b` emits, e.g. `dist/cli.js`, `dist/commands/*.js` — now-unreachable now that `bin.js`/`index.js` are self-contained bundles, but would otherwise still get published per `"files": ["dist"]`) — removed by `build-publish.mjs` after bundling, `.d.ts`/`.d.ts.map` deliberately left completely alone (still exactly what `"types": "./dist/index.d.ts"` resolves through).

**Proof — reproduced A33's own exact methodology, now green.** `pnpm pack` in `packages/cli` (real tarball, `team-monet-aart-0.1.0.tgz`), installed via plain `npm install <tarball-path>` in a freshly created directory outside this repo entirely (`npm init -y` first, zero access to this workspace's `node_modules`):

```
$ npm install /Users/johnlee/code/aart/packages/cli/team-monet-aart-0.1.0.tgz
npm warn EBADENGINE Unsupported engine { package: 'isolated-vm@7.0.0', required: { node: '>=26.0.0' }, current: { node: 'v22.22.2', npm: '10.9.7' } }
added 105 packages, and audited 106 packages in 39s
```

No E404 (A33's original failure). Then ran the installed binary directly (`node_modules/.bin/aart`, correctly `chmod 755` by `npm install`), both commands A33's own remediation note names:

```
$ ./node_modules/.bin/aart list
{ "ok": true, "workflows": [] }

$ ./node_modules/.bin/aart init-agent
{ "ok": true, "mcpConfigPath": ".../.mcp.json", "instructionsPath": ".../AGENTS.md" }
```

Both real command dispatches through the fully bundled `@aart/mcp`-derived logic, not stubs — `init-agent` actually wrote a correct `.mcp.json` (`{"mcpServers":{"aart":{"command":"npx","args":["-y","@team-monet/aart","mcp"]}}}`) and a real, complete `AGENTS.md`. (`aart --version` isn't a real command in this CLI's surface — the `USAGE` string has no such flag; substituted `aart list`, a genuinely read-only command, as the brief's own "(or equivalent read-only command)" allowance anticipated.) `find node_modules -iname "@aart*"` after install: zero matches — confirms the `devDependencies` reasoning above empirically, not just by argument.

**Honest disclosures, not claimed away:**
1. **`dist/index.d.ts` still verbatim re-exports 9 handler symbols `from "@aart/mcp"`** (`approveHandler`, `deployWorkflowHandler`, etc. — `tsc`'s normal declaration-emit behavior for a named re-export, unrelated to `verbatimModuleSyntax`). Zero runtime effect (`.d.ts` is erased before execution — this is exactly why the isolated-install proof above, which only exercises runtime behavior, is unaffected). A TypeScript consumer who both imports the package **and** specifically imports one of those 9 re-exported names would see an unresolvable-type reference, since `@aart/mcp` is never installed for them. Not fixed here — doing so properly needs a `.d.ts` bundler (e.g. `dts-bundle-generator`/`rollup-plugin-dts`), a real added tool and a different kind of change than the runtime-bundling problem this entry actually fixes; out of scope for what A33 reproduced (an install/runtime failure, not a downstream type-check failure).
2. **`isolated-vm@7.0.0` declares `engines.node: ">=26.0.0"`**, surfaced by the `npm warn EBADENGINE` above — this repo's own root `package.json` declares `"engines": { "node": ">=22" }` and this session runs Node v22.22.2. Pre-existing (isolated-vm has been a real transitive dependency via `@aart/engine` since long before this session; it simply had no path to ever being *installed standalone* until this fix made the CLI installable at all, which is what finally surfaced the warning). Not a new bug introduced here, not fixed here — a real, now-visible engine-floor question for a follow-up session, not a mechanical fix.
3. Full `packages/cli` test suite (`pnpm --filter @team-monet/aart test`) and the whole-repo suite both re-run green after this change (see this session's final report) — but note they exercise `src/` directly via vitest's own transform, not the `dist/` bundle; the isolated-install proof above is what actually exercises the bundled artifact end-to-end, deliberately, since that's the thing A33 found broken.

### A36 — S9 Phase 3 adversarial security findings F1/F2/F4/F5 resolved (redaction chokepoint); F5's fix generalizes the codebase's own existing "can't redact pixels" posture rather than inventing a new one

All four findings from `packages/governance/src/redact-adversarial.test.ts` (F1, F2, F4) and `packages/mcp/src/redaction-adversarial.test.ts` (F5) are fixed. Every adversarial test that documented a finding is relabeled and now asserts the fixed behavior — see each test's own comment for the exact before/after. Full list of flipped/added test names at the end of this entry.

**F1 — a resolved secret value used as an OBJECT KEY was never scanned.** `applyReplacements` (`packages/governance/src/redact.ts`) recursed into object VALUES only (`out[key] = applyReplacements(v, ...)`, copying `key` verbatim) — a realistic trigger is a group-by/index-by aggregation keyed on a field whose value is a resolved secret (`{ [secretToken]: [...] }`). Fix: the object branch now runs the key through the same `applyStringReplacements` core every string field already used, via a small refactor that extracts that core into its own function (also reused by F2's number/boolean branch below). Still not a field-NAME allowlist (this codebase's own hard rule, ADR-10's rejected-alternative rationale, both unchanged) — nothing inspects what a key IS, only whether its literal text contains a resolved secret VALUE, the identical test every value already got. On the vanishingly rare chance two distinct original keys redact to the identical string, the later write simply wins, same as any plain-JS duplicate-key collision — a data-fidelity nicety, not a security property (the secret not leaking is unaffected either way).

**F2 — redaction was string-only, in TWO compounding ways, both fixed:**
1. **Governance layer** (`redact.ts`'s `applyReplacements`): a resolved secret coinciding with a NUMBER or BOOLEAN record field (e.g. a block that parses a numeric OTP/PIN and returns `{ code: 782341 }` as a real `number`, not a string) was never scanned — the function had no branch for these types at all, only `string`/`Array`/`object`. Fix: a new branch stringifies the value, scans it through the same core, and — only if something actually matched — returns the redacted STRING; an unmatched number/boolean passes through completely unchanged (same type, same value), so this doesn't gratuitously turn every numeric field in every record into a string. When a secret genuinely is a number this DOES change that field's type from number to string in the persisted record — documented as a deliberate side effect (there is no numeric way to spell `[REDACTED:...]`, and leaking the secret is not the alternative).
2. **Engine layer, the compounding cause** (`packages/engine/src/redaction.ts`'s `createTrackingSecretResolver`): `SecretResolver` is typed `(name: string) => unknown`, but only `typeof value === "string"` resolved values were ever added to the tracked-refs set — a resolver that legitimately returns a raw number/boolean/bigint never entered the scan set AT ALL, so even fixing (1) alone would have nothing to search for. Fix: a new `toCanonicalSecretString` helper stringifies number/boolean/bigint canonical forms into the set at the moment of resolution. Deliberately excludes `null`/`undefined` (tracking the literal string `"null"`/`"undefined"` would redact every ordinary null-shaped field in every run — a catastrophic over-redaction bug, not a fix) and excludes objects/arrays (a composite isn't itself a flat scalar to string-match against).

**F4 — overlapping secrets were applied in Set-insertion order, no longest-match-first sort.** If a SHORTER secret that is a substring of a LONGER one was resolved first, its pattern got tried first, consuming the shared substring and leaving the longer secret's non-overlapping fragment (e.g. a `"prefix-"` portion) exposed in the persisted record. Fix: `Replacement` now carries a `literalLength` (the actual matchable substring length — deliberately NOT `pattern.source.length`, which `escapeForRegex` can inflate, e.g. `"."` -> `"\."` doubles it, the wrong quantity to sort by), and both `redactRecord` and `redactRecordWithNames` sort the full replacements array by `literalLength` descending before `applyReplacements` ever runs — longest match always gets first crack at the text, independent of insertion order. Marker NUMBERS are unaffected (still assigned by Set-insertion order, preserving "same secret value -> same marker within one call"); only which pattern is TRIED first changed. Because F1's key-scan and F2's number/boolean-scan both route through the same shared `applyStringReplacements` core against the same sorted array, this ordering fix covers all three automatically, not just the original plain-string case.

**F5 (priority) — artifact bytes bypassed the redaction chokepoint entirely.** `ctx.writeArtifact` (`packages/engine/src/step-executor.ts`'s `buildBlockContext`) called `config.store.artifacts.put()` directly with the block's raw bytes — the one persist path in this package that never routed through `applyRedaction`; RunRecord/StepTrace/wait-checkpoint persistence all did. Fix generalizes this codebase's own existing, pre-fix posture (`packages/blocks-core/src/browser/screenshot.ts`'s `maskSelectors` comment: "text-based redaction doesn't work on a bitmap") from one block to every artifact write, split on declared MIME type:
- **TEXT-typed artifacts** (new `isTextMime` in `packages/engine/src/redaction.ts`: `text/*`, `application/json`, `+json`, `application/xml`, `+xml`) decode as UTF-8, pass through the SAME `applyRedaction`/`resolvedSecretRefs` chokepoint every other persist call site uses, and re-encode before `store.artifacts.put`. Redaction runs against the LIVE `resolvedSecretRefs` set at the moment `writeArtifact` is actually called (not a snapshot taken before dispatch), so a block that resolves a secret and then writes an artifact within the same `execute()` call is covered.
- **BINARY-typed artifacts** (everything else — screenshots, downloads, PDFs) are deliberately NOT scan-redacted. There is no text to scan-and-replace in a bitmap or arbitrary binary blob; attempting to would either silently do nothing (the secret's bytes are encoded/compressed/rendered, not present as a literal substring) or corrupt the artifact outright. **The control for binary content is not "redact on write"** — it's (a) prevention at the point of capture where that's possible (`browser.screenshot`'s pre-existing `maskSelectors`, which blacks out a region BEFORE the bitmap is ever created — unchanged by this fix, already correct), and (b) the fact that nothing in this codebase's production code ever reads artifact bytes back automatically. Verified directly, not assumed: `grep`'d every call site of `ArtifactStore.getBytes` across the entire repo — the ONLY callers anywhere are test code (this session's own new binary-artifact test, and `store/src/conformance.ts`'s adapter-conformance suite); zero production call sites. Every report/MCP-tool-result this codebase builds references an artifact by METADATA only (id, path, kind, mime, byte count — `@aart/evidence`'s `report-model.ts`), never inlined bytes. Reading a binary artifact's real content back is necessarily a deliberate, separate, explicit act — this is "the sensitive-read suppression mechanism" being the control instead of scan-redaction: nothing automatically surfaces the bytes, so there is nothing for a passive/lower-trust reader to be exposed to. This boundary is documented at the write site itself (`buildBlockContext`'s doc comment), not just here.

**Test names flipped (all now pass; full suites re-verified green, see this session's final report):**
- `packages/governance/src/redact-adversarial.test.ts`: `[GAP: F1]` x2 -> `[SAFE: F1]` x2; `[GAP: F2]` x1 -> `[SAFE: F2]` x2 (added one boolean case, proving the fix generalizes beyond numbers); `[GAP: F4]` x2 -> `[SAFE: F4]` x2. The `[LIMIT]` cases (base64/hex/case-fold — inherent to value-scan redaction, not fixed, not claimed fixed) are untouched.
- `packages/engine/src/redaction.test.ts`: 4 new cases on `createTrackingSecretResolver` — canonical-string tracking of a number and a boolean, and confirming `null` and a plain object are still correctly NOT tracked (same reasoning as the pre-existing `undefined` case).
- `packages/mcp/src/redaction-adversarial.test.ts`: `[FINDING: F5]` x1 -> `[SAFE: F5]` (the original test's own `text/plain` fixture, now actually redacted) + `[LIMIT: F5]` x1 new (a parallel binary-mime fixture, proving the documented boundary holds, not just asserted in prose).

### A37 — Found while building the mid-step worker-kill E2E (plan §4): `createRealEngineBoundary` was claimed to exist since S9 integration, but never actually did — `aart worker`/`aart server` were complete no-ops in the shipping CLI. Built for real; the worker-kill E2E now proves the real machinery, not a workaround.

**Discovery.** Building a faithful "SIGKILL a real worker process mid-step, prove architecture §4.7's lease/reclaim machinery recovers it" E2E requires a REAL `@aart/server` `startWorker` wired to a REAL `@aart/engine` `Engine`. `packages/mcp/src/real-context.ts`'s own module header claims this wiring already happened: "ServerPort (CLI-only) <- @aart/server's real startServer/startWorker/... via a real EngineBoundary adapter over the same real Engine instance (packages/server/src/engine/boundary.ts's createRealEngineBoundary)." That function did not exist anywhere in the repo — confirmed by `grep`, one match total (this same comment) — and `packages/server/src/engine/boundary.ts` contained only `createFakeEngine`. Traced further: `packages/cli/src/cli-context.ts` unconditionally calls `createStubServerPort` (`packages/cli/src/stubs/server.ts`) regardless of anything else in the composition being real — and that stub's own `startWorker` is `async (_options) => ({ async stop() {} })`, a complete no-op. `startServer` similarly returns a fake port with no real HTTP server; `produceBundle` writes a "minimal, structurally-correct" fake bundle rather than the real transitive-closure computation. **The real, shipping CLI's `aart worker` and `aart server` commands currently do nothing** — a previously-unflagged gap (SEAMS.md's own "stub-swap points" table lists `ServerPort` as one of five S9 was supposed to reconcile at merge time; the other four — `EnginePort`/`GovernancePort`/`EvidencePort`/`RegistryPort` — genuinely were, verified directly in `real-context.ts`; `ServerPort` was not, despite the comment claiming otherwise).

**Fix — `createRealEngineBoundary(store, engine)` built for real in `packages/server/src/engine/boundary.ts`.** A thin adapter, matching `createFakeEngine`'s sibling shape exactly: `startRun` resolves the target workflow (latest version if unpinned) and calls `engine.triggerRun`, mapping `ConcurrencyRejectedError` to `{kind: "rejected"}` and reading the `waitingOnConcurrency` flag `run-lifecycle.ts` stashes in `RunRecord.params` to distinguish `"started"` from `"queued"`; `resumeWithSignal`/`resumeDirect` call the real `Engine` resume methods, mapping a thrown `CorrelationError` (wait-machine.ts's real signal for ">1 outstanding wait matched") to `{kind: "ambiguous", matches}` using the error's own `detail.matches` array; `getDueWaits` and `executeClaimedRun` pass straight through to `engine.getDueWaits`/`engine.executeRun` (the latter is literally "what a worker calls after claiming a run from job_queue," per `Engine.executeRun`'s own doc comment). `@aart/server` did not previously depend on `@aart/engine` at all (only the fake needed no such dependency) — added to `package.json` `dependencies` + the matching `tsconfig.json` reference (A34's guard caught nothing here only because I added both together, deliberately). Exported from `@aart/server`'s public `index.ts` alongside `createFakeEngine`. 7 new unit tests in `packages/server/src/engine/boundary.test.ts` against a real fs-backed store + real `Engine` (not mocks) — `startRun` (latest-version resolution, rejected-workflow, reject_new-policy rejection), `resumeDirect`/`resumeWithSignal` (no_match), `getDueWaits` (passthrough), `executeClaimedRun` (a pending run genuinely executes and completes).

**Not fixed here — flagged for a deliberate decision, matching this file's own established discipline (A33's framing).** Wiring `createRealEngineBoundary` into `packages/cli/src/cli-context.ts` (so the shipping `aart worker`/`aart server` genuinely work) needs a full real `Engine` instance constructed inside the CLI's own composition root — but `cli-context.ts` currently only has access to `@aart/mcp`'s narrower `EnginePort` (via `createAartContext`), which lacks `getDueWaits`/`cancelRun`/`resumeTimerWait`/`resumeExternalJobResult` and the others `EngineBoundary` needs. Threading a full `Engine` into the CLI means either broadening `@aart/mcp`'s public export surface or duplicating engine construction outside it — a genuine architecture-level call (which composition root owns constructing the real `Engine`, and how much of it the CLI should see) squarely outside a mechanical fix, same category as A33's own bundler-choice deferral. `produceBundle`/`writeBundleToDisk`'s real implementations (`packages/server/src/bundle/bundle.js`) also already exist and are NOT wired into the CLI either (`ServerPort.produceBundle`'s `environment?: string` doesn't match the real function's `deployment?: Deployment` parameter — needs a name-to-`Deployment` lookup to bridge) — noted here, not attempted, same reasoning. **This blocks `aart worker`/`aart server`/`aart bundle` from doing anything real in the currently-shipping CLI** — a genuine, real, now-clearly-documented gap for a deliberate follow-up, not silently left undiscovered.

**The E2E test itself (`packages/server/src/e2e/worker-kill.e2e.test.ts` + `worker-kill-worker.mjs`, same real-separate-OS-process/SIGKILL discipline as `packages/mcp/src/e2e/redacted-legacy-b.e2e.test.ts`) uses `createRealEngineBoundary` directly** (bypassing the CLI layer entirely, same reasoning `redacted-legacy-b-worker.mjs` already established for bypassing `@aart/mcp`'s public index in favor of a fuller relative import) — this is the first real-process proof of the job_queue lease/reclaim machinery specifically, distinct from `redacted-legacy-b`'s own real-process proof (which kills a worker only ever at a WAIT boundary, after a checkpoint already durably persisted, never exercising `job_queue`'s claim loop at all). Sequence, all real, no mocked clock: trigger a run (enqueues, does not execute) -> spawn worker process A (real `startWorker`) -> wait for the fixture block's own `"step-started"` event (genuinely inside `execute()`, mid-30-second-sleep) -> genuine `SIGKILL` -> confirm the on-disk `RunRecord` has ZERO trace entries for the killed step (proof this is mid-step, not between-steps — a wait-boundary kill always leaves a full checkpoint) and the `job_queue` claim is still held by the dead worker -> sleep past the real lease duration -> run the REAL `runReclaimSweep` (normally invoked from the scheduler ticker; the sweep function itself doesn't need to run inside any particular process to be a real proof, only the kill and the recovery do) -> confirm it requeued the run (not exhausted) -> spawn worker process B, a completely fresh process -> confirm it claims the requeued job, re-runs the SAME step from its own start (a marker-file fixture distinguishes "first attempt, sleep" from "retry, complete fast" — the only way a stateless-across-restarts block fixture can model step-level, not instruction-level, retry granularity) -> confirms final `RunRecord.status: "completed"` with exactly one trace entry. Verified stable, not a one-off: 3/3 clean runs, ~2.7s each.

One real bug in my own first draft of this test, not the system under test: I initially asserted the `job_queue` entry is fully removed after normal completion — actually `worker.ts`'s own `executeOneClaim` backstop calls `jobQueue.release` (not `remove`) on completion, since `run-lifecycle.ts`'s finalization never touches `job_queue` at all (only `enqueue`, at trigger time) — job_queue lifecycle past that point is entirely `@aart/server`'s own responsibility. Fixed the assertion to match real, verified behavior, not the assumption. A second, unrelated timing issue in the same first draft: a watcher that reports "run finished" the instant `RunRecord.status` flips to `"completed"` can genuinely race `executeOneClaim`'s own trailing work (a `maybeFlagPoison` store read, then the release call) — fixed by polling for the `job_queue` state to settle rather than asserting a single point-in-time read.

### A38 — Rolling-upgrade / schema-version E2E (plan §4): a real constraint in `assertSchemaVersionCompatible` shaped how "a newer engine process" had to be modeled

**The constraint, found while designing this test, not previously documented anywhere:** `assertSchemaVersionCompatible` (`packages/engine/src/schema-version.ts`) is called from `run-lifecycle.ts`/`wait-machine.ts` WITHOUT a third `engineVersion` argument at every real call site — meaning the check is hardcoded against the literal `CURRENT_ENGINE_SCHEMA_VERSION` constant, and does **not** respect `EngineConfig.schemaVersion` (which only controls what NEW records get *tagged* with at write time, in `triggerRun`/`executeWaitDispatch`). Consequence: configuring `EngineConfig.schemaVersion: 2` to simulate "a newer engine build" breaks that SAME process's own subsequent `executeRun`/resume calls too — a "v2-configured" engine immediately refuses the v2-tagged record it just wrote itself, since its read-time check still compares against the real constant (1), never the config override. Confirmed directly (an earlier draft of the test script hit exactly this).

**What this means for the test, honestly:** a fully faithful "two genuinely different engine builds" simulation would require a real second compiled build with the literal constant bumped (temporarily mutating this repo's source mid-test, rebuilding, then reverting) — fragile, and risky to attempt live in a shared working tree. Instead, `schema-version-runner.mjs` (`packages/engine/src/e2e/`) uses the REAL, unmodified engine to run a step and reach a `wait.manual` checkpoint through 100% real execution, then — only when `--schemaVersion=N` differs from the real constant — re-tags ONLY the `schemaVersion` field on that already-correct, real checkpoint via a direct store write, modeling what a real v-N build's own bumped constant would have stamped on an otherwise-identical run. This is real on the READ/RESUME side (the interesting side for this test — a completely real, unmodified engine, in a completely fresh process, hitting the real hardcoded check) and an honestly-disclosed simplification on the WRITE side.

**Two real process boundaries, two scenarios, both in `packages/engine/src/e2e/schema-version.e2e.test.ts`:**
- COMPATIBLE: a run+wait persisted (schemaVersion 1, the real default) by one process resumes successfully in a completely fresh process running the same real engine.
- INCOMPATIBLE: a run+wait re-tagged to schemaVersion 2 is resumed by a completely fresh process running the real (schemaVersion 1) engine — asserts the exact thrown `SchemaVersionMismatchError`, its message content, and its `detail: {kind: "schemaVersionMismatch", recordVersion: 2, engineVersion: 1}` — **and** the critical safety property: the refused run is left exactly as it was (still `"waiting"`, still tagged 2, trace/wait checkpoint untouched), not half-mutated by the refused attempt.

Two of my own assertions were wrong in the first draft, not the system: I assumed the "before" step alone would be in the trace at the wait checkpoint — the wait step itself (`"pause"`) also gets its own trace entry (`status: "waiting"`, recorded when the wait is entered), so the real trace is `["before", "pause"]`, not `["before"]`. Fixed to match observed, verified behavior. Verified stable: 4/4 clean runs across both scenarios.

### A39 — Verify-loop E2E (plan §4) surfaced TWO real, previously-undiscovered bugs in `aart_verify` (spec §32.6) — both fixed as code

Built `packages/mcp/src/e2e/verify-loop.e2e.test.ts` against the REAL `aart_verify` tool (`verifyHandler`, `handlers/execution.ts`) — real local HTTP server, real headless browser (Playwright, via `@aart/blocks-core`'s real `browser.goto`/`web.read`/`browser.screenshot`/`assert.contains` blocks), real evidence rendering (`createRealAartContext`, not `createAartContext`'s stub composition). Exactly the class of bug per-package/stub testing structurally cannot catch (same story as A27's redaction bug) — `execution.test.ts`'s own existing `verifyHandler` tests all pass today, against the STUB composition, and would keep passing even with both bugs below present, because the stub catalog's simplified `assert.contains` never enforces the real block's Zod schema.

**Bug 1 — `aart_verify`'s `expect` parameter has been completely broken since it was written.** `ensureVerifyWorkflow`'s synthetic `assert.contains` step declared `with: { value: "{{ steps.read.outputs.text }}", expected: "{{ inputs.expect }}" }` — but `assert.contains`'s real, frozen input schema (`packages/blocks-core/src/assert/contains.ts`) requires the field name `actual`, not `value`. Every real call to `aart_verify` with an `expect` string given would fail Zod schema validation on the assert step, unconditionally — regardless of whether the page's content actually matched. Reproduced directly (a debug script against the real composition, not inferred): `assert.contains: input failed schema validation — ... "path": ["actual"], "message": "Invalid input: expected string, received undefined"`. **Fixed**: `value` -> `actual` in `execution.ts`.

**Bug 2 — `RunRecord.artifacts` is set to `[]` at run creation (`run-lifecycle.ts`) and then never updated anywhere in the engine, for the life of the run.** A block calling `ctx.writeArtifact` genuinely persists real bytes to `store.artifacts` (confirmed: `store.artifacts.getBytes` returns real PNG bytes, real `\x89PNG` signature, after a `browser.screenshot` step) — but nothing ever reflected that write back onto the `RunRecord` itself. Consequence: `@aart/evidence`'s `renderModelFacing` (which builds `artifactRefs` from `run.artifacts`) silently reported ZERO artifacts for every run in this entire codebase's history, no matter how many screenshots/downloads/reports a workflow actually captured — a real, significant evidence-completeness gap (an agent calling `aart_verify`/`aart_get_report` had no way to discover a screenshot even existed, let alone retrieve it). **Fixed** in `step-executor.ts`'s `appendTracesAndPersist` — the ONE function every step-completion path already funnels through for trace/redaction — by refreshing `run.artifacts` from `store.artifacts.listByRun(runId)` before each persist. Chosen over threading artifact-collection through `dispatchOnce`'s own closures (the way `capturedLlmCall` works) because by the time `appendTracesAndPersist` runs, `dispatchOnce` has already returned and any artifacts the step wrote are already durably in the store — correct regardless of how many artifacts a step writes (zero, one, or several), no new per-call bookkeeping needed. Full `@aart/engine` suite re-verified green after this change (253 tests, no regressions), and F5's own adversarial artifact-redaction tests (A36) re-verified unaffected.

**The test itself proves the full "verify reflex" loop AGENTS.md's own generated instructions describe** (`aart_verify` -> observe -> revise -> `aart_verify` again): a first call against a page not yet in the expected state genuinely fails (`headline: "failed"`, a real `assert.contains` failure entry) with real evidence, not a rubber stamp; the underlying page then changes; a second, independent `aart_verify` call against the same registered synthetic workflow (reused by design — its version is keyed only on whether `expect` is given, never on `expect`'s value) genuinely passes. Both runs independently, durably on record. Verified stable: 3/3 clean runs across all three test cases (pass path, fail path, the loop itself).

### A40 — Load/soak sanity E2E (plan §4) found a genuine production bug: a worker under real sustained load gets stuck in an infinite re-claim loop on its own already-finished runs, starving every other pending run forever

**This is the most severe bug found this session.** Not a redaction gap, not a broken one-shot tool call — a worker process that, under real multi-run load, permanently stops making progress on new work after its first `maxConcurrentRuns` runs complete, silently, with no error, no crash, no log line an operator would necessarily notice as alarming (the claim/complete cycle looks superficially like healthy activity in the logs — it just never touches anything new). No test in this codebase's history had ever pushed more than a small, sequential handful of runs through a real `startWorker` claim loop before this session — precisely why the plan flagged this as unattempted.

**Reproduction (concrete — a debug script against the real composition, not inferred from reading code):** 20 runs triggered against a real `startWorker` (`maxConcurrentRuns: 3`). The first 3 runs claim and complete correctly. Every run after that: stuck. Logging every worker log line (the default `logSink` is `noopSink` — silent by default, which is itself part of why this was easy to miss) showed the SAME TWO run ids being claimed, "finished," and released **in a sub-millisecond tight loop, forever** — never once reaching any of the 17 still-pending runs.

**Root cause, traced precisely:** `worker.ts`'s `executeOneClaim` success path called `store.jobQueue.release(runId)` unconditionally after every successful `executeClaimedRun` — including when the run being "executed" was ALREADY terminal. `run-lifecycle.ts`'s real `executeRun` correctly treats an already-`completed`/`failed`/`cancelled`/`waiting` run as an idempotent no-op (returns it unchanged, does not re-run steps) — so nothing ever threw, nothing ever looked broken from inside a single claim. But `JobQueueStore.release` clears `claimedBy` to `null` and **leaves the row in place** — and `listClaimable`'s own documented contract ("never claimed, or claimed with an expired lease") cannot distinguish a released-because-finished row from one that was genuinely never claimed. The instant a run finished and its entry was released, it became claimable again, immediately, forever — a self-sustaining loop that starves every other row in `job_queue` for as long as the worker runs, since `claimTick`'s own admission-control loop (`while (claiming && claimedRunIds.size < maxConcurrentRuns)`) keeps finding "claimable" work in the SAME 2-3 already-done rows before ever reaching the genuinely-pending ones behind them in `listClaimable`'s result order.

**The comment on the code being replaced was itself the tell — a documented but never-verified assumption:** "A run correctly checkpointed into `waiting` by the engine has already had its claim released as part of that checkpoint (S1's job)." Verified directly, this session: `wait-machine.ts` and `run-lifecycle.ts` (`@aart/engine`) call exactly one `job_queue` method, ever — `enqueue`, at trigger time. Neither ever calls `release` or `remove`. `job_queue` lifecycle for a claimed run has ALWAYS been entirely `@aart/server`'s own responsibility — the "backstop" framing in the old comment was wrong about there being a primary mechanism it was backing up.

**Fix:** `.release(runId)` -> `.remove(runId)` in `worker.ts`'s `executeOneClaim`. Verified this is correct for every status a successfully-returned `executeClaimedRun` can leave a run in (`executeRun`'s own loop never returns while still `"running"` — only completed/failed/cancelled/waiting): completed/failed/cancelled obviously never need to be reclaimed again; `waiting` doesn't either, because resumption (`resumeManual`/`resumeBySignal`/`resumeApproval`/`resumeTimerWait`) drives execution forward itself (`engine.ts`'s `continueAfterResume` calls `runStepsLoop` directly) — nothing about resuming a wait depends on a worker re-claiming it from `job_queue`. Confirmed no existing test depended on the old `release`-and-leave-present behavior: full `worker.test.ts` (15 tests, all reclaim-sweep-related, all exercise `reclaim.ts`'s own separate `release`/`remove` calls, untouched by this fix) re-verified green.

**One of my own E2E tests (A37, worker-kill) had baked in an assertion matching the OLD, buggy behavior** (`expect(finalJobQueueEntry).toMatchObject({ claimedBy: null, reclaimCount: 1 })` — asserting the row still existed after completion) — updated to assert the row is now genuinely gone (`store.jobQueue.get(runId)` resolves `undefined`), matching the corrected, actually-correct behavior. Re-verified: 4/4 clean runs (worker-kill + load-soak together).

**The load/soak test itself** (`packages/server/src/e2e/load-soak.e2e.test.ts`, in-process — deliberately not a spawned child process, unlike the SIGKILL-driven E2E tests, since directly sampling `process.memoryUsage()` for the actual worker doing the work is more meaningful than querying a child process over IPC for a memory-sanity concern): 20 runs, `maxConcurrentRuns: 3`, a real ~80ms-delay block (long enough that concurrency is genuinely observable, not just theoretically possible). Polls `handle.claimedRunIds.size` continuously (not just start/end) asserting the cap is NEVER exceeded and IS genuinely reached (`toBe(3)`, not just `toBeLessThanOrEqual` — proving admission control is doing real work, not vacuously true because nothing ever contended); confirms all 20 runs reach `"completed"` with a correct one-entry trace each; asserts heap growth stays under both a 3x-factor and a 150MB-absolute ceiling (the plan's own deliberately loose "not obviously alarming" bar — a sanity check, not a precision leak detector). Before the fix: hung past 45 seconds, stuck at 3/20 complete. After: stable, ~1.5s, 3/3 clean runs.

---

### A41 — S9 reconciliation ledger item 14 ("triage remaining findings to zero") closed: all 186 redaction-lint findings triaged, 1 real bypass fixed as code, 185 reviewed and suppressed; CI gate flipped from informational to blocking

**Scope.** A29 wired `pnpm run lint:redaction` into CI as `continue-on-error: true` specifically because 187 findings (186 on this session's fresh run — a `console.log`/`process.exit` behaves slightly differently across environments, as A29 itself flagged) were untriaged. This session triaged every one of them individually — reading the guarded call site's actual code, and where relevant the call chain it feeds into, never suppressing by filename/package pattern alone — and flipped the CI step to blocking.

**Mechanism used (already built before this session, not modified in shape): `packages/governance/src/redaction-lint-suppressions.ts`** exports `REDACTION_LINT_SUPPRESSIONS: readonly RedactionLintSuppression[]`, each a `{file, snippet, reason}` matched on repo-relative file path + exact trimmed source line (never line number, so a suppression silently stops applying — reported as STALE, a CI failure — the moment its guarded code actually changes). `redaction-lint-cli.ts`'s `applySuppressions` filters findings against this list and fails if any entry goes stale. This session populated the list; the detection logic in `redaction-lint.ts` (the regexes/heuristics deciding whether a line IS a finding) was **not** weakened — the one change made there is a file-scope exclusion, see the second bug below, not a detection-pattern change.

**Real bypass #1 (fixed as code, not suppressed) — `packages/engine/src/step-executor.ts`'s `executeWaitDispatch`, `human.approval` branch.** Built and persisted a brand-new `ApprovalTask` directly via `config.store.approvals.put({...})` using `resolvedWith.title`/`.description` — `resolvedWith` is the step's `with:` block *after* template resolution (`resolveWithRecord`, called earlier in `executeStep`), so a workflow author referencing `{{ secrets.X }}` in a `human.approval` step's title/description would have the raw resolved secret value persisted, unredacted, into a new `ApprovalTask` row — the ONE persist call site in this file that never routed through `applyRedaction`, despite `resolvedSecretRefs` already being a live parameter of this exact function. Distinct from the `WaitCondition` `enterWait` redacts immediately afterward (`wait-machine.ts`) — `ApprovalTask` is a separate store collection that write never covered. **Fix:** build the record as a typed `ApprovalTask` local, then `await config.store.approvals.put(applyRedaction(config.redact, approvalTask, resolvedSecretRefs));` — the same `applyRedaction`-before-`store.X.put` shape every other persist call site in this file already uses. New regression test in `step-executor.test.ts` (`executeStep — redaction routing` describe block): a `human.approval` step with `with: { title: "{{ secrets.APPROVAL_TITLE }}" }` against a real scan-and-replace redactor, asserting the persisted `ApprovalTask.title` contains `[REDACTED]` and never the raw resolved value, while a sibling non-secret field (`description`) passes through unchanged. Full `@aart/engine` suite re-verified green (no regressions from routing this one call site through redaction).

**Real bug #2 (in the lint's own tooling, fixed) — `redaction-lint-suppressions.ts` was scanning itself.** The moment the suppression list held real entries, `pnpm run lint:redaction` reported 41 findings — every one pointing at `redaction-lint-suppressions.ts` itself. Root cause: `RedactionLintSuppression.snippet` is, by design, a VERBATIM QUOTE of another file's own source line (so `applySuppressions` can exact-match it) — so a suppression justifying e.g. a `store.runs.put(...)` call site necessarily contains the literal substring `store.runs.put(` as a STRING LITERAL inside `redaction-lint-suppressions.ts`, indistinguishable from a real call site by this lint's line-based heuristics (which don't parse string-literal context). `redaction-lint.ts` already had exactly this class of self-reference problem solved for its own two files — `SELF_EXCLUDED_BASENAMES = new Set(["redaction-lint.ts", "redaction-lint-cli.ts"])`, with its own doc comment explaining why scanning the lint's own diagnostic output is nonsensical. `redaction-lint-suppressions.ts` is the identical category (the lint's own configuration DATA about other code, never application record data itself) and simply hadn't been added to that set yet, because it held no real entries (`[]`) until this session. **Fix:** added `"redaction-lint-suppressions.ts"` to `SELF_EXCLUDED_BASENAMES`, with the doc comment extended to explain why. This is a file-scope exclusion (which files get scanned at all), not a change to any detection pattern (the regexes/heuristics unchanged) — same category as the two pre-existing exclusions, and the same category `redaction-lint-cli.ts`'s own `DEFAULT_EXCLUDED_PACKAGES` already uses at package granularity ("the lint supports exclusions — extend its config, don't weaken its detection," this file's own header). Flagging explicitly per this session's brief: this touches `redaction-lint.ts` (the detection file), not only the suppressions file — a deliberate call, not an oversight, and the narrowest fix available (extending an existing, purpose-built exclusion set to cover a file that didn't exist when that set was last touched) rather than inventing a second mechanism in `redaction-lint-cli.ts` to solve the identical problem a different way.

**The other 154 unique (file, snippet) suppression entries (covering 185 of the 186 findings — several files repeat an identical snippet on multiple lines, e.g. `stubs/engine.ts`'s `await store.runs.put(run);` appears 5 times, sharing one suppression), taxonomy:**
1. **Not a data-emission path at all** — returns a closure/class instance/void/trivially-empty value, is itself inside the redaction implementation's own helper (`stubs/governance.ts`'s `redact()`), or the "MCP-tool return" heuristic (`isMcpFile && /^\s*return\b/`) fired on an internal helper whose return value never itself reaches an MCP client (e.g. `real-context.ts`'s composition-root closures/port objects; `yaml-compiler.ts`'s pre-resolution workflow-definition compilation; static catalog/schema/recipe data). Also one literal false positive: `server/src/poison.ts:59`'s finding is a JSDoc *prose sentence* mentioning `store.runs.put()` as documentation of a caller's responsibility — not code at all.
2. **Deterministic, production-reachable-but-secrets-incapable stub files** — `packages/mcp/src/stubs/{engine,evidence,governance,registry}.ts`. Important correction to the obvious assumption: these are **not** test-only fixtures — traced the actual composition wiring (`packages/cli/src/cli-context.ts` -> `createCliContext` -> `createAartContext`, which every real `aart` CLI/MCP invocation uses) and confirmed `createRealAartContext` (the wiring `real-context.ts`'s own module comment describes) has **zero non-test callers anywhere in the repo** — `createAartContext`'s STUB engine/governance/evidence/registry is what the actually-shipping binary runs today. They're suppressed instead because they're structurally incapable of ever holding a resolved secret: no `@aart/expr` import, no `resolveSecret`, no expression evaluation anywhere in any of the four files — every field is either copied verbatim from caller-supplied JSON (never "resolved") or a hardcoded/empty literal. A **separate, real, significant, out-of-scope architectural gap** — flagged below, not fixed here (redaction-lint triage only).
3. **Genuinely redacted via a verified call chain** — traced end-to-end, not assumed: `@aart/engine`'s `triggerRun`/`executeRun`/`cancelRun`/every `resume*` mechanism/`failExpiredWait` all call `applyRedaction` immediately before every `return` AND every `store.runs.put`/`store.approvals.put`/`store.waits.put` (verified across `run-lifecycle.ts`, `step-executor.ts`, `wait/wait-machine.ts` line by line) — so any MCP handler surfacing `ctx.engine.*`'s return value (execution.ts/deployment.ts/governance.ts handlers) is safe by construction. Also covers "spreads an already-redacted base record with only static metadata added" (flag-clear/poison-flag/reclaim writes across `cli/stubs/server.ts`, `dashboard/stub-deps.ts`, `server/{flags,poison,worker/*}.ts`, `engine/concurrency.ts`) and "EvalRun is a verified aggregate-only summary type, no raw example content" (`evidence/src/evals/run-suite.ts`).
4. **Configuration/reference data, not resolved secret values, by the lint's own established threat model** — `resolvedSecretRefs` only ever holds values obtained through `@aart/expr`'s injected secret resolver during ONE live `triggerRun`/`executeRun` call (`engine/src/redaction.ts`'s own doc comment); it is never persisted for reuse. Several call sites operate on data structurally outside that model: `Deployment.triggerConfig`/`Environment.secretSource` (symbolic refs/config, e.g. `webhook-hmac-secret-ref`, not resolved values — `cli/src/commands/deployment.ts`, `server/src/{environments,promotion}.ts`), `RejectedTrigger.rawPayload` (third-party inbound delivery data, `server/src/triggers/intake.ts`), and `Correction.observed`/`corrected`/`ApprovalTask.decision` (human/agent-authored free-form data entered via a separate, later MCP/CLI/HTTP call with no live-run `resolvedSecretRefs` set obtainable at that call site at all — `evidence/src/corrections/*.ts`, `dashboard/stub-deps.ts`, `mcp/stubs/*.ts`, `server/src/http/server.ts`, `server/src/triggers/adapters.ts`). This last group carries a disclosed residual limitation, not a bypass: under normal use the RunRecord a correction is *about* was already fully redacted before anyone could read it to formulate the correction (verified), but an agent could in principle type a secret it learned out-of-band directly into a free-text field — the same limitation every free-text field in this system has, not something value-scan-and-replace against a known resolved-secret set can structurally ever catch.
5. **Test-only, verified** — `packages/mcp/src/test-utils.ts` (zero non-test importers, confirmed by grep) and `packages/server/src/{engine/boundary.ts's createFakeEngine, test-helpers.ts}` (confirmed transitively test-only: `test-helpers.ts`'s only importer, `dashboard/src/test-support/fixtures.ts`, itself has zero non-test importers; `createFakeEngine` is also structurally incapable of resolving secrets, same as category 2, and separately confirmed unreachable from the shipping CLI, which wires `createStubServerPort` instead).

**Not fixed here, flagged as a genuine, separate, out-of-scope architectural gap surfaced as a side effect of this triage:** the shipping `aart` CLI/MCP binary runs entirely on `createAartContext`'s stub composition (`StubEngine`/`StubGovernance`/`StubEvidence`/`StubRegistry`) — `createRealAartContext` (real `@aart/engine`/`@aart/governance`/`@aart/evidence`/`@aart/registry`, `real-context.ts`) has no production caller anywhere, only test/E2E callers. This is orthogonal to redaction (the stub composition is safe for exactly the reason category 2 above establishes — it structurally cannot resolve a secret in the first place) but is a significant fact about what this system currently does in production, discovered while verifying category-2 suppression reasons were honest rather than assumed. Not touched — wiring the real composition into the CLI is a deliberate architecture-level call (which composition root owns constructing the real engine, per A37's own identical framing for `ServerPort`), squarely outside a redaction-lint triage session's mandate.

**Verification, including a real detour chasing what turned out to be someone else's (already-fixed) bug:** `pnpm run build && typecheck && typecheck:tests` clean; `pnpm run lint:redaction` clean (185 suppressions applied, 0 stale, 0 kept findings). `pnpm run test` was flaky mid-session — of several full runs, each had either zero failures or exactly one failing real-process E2E test file, never the same file twice in a row (`mcp/src/e2e/verify-loop.e2e.test.ts` once, `server/src/e2e/load-soak.e2e.test.ts` repeatedly, `server/src/e2e/worker-kill.e2e.test.ts` once) — investigated rather than assumed unrelated: confirmed zero content overlap with this session's diff; confirmed via `git status` that all affected `*.e2e.test.ts` files are untracked, part of the separate, concurrently-in-progress "plan §4" E2E effort (A37-A40 above, not this session's work); confirmed `npx vitest run --exclude "**/e2e/**"` was 100% clean (209 files, 2059 tests) on every attempt; a standalone reproduction of the `verify-loop` failure, run in isolation with no cross-file resource contention, completed successfully end to end. The `load-soak`/`worker-kill` instability specifically turned out to have a real, identified root cause — A40 (above), landed concurrently with this session by someone else, fixing a genuine `job_queue` release-vs-remove bug. Re-verified after that fix: `pnpm run test` clean twice in a row, 215 files / 2071 tests, 0 failures. Recorded here rather than silently assumed-fine, since a mid-session green run can be luck and this one very nearly was reported as such before the actual cause surfaced.
