## 2026-07-11 — S11 Real composition + founder test-drive

### A42 — The gap A37 and A41 both independently flagged and both declined to fix ("wiring the real composition into the CLI is a deliberate architecture-level call... squarely outside [this] session's mandate") is closed: `packages/cli`'s composition root now builds the REAL stack by default — `aart run`/`aart worker`/`aart server`/`aart bundle`/`aart mcp` all execute for real in the shipping binary, proved by an isolated tarball install, not by reading code

**The decision this entry documents (made by the orchestrator, not unilaterally by this session).** `packages/cli/src/cli-context.ts`'s `createCliContext` is the ONE composition root every `aart` command (and `aart mcp`, since `mcpCommand` passes `cli.aart` straight to `startMcpStdioServer`) is built against. Before this session it unconditionally called `createAartContext` (the stub engine/governance/evidence/registry — `stubs/engine.ts`'s own doc comment: "every other step 'completes' immediately with empty outputs") and `createStubServerPort` (`startWorker`/`startServer` literally `return { async stop() {} }`/`{ port, async close() {} }` — no-ops), regardless of the fact that `createRealAartContext` (real `@aart/engine`+`@aart/blocks-core`+`@aart/governance`+`@aart/evidence`+`@aart/registry`+`@aart/llm`, `packages/mcp/src/real-context.ts`) and `createRealEngineBoundary` (A37, real `@aart/server` worker/claim/lease/reclaim machinery) both already existed, fully built and E2E-tested, with zero production callers. **Now the default is real** — `real: false` (or `AART_TRUST_MODE`-style env equivalent is NOT how this is spelled; it's a literal option field, see below) opts back into the stub, kept alive deliberately for this package's own fast/offline/deterministic unit-test suite, never deleted.

**What got built, file by file:**

- **`packages/mcp/src/context.ts`** — `createRealAartContext` refactored (behavior/signature unchanged) to delegate to a new `createRealAartContextWithEngine(options): { context: AartContext; engine: Engine | undefined }`. Why a new function rather than just changing the old one's return shape: `AartContext` has no `engine: Engine` field (only the narrower `EnginePort`) and every existing caller (this session's own flagship E2E tests included) destructures/uses it as a plain `AartContext` — widening its shape would have been a silent, ambient breaking change to a frozen-shaped export. `engine: Engine | undefined` is `undefined` only if a caller overrides BOTH `options.engine` and `options.evidence` (the two ports that would otherwise construct one) — not a path any real caller in this repo takes.
- **`packages/mcp/src/index.ts`** — re-exports `createRealAartContextWithEngine`/`RealAartContextResult`, plus `type { Engine } from "@aart/engine"` (this package already depends on `@aart/engine`; this just threads its type through the public surface). This IS the "broadening `@aart/mcp`'s public exports" the brief pre-approved — the alternative (`@aart/cli` importing `@aart/engine` directly for the type) would have added a devDependency this package doesn't otherwise need, for a type `@aart/mcp` already has in hand.
- **`packages/cli/src/real-server-port.ts`** (new) — `createRealServerPort(store, engine): ServerPort`. `startServer`/`startWorker` build one `EngineBoundary` (`createRealEngineBoundary(store, engine)`, A37) and pass it into `@aart/server`'s real `startServer`/`startWorker` — the SAME `Engine` instance `createRealAartContextWithEngine` built for the rest of this same process's `AartContext`, not a second, divergent one. `clearRunFlag`/`listFlaggedRuns` delegate to `@aart/server`'s real `flags.ts` exports directly (the CLI's former stub had already correctly hand-rolled the identical logic against the real, frozen `RunRecord.flag` field — this just removes the duplicate). **`produceBundle` is the one real bridge, not a pass-through:** `ServerPort.produceBundle`'s `environment?: string` is a NAME (what a human types after `--environment`); `@aart/server`'s real `produceBundle(store, { workflowId, workflowVersion, deployment?: Deployment })` wants an actual `Deployment` record. `resolveDeployment()` does `store.environments.getByName(name)` (throwing a clear, actionable error ONLY if the named environment doesn't exist at all — `aart bundle: environment "X" not found. Register it first, e.g. "aart deploy <id> --target X".`) then `store.deployments.list({ environmentId, workflowId }).find(d => d.workflowVersion === version)` — an environment that exists but has no deployment yet for this exact workflow+version is NOT an error (`produceBundle`'s own `deployment` param is documented optional: "omit for a bare workflow-closure bundle"). The real function's richer `Bundle` return (definitions/packs/registry/triggers, content-addressed `bundleHash`) is then flattened into `ServerPort`'s flatter `BundleLike` (`bundleToBundleLike`) using the exact same relative-path layout `@aart/server`'s own `writeBundleToDisk` uses, so the files landing on disk are byte-identical to what a direct `@aart/server` caller would produce.
- **`packages/cli/src/bundle-files.ts`** (new) — `writeBundleFilesToDisk(files, outDir)`, the six-line mkdir/writeFile loop `stubs/server.ts`'s `writeBundleToDisk` used to own inline, now shared by both the stub and real `ServerPort` (both need it; neither needs its own copy).
- **`packages/cli/src/cli-context.ts`** — `createCliContext(options: CreateCliContextOptions)`: `options.real === false` → the old stub path, unchanged, verbatim. Otherwise (the default) → `createRealAartContextWithEngine(options)` + `createRealServerPort(context.store, engine)`.
- **`packages/cli/src/test-utils.ts`** — `createTestCli()` (the one thing every CLI unit test builds its `CliContext` through) now passes `real: false` explicitly — the ONLY line that had to change for this package's entire existing 2000+-test suite to keep passing unmodified (verified: it does — see below).
- **`packages/cli/src/commands/process.ts`** — `workerCommand`/`serverCommand`/`mcpCommand` used to `await new Promise(() => {})` while blocking, with an `await handle.stop()`/`.close()` line immediately after that was — until this session — genuinely **dead code**: nothing ever resolved that promise, so a real SIGTERM hit Node's default no-handler behavior (immediate, no-cleanup termination) rather than that already-written cleanup line. Discovered empirically, not by inspection, during this session's own isolated-install SIGTERM test (below). Fixed for all three: `workerCommand`/`serverCommand` now resolve on `SIGTERM`/`SIGINT` (`waitForShutdownSignal()`), letting the pre-existing `handle.stop()`/`.close()` finally run. `mcpCommand` needed a THIRD signal raced in alongside SIGTERM/SIGINT and the SDK's own `transport.onclose` hook: `process.stdin`'s own `"end"` event — because `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk@1.29.0`'s `StdioServerTransport`, as shipped, only ever attaches `"data"`/`"error"` listeners to `stdin`, never `"end"`, so a client disconnecting via `StdioClientTransport.close()`'s own documented mechanism (`childProcess.stdin.end()` — no signal sent at all) was previously invisible to this process, which then got force-terminated by Node's empty-event-loop exit with a `"Detected unsettled top-level await"` warning instead of stopping cleanly. All three verified fixed against the real installed binary (below).
- **`packages/cli/package.json` / `packages/cli/tsconfig.json`** — one new devDependency + matching reference, `@aart/server` (workspace-only, build-time; `real-server-port.ts` is the only importer). **Zero new esbuild externals or published `dependencies`** — verified, not assumed: `build:publish`'s fail-loud metafile check (A35) still reports the identical 8-package external list after this change. Root cause, worth recording since it's non-obvious: `@aart/mcp/src/context.ts` already imports `real-context.ts` unconditionally at module scope (`import { buildRealCatalog, createRealEnginePort, ... } from "./real-context.js"`), and that file in turn unconditionally imports `@aart/engine`/`@aart/blocks-core`/`@aart/governance`/`@aart/evidence`/`@aart/registry`/`@aart/llm` at ITS module scope — so merely importing `createAartContext` (the STUB function) from `@aart/mcp`, which is all `cli-context.ts` did before this session, already forced esbuild to bundle the entire real-composition dependency graph into `dist/bin.js`, since ES module imports execute for their side effects at load time regardless of which named export a caller actually uses. `@aart/server`'s own dependencies (`@aart/engine`, `@aart/expr`, `@aart/governance`, `@aart/store`, `@aart/types`, `zod`) were therefore already fully covered by A35's existing externals list before this session ever ran — this is also the honest answer to why A35's list already contained `@anthropic-ai/sdk`/`isolated-vm`/`playwright` despite `createRealAartContext` having zero production callers at the time: the BUNDLE already carried the real stack; only the RUNTIME CALL PATH didn't use it.
- **`packages/cli/src/cli-context.test.ts`** (new, 4 tests) — verifies the real/stub split BY BEHAVIOR, not by construction: a workflow with a genuinely-failing `assert.equals` step reports `status: "failed"` under the default (real engine actually evaluates the assertion and throws) vs. `status: "completed"` under `{ real: false }` (the stub's documented "every other step completes immediately with empty outputs" — it never evaluates the assertion at all). Two more cover `real-server-port.ts`'s new environment-bridge directly: an unknown `--environment` name rejects with the exact clear-error message, no environment at all still produces a correct bare-closure bundle with the real on-disk layout.
- **`packages/mcp/src/real-context.ts`** — module header comment corrected (A37 flagged it, left stale until now): it previously claimed ServerPort was wired "here" via "a real EngineBoundary adapter... this package's `createRealEngineBoundary`" — both halves were false when written (the function didn't exist anywhere yet, and this file has never had a ServerPort-shaped export; `ServerPort` isn't even part of `AartContext`, architecture §13.3's CLI-only exception). Now correctly points at `@aart/server/src/engine/boundary.ts` (the function) and `@aart/cli/src/real-server-port.ts` (the adapter, this session).
- **`packages/governance/src/redaction-lint-suppressions.ts`** — two new reviewed suppressions for `context.ts`'s new/changed return statements (both are dependency-injection-bundle construction — a store handle, port objects made of methods, a raw `Engine` instance — never persisted/emitted data, same category as the adjacent pre-existing entry for the line they're new siblings of), plus that adjacent entry's own reason text corrected (it referenced `createRealAartContext` by name for a return statement that function no longer literally has, post-refactor). `pnpm run lint:redaction` genuinely caught this (2 kept findings) before the fix — real proof the gate's detection logic is unweakened, not merely re-affirmed by inspection.

**Verification — the from-clean gate suite, then the isolated-install proof (A33/A35's own methodology, repeated for this change).** `pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`: all green, 216 files / 2075 tests (2071 baseline + 4 new in `cli-context.test.ts`), 186 redaction-lint suppressions applied (185 baseline + 2 new, net of the one adjacent entry's match-count correction above), 0 kept findings, 0 stale. Then `pnpm --filter @team-monet/aart run build:publish && pnpm pack`, installed via `npm install <tarball>` in a directory with zero access to this workspace (`npm init -y` first, outside the repo entirely) — confirmed clean (no `E404`, `find node_modules -iname "@aart*"` → zero matches, same as A35). From that installed binary, EVERY item this session's own DoD named was run for real, not simulated, and is quoted verbatim in this session's final report (not reproduced here in full — see that report / `TEST-DRIVE.md`, which a founder can rerun on this exact machine): `aart init-agent` (wrote a real, correct `.mcp.json` + `AGENTS.md`); a real, authored-for-this-purpose "no browser, no LLM" smoke workflow (`data.stringify` + `assert.contains`) registered, validated (real 5-class validation — a draft version of the same workflow WITH an `artifact.write` step genuinely surfaced a real `capability`-class finding, `file.write` needing approval, before it was simplified out) and run to `"completed"` with a real per-step trace; a DELIBERATELY-mis-asserted variant of the same workflow run to a genuine `"failed"` status with a real `assert.contains` error message quoting the actual (correctly template-resolved) runtime value — proof this is real block dispatch, not a stub echo, by direct negative example; `aart_get_report` (there is no CLI `aart report` command — the surface's equivalent is this MCP tool, called for real over stdio) returning a full real per-step trace with real timestamps and durations; `aart mcp` driven by the real `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` `Client`/`StdioClientTransport` (not a hand-rolled JSON-RPC framer) through a real `initialize` handshake, `tools/list` (21/21 tools, once a real `Environment` and real `EvalSuite` existed in the store to satisfy the two progressive-disclosure gates — 16 in a bare-fresh project, both real, correct, architecture §10.1-documented gating, not a bug), and a real `tools/call aart_find_blocks`; `aart worker`/`aart server` genuinely bound their documented ports, answered real `GET /health` (worker: `{"status":"ok","claimedRuns":0,...}`; server: `{"status":"ok"}`, plus `GET /workflows` returning the real registered ids from the same store), and — after the `process.ts` fix above — stopped cleanly on `SIGTERM` with exit code 0 and the correct `"Worker stopped."`/`"Server stopped."` message, verified by sending a real signal to a real backgrounded process and checking both the exit code and that the PID was actually gone; and the governed-pause demo (DoD's own required proof) — a second tiny workflow (`human.approval` then a trivial follow-up step) run to a genuine `"waiting"` status, its pending task's real (`crypto.randomUUID()`-generated, NOT the block's own `with.taskId`/`${runId}:${stepId}` default — see the finding below) `ApprovalTask.id` discovered via a real `aart_list_waiting_runs` MCP call (there is no CLI equivalent either), then `aart approve <taskId> --decision approved --reviewer ...` genuinely resuming the run through its second step to `"completed"`.

**Real findings surfaced by actually running this, not by reading code — recorded honestly rather than smoothed over:**
1. **`@aart/expr`'s template resolution is TOP-LEVEL-`with`-field-only, does not recurse into arrays or nested objects** (`packages/expr/src/resolver.ts`'s `resolveExpression`: `if (typeof value !== "string") return value;` — an array/object value is returned completely unresolved, whatever `{{ }}` tokens it contains buried inside; `packages/engine/src/expr-context.ts`'s `resolveWithRecord` only ever calls `resolveExpression` on each top-level `Object.entries(withRecord)` value, never recursively). Not a bug — verified as the genuine, consistent, existing contract (every pre-existing example workflow's own `with:` blocks already only ever reference `{{ }}` at a top-level string field) — but a real authoring pitfall this session hit firsthand while drafting the smoke workflow (an early `data.merge`-based draft silently left `{{ inputs.who }}` unresolved, buried inside an array element, and only surfaced because the assertion happened to still pass for an unrelated reason). `TEST-DRIVE.md` calls this out explicitly so the founder doesn't lose the same twenty minutes.
2. **`aart deploy` enforces real governance gates** (`Deployment refused: required gates unmet for environment "staging"`, `unmetGates: ["validate","humanReview"]`) — a draft, unreviewed workflow cannot be deployed to a `governed`-trust-mode environment, exactly as `@aart/governance`'s real, pre-existing, independently-tested policy dictates. Discovered by actually trying it (the natural next step after `aart register`), not by reading the policy code.
3. **A workflow with an unapproved, Medium-risk capability (`file.write` via `artifact.write`) still ran to completion under `aart run` in `governed` mode**, despite `aart_validate`'s own finding wording ("this will need approval before it can run... will block it otherwise") reading as if it should be hard-blocked. Empirically: it wasn't blocked. This is pre-existing `@aart/governance` capability-grant policy (`getGrantedCapabilities`, unrelated to anything this session touched) — recorded here as an honest, unresolved observation from real testing, not investigated further or fixed (renegotiating governance's own risk-tier/trust-mode capability-grant policy is squarely outside a composition-wiring session's mandate, same framing A41 used for the gap this entry closes).

### A43 — The founder's own A42 test drive found a real dashboard bug on the very next thing he tried: workflow detail 404'd and block detail didn't exist at all. Both fixed; the workflow-detail fix closes the SEAMS.md-flagged "enrich GET /workflows" gap a prior pass had explicitly decided NOT to close

**Reproduction, against the founder's own still-running processes, not a synthetic repro.** `~/aart-test-drive` (outside this repo) already had `aart server --port 8080` (pid 57425) and `node dashboard-dev.mjs` (pid 58122) running — the exact processes from his own A42 test drive, with three real registered workflows in `.aart/registry/workflows/`. `curl http://localhost:4000/workflows` correctly listed all three (reads through `ApiClient` -> the real `aart server`). `curl http://localhost:4000/workflows/smoke-data-pipeline` returned `HTTP 404` / `<p>No such workflow.</p>` — for a workflow verifiably present on disk and in the list page one click away. `curl http://localhost:4000/blocks/artifact.write` (guessing at a detail URL) returned the router's own generic `HTTP 404` / `<h1>404 Not Found</h1>` — no route matched at all, and `/blocks`'s own list page rendered every block id as plain `<code>` text, no `<a href>` anywhere — no way to reach a block's own page from the UI existed, by construction.

**Root cause 1 — workflow detail: not a missing feature, a silently-empty second store handle.** `packages/dashboard/src/server.ts`'s `/workflows/:id` route (pre-fix) never went through `ApiClient` at all — it called `store.workflows.getLatest(id)` on a **second, independently-constructed** `AartStore` handle local to the dashboard process, because S2's `GET /workflows` only ever returned bare `{workflowIds}` (SEAMS.md, "@aart/server's HTTP API surface"). `TEST-DRIVE.md`'s own example `dashboard-dev.mjs` script builds that handle from a literal, never-substituted placeholder: `createFsStore("/Users/YOU/aart-test-drive/.aart")` — and the founder's actual on-disk copy at `~/aart-test-drive/dashboard-dev.mjs` was confirmed (`diff`) to be a byte-for-byte copy of the guide, placeholder included. `/Users/YOU` doesn't exist on this or any machine; `createFsStore` doesn't fail on a nonexistent root (the fs adapter's `isEnoent` handling in `listVersionsOnDisk`/`listWorkflowIds`, `packages/store/src/adapters/fs/workflows.ts:68-93`, treats ENOENT as "zero versions/ids" rather than an error), so the dashboard's own store silently believed no workflow ever existed. Meanwhile the list page — reading through `ApiClient` -> the real, correctly-configured `aart server` process (cwd `~/aart-test-drive`, confirmed via `lsof -p 57425/58122 | grep cwd`) — showed the correct data. Two store handles, two different answers, no error anywhere: the worst kind of bug, and exactly the failure mode a **prior** pass explicitly re-examined and declined to fix — `SEAMS.md`'s S9-verification note on this exact seam concluded "this package's existing workaround... is therefore the CORRECT resolution already, not a gap needing S9 to close." That conclusion held only because nothing had yet exercised the two-independently-configured-stores failure mode; a real test drive did. Documented as a correction in `SEAMS.md` itself (see below), not silently overwritten.

**Root cause 2 — block detail: never built, no wiring bug to find.** `packages/dashboard/src/views/blocks-packs.ts`'s `renderBlocksPage` (pre-fix) rendered every block id as bare `<code>${id}</code>` — no anchor — and no `/blocks/:id` route existed anywhere in `packages/dashboard/src/server.ts`. Unlike workflow detail, this has nothing to do with store configuration: the block catalog (`capability-catalog.ts`'s `realBlockImplementations`) is a pure, static, in-memory construction from `@aart/blocks-core`/`@aart/llm` manifests, never filesystem-path-dependent — confirmed by the fact block detail is broken identically regardless of which `.aart` path the dashboard's store handle points at. Genuinely missing, not misconfigured.

**Fix, file by file:**
- **`packages/server/src/http/server.ts:188-199`** — new `GET /workflows/:id[?version=X]` -> `{workflow, versions}` (404 `{error: "not found"}` if unknown), same shape convention as the existing `GET /runs/:id -> {run}`. Latest version by default; `?version=` fetches a specific one (`store.workflows.get`); `versions` (`store.workflows.listVersions`) is returned either way so a caller never needs a second round trip for version history. Closes the SEAMS.md-flagged gap for real.
- **`packages/dashboard/src/api-client.ts`** — new `WorkflowDetail` type + `ApiClient.getWorkflow(id, version?)`, implemented in both `createHttpApiClient` (hits the new route, 404 -> `undefined`, mirroring `getRun`) and `createFakeApiClient` (direct store reads, for this package's own store-backed local/test topology).
- **`packages/dashboard/src/server.ts:91-105`** — `/workflows/:id` now calls `api.getWorkflow(id, ?version)` + `api.listRuns({workflowId: id})` instead of touching `store` directly; 404 handling unchanged. This is the actual fix: the page no longer depends on the dashboard process's own store handle being correctly configured AT ALL, closing off the whole failure mode rather than papering over one instance of it.
- **`packages/dashboard/src/views/workflows.ts`** — `renderWorkflowDetailPage` gains `versions`/`recentRuns` params and two new sections: **Versions** (newest-first, each non-current version linking back to the same page with `?version=`, matching `versions`' real semver-aware order from `store.workflows.listVersions`/`compareVersions` rather than re-deriving a naive one) and **Recent Runs** (sorted newest-first, capped at 20, linking to `/runs/:runId`, an honest "No runs yet." when empty) — the "full definition, versions, approval/gates state, recent runs" this entry's own brief asked for; approval/gates state was already rendered pre-fix.
- **`packages/dashboard/src/capability-catalog.ts`** — new `getBlockManifest(store, id)`, a single-manifest lookup alongside the existing `listBlockManifests`.
- **`packages/dashboard/src/views/blocks-packs.ts`** — new `renderBlockDetailPage(manifest)`: version, category, description, capabilities (an honest "(none)" when empty, not a blank list), and both the input/output JSON Schemas the engine itself validates `with:`/outputs against at dispatch (`json-schema.ts`-derived, architecture §2.5's frozen `BlockManifest` shape). `renderBlocksPage`'s id cells now link to `/blocks/:id`.
- **`packages/dashboard/src/server.ts:150-165`** — new `GET /blocks/:id` (404 page if unknown), reading the same real, local, in-memory catalog `/blocks` already does — no store/API involvement, matching root cause 2's own diagnosis.
- **`TEST-DRIVE.md`** part (e) — `dashboard-dev.mjs`'s store path changed from the unsubstituted absolute placeholder to `createFsStore("./.aart")`, a relative path that resolves correctly against whatever directory you launch `node` from (the doc already tells you to run it from `~/aart-test-drive`, where you saved it) — nothing left to individually substitute, so this exact class of copy-paste footgun can't recur even for the other dashboard routes/actions that still do read/write `store` directly (corrections, evals, approve/promote — pre-existing, unrelated to this entry, not touched). Added a paragraph naming the old footgun explicitly and pointing at this entry, plus example links into the two new detail pages.
- **`SEAMS.md`** — appended a dated correction to the S9-verification note on this exact seam (`GET /workflows` enrichment), rather than silently editing it: the "not a gap needing S9 to close" conclusion is superseded, citing this entry.

**Verification.** `pnpm run clean && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`: all green from clean, 216 files / 2084 tests (2075 baseline + 9 new: 1 in `packages/server/src/http/server.test.ts`, 2 in `api-client.test.ts`, 2 in `views/workflows.test.ts`, 3 in `views/blocks-packs.test.ts`, 1 in `server.test.ts`), `test` re-run twice clean (no flakiness). New `server.test.ts` (dashboard) coverage specifically targets the reproduced failure mode, not just the happy path: a dedicated test starts the dashboard with `store` pointed at a genuinely empty fixture and `api` fronting a **separate** store holding the real workflow, and asserts workflow detail still renders 200 — the divergent-store scenario the founder actually hit, reproduced under test so it can't silently regress. Live re-verification used a fresh, independent instance built from this fix (not the founder's own live `~/aart-test-drive` processes — killing/reconfiguring those was correctly refused by the permission system as interference with a workload this session didn't create; the founder can pick up the fix by restarting his own `aart server`/`dashboard-dev.mjs` whenever he chooses): registered `smoke-data-pipeline` for real, ran it to `"completed"` for real, then `GET /workflows/smoke-data-pipeline` returned full definition + gates + a `Versions` section + the real run under `Recent Runs`, `GET /blocks/data.stringify` returned its real manifest (version, capabilities, both JSON Schemas), and — the decisive check — a **second** dashboard instance started with the exact original broken placeholder path (`/Users/YOU/aart-test-drive/.aart`, confirmed via `ls` to genuinely not exist) still rendered workflow detail correctly, because that route no longer reads the misconfigured handle at all.

**Not fixed here, flagged rather than expanded into:** every OTHER dashboard route that reads `store` directly — risk-diff (`POST /workflows/:id/risk-diff`), corrections, evals, approve/promote — is unaffected by this entry and remains exactly as fragile to the same class of store-misconfiguration as workflow detail used to be, should a founder ever hit it there too. Out of this entry's scope (only workflow detail and block detail were reported/asked for); `packages/dashboard/src/deps.ts`'s partial-stub write actions (documented pre-existing gap, TEST-DRIVE.md part (e)'s own note, A42/S11's stated non-scope) are equally untouched.

**Honest disclosures, not claimed away (see `TEST-DRIVE.md` for the founder-facing version):** real-LLM `llm.*` blocks are wired for real (`@aart/llm`'s real Anthropic provider adapter) but untested end-to-end in this session — no Anthropic API key is available in the environment this was built in, matching this repo's own established precedent for that exact limitation (`real-context.ts`'s `RealCatalogLlmOptions` doc comment, written for this same reason by an earlier session). `@aart/dashboard` was not touched — it remains on its own, separately-tracked, already-documented partial-stub `DashboardDeps` (`stub-deps.ts`'s own header comment: real governance/promotion/risk-diff, still-stub redact/engine-trigger/evidence-report-renderers) — out of scope (the brief's decision section named `packages/cli`'s composition root specifically, not `@aart/dashboard`'s separate one; three-client principle, three separate composition roots). No `LICENSE` file — deliberately deferred by the founder, `README.md`'s existing flag for this left untouched, not resolved here.
