## 2026-07-11 — S12 Deploy-story completion (partial — scope reduced on contact, see below)

### A44 — Bundle loader + `--bundle` built and verified end-to-end; runtime environment scoping / SQLite export / `--root` deliberately NOT built this session — the founder's own approval, read literally, only covers the bundle half

**The scope decision this entry documents, and why it's recorded here rather than silently either expanded or narrowed.** This session's brief offered the founder all four S12 items as one package ("S12 would close all four: a bundle loader + `--bundle` flag, runtime environment scoping, the SQLite/store-root flags") and quoted his reply verbatim: **"Yes for bundle."** Read against the offer's own wording — which names "a bundle loader + `--bundle` flag" as one item among four, distinctly, and no other item ever uses the word "bundle" — a three-word reply that names one item by name is the founder selecting that item, not a shorthand "yes" to the whole set (which would read as "yes", "go ahead", or "yes to all four", not a qualifier that happens to exactly match one item's own name). Per this codebase's own governing discipline for this kind of ambiguity (a developer "MUST NOT... redefine product scope," must "treat the user's confirmed request as the scope boundary," and must stop and flag rather than implement against the evidence when a briefing's framing outruns what its own cited evidence supports) — this session implemented **item 1 only** (bundle loader + `--bundle`) and did **not** implement items 2–4 (`aart server --environment`, the SQLite adapter's export + `--store` flag, `--root`), despite the brief's own wrap-up section describing work that assumes all four exist (a `TEST-DRIVE.md` command line using `--environment`/`--root`/`--store` together, a gate list mentioning an env-scoping test and a sqlite-flag smoke test). **This is a scope-boundary decision, not a capability gap** — nothing technical blocked building items 2–4 (the code-map the brief supplied for all three was verified accurate by this session's own read-only pass); the decision was made because the cited evidence for "cleared to implement" only supports one of the four items literally offered. Flagged here, in the same spirit as A12/A15/A19/A26 (a deliberate, load-bearing choice recorded for visibility, not an interface-shape change), so the founder — or whichever session picks up items 2–4 next — sees this was a considered call, not an oversight, and can re-confirm scope explicitly before that work starts.

**What item 1 actually is: the consuming half of the bundle system.** `packages/server/src/bundle/bundle.ts` (`produceBundle`) already wrote a self-contained, content-addressed bundle to disk; nothing read one back. This session built that other half:

- **`packages/server/src/bundle/bundle.ts`** — refactored, not rewritten: `BundleManifest` is now a real Zod schema (`BundleManifestSchema`) instead of a bare TS interface (the loader needs to validate an untrusted `manifest.json` read off disk the same way it validates every other definition), and `bundleHash`'s computation was extracted into an exported `computeBundleHash()` shared by both the producer and the loader's `verifyBundleHash` — one canonicalization implementation, not two that could silently drift and produce/accept mismatched hashes for logically-identical content. `sanitizeFilename` is now exported (the loader needs the exact same filename-from-key derivation the writer used). The stale `writeBundleToDisk` doc comment claiming `aart worker --bundle=...` already consumed this output (aspirational when written — nothing did) is corrected to point at what now actually does.
- **`packages/server/src/bundle/load.ts`** (new) — `readBundleFromDisk(bundleDir)`: reads `manifest.json`/`triggers.json`/every `definitions/`/`packs/`/`registry/{prompts,schemas}/` file the manifest's own arrays name, **verifies `bundleHash` first** (recomputed over the raw, not-yet-Zod-validated parsed JSON, via the same `computeBundleHash` the producer uses — a tampered/corrupted bundle fails on "this isn't the sealed content" before it's even asked "is this well-formed"), **then** validates every workflow/pack/prompt/schema against the real `@aart/types` Zod schemas plus an identity/contentHash cross-check against its own manifest entry, aggregating every failure into one error rather than stopping at the first. `hydrateBundle(store, bundle)` — idempotent, keyed on the bundle's root `workflowId@workflowVersion` via a deterministic synthetic `Deployment` id (`` `bundle:${workflowId}@${workflowVersion}` ``, never colliding with `generateId("deploy")`'s random `deploy_<uuid>` ids): same `bundleHash` already hydrated → no-op (`kind: "already_hydrated"`); a **different** hash for the same workflow@version → throws, refusing to silently overwrite; genuinely new → writes every definition, plus a synthetic `env_bundle` `Environment` and the marker `Deployment` (whose `triggerConfig` is the bundle's own `triggers.json`, verbatim — this is what makes the existing, unmodified `loadTriggerBindingsFromDeployments` pick up bundle-sourced triggers with zero changes to the trigger subsystem), all inside one `store.transact()` call (all-or-nothing — a mid-hydration crash can't leave the idempotency marker written without the definitions it vouches for). Definitions land with `approval`/`gates`/`needsReview`/`promotionBlocked` exactly as bundled — governance is not re-run; sealing the bundle **is** the governance decision, per the brief's own framing. `hydrateBundleFromDisk` composes both for the common case.
- **`packages/cli/src/commands/process.ts`** — `--bundle <dir>` on `workerCommand`/`serverCommand`: hydrates against `cli.aart.store` **before** starting the port (so triggers sourced from the bundle are already in the store by the time `startServer`/`startWorker` read them), via a `maybeHydrateBundle` helper. Deliberately **not** added to `ServerPort`'s (`@aart/mcp`) `startServer`/`startWorker` config shape — hydration is a store-seeding step independent of whether `cli.serverPort` is the stub or real port (both share the same real, always-fs-backed `cli.aart.store`), so it doesn't belong inside that interface. A hydration failure throws before the port ever binds, surfacing through the exact same `{ ok: false, error }` path every other CLI command error already uses. `cli.ts`'s `USAGE` updated (`aart worker [--bundle <dir>]`, `aart server [--port <n>] [--bundle <dir>]`).
- **`packages/server/src/index.ts`** — exports the new loader surface (`readBundleFromDisk`, `hydrateBundle`, `hydrateBundleFromDisk`, `verifyBundleHash`, `HydrateBundleResult`) plus the producer-side additions (`BundleManifestSchema`, `computeBundleHash`, `sanitizeFilename`).
- **`packages/governance/src/redaction-lint-suppressions.ts`** — 5 new reviewed suppressions for `load.ts`'s `store.{packManifests,promptRegistry,schemaRegistry,environments,deployments}.put` calls inside `hydrateBundle`'s transaction, each mirroring an already-established precedent for the identical class of write elsewhere in this codebase (`packages/registry/src/import.ts`'s `packManifests.put`, `packages/llm/src/registry.ts`'s `promptRegistry.put`/`schemaRegistry.put`, `packages/mcp/src/handlers/deployment.ts` / `packages/server/src/environments.ts`'s `environments.put`/`deployments.put`): content that has already passed bundleHash content-addressing and real schema validation, structurally outside `resolvedSecretRefs`' scope (no `@aart/expr` import, no live `triggerRun`/`executeRun` call anywhere in this file). `store.workflows.put` in the same loop is not flagged at all — `workflows` isn't in the lint's own `STORE_WRITE_MEMBERS` list (pre-existing, not something this session changed).

**Verification — gates from clean, then the founder's own methodology (A33/A35), then the deploy story live.** `pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`: all green — 217 files / 2092 tests (2075 baseline + 17 new: 12 in `bundle/load.test.ts`, 5 in `cli.test.ts`'s new `--bundle` describe block), 191 redaction-lint suppressions applied (186 baseline + 5 new), 0 kept findings, 0 stale. Full suite re-run a second time from the same build, clean both times (2092/2092 both runs) — non-determinism doesn't hide from a single run. `pnpm --filter @team-monet/aart run build:publish` reports the identical 8-package external list A35/A42 already established (no new dependency leaked in) — `pnpm pack`, installed via `npm install <tarball>` in two independent directories with zero access to this workspace (`find node_modules -iname "@aart*"` → zero matches, both directories), playing the "laptop" and "server" roles. From those two real, independently-installed binaries: laptop registers a workflow, bundles it (bare closure — see the deploy/trigger-add finding below), transfers **only the bundle directory** (not the `.aart` store) to the server side; `aart server --port <n> --bundle <dir>` on a store that didn't exist a moment earlier hydrates then binds, `GET /deployments`/`GET /workflows`/`GET /environments` show exactly the bundled content and nothing else; a second, independent `aart server --bundle <dir>` process against the now-hydrated store reports `kind: "already_hydrated"` (idempotent across process restarts, not just repeated in-process calls); a bundle re-produced for the same `workflowId@workflowVersion` with **different** content is refused (`ok:false`, exit 1, both bundleHashes quoted in the error) *before* the port ever binds, and the store's workflow content is verified byte-identical to before the refused attempt; `aart worker --bundle <dir>` (a second, separate process against the same store) also reports `already_hydrated`; `aart run smoke-deploy-story --input '{"who":"final-demo"}'` genuinely dispatches both steps and completes, visible identically via `GET /runs` on the running server (same store); both processes stop cleanly on a real `SIGTERM` (process confirmed gone, exit path prints `"Server stopped."`/`"Worker stopped."` with the hydration outcome included, ports confirmed released). Full transcript in this session's final report.

**Real findings, surfaced by actually running this rather than assumed — both pre-existing, both out of scope to fix here:**
1. **`aart server`'s real `ServerPort` never wires a `secretResolver`** (`packages/cli/src/real-server-port.ts`'s `startServer` call has no `secretResolver` field at all) — `verifyHmacSignature` is unconditionally mandatory for `webhook`/`github`/`slack` triggers (architecture §6.1/§15: "a delivery with a missing/invalid signature is rejected... not just logged"), and with no resolver wired, `binding.webhookHmacSecretRef ? await config.secretResolver?.(...) : undefined` always resolves to `undefined` regardless of what the trigger config names — meaning **no HTTP-delivered trigger can currently fire successfully through the shipping `aart server`**, independent of anything this session touched. Discovered while trying to build a live webhook-firing demo for this exact deploy story; worked around for this session's own proof by using `aart run` directly (the brief's own offered fallback) against the hydrated store instead. Whoever wires `--environment`/secret-source resolution next (item 2, or a dedicated secrets session) should know this gap exists independent of that work.
2. **The CLI surface alone has no path to create the workflow-version-level `ApprovalTask` `aart approve` needs** — `commands/governance.ts` has `approveCommand` (decide on an existing `taskId`) but nothing that mints one (`aart_request_approval` is MCP-only today). Combined with A21's dev-mode-never-vacuously-approves rule, this means **`aart deploy` cannot currently be made to succeed from the CLI alone**, in any trust mode, without going through MCP first — confirmed by direct attempt (`governed`: refused with `unmetGates: ["validate","humanReview"]`; `AART_TRUST_MODE=dev`: still refused, `unmetGates: []` but `workflow.approval` never left `"draft"`). This is why this session's live proof bundles a bare (undeployed) workflow closure rather than one with a live trigger attached — also pre-existing, also out of scope here.

**What's left, explicitly, for whoever gets founder confirmation to proceed:** item 2 (`aart server --environment <name>` / `AART_ENVIRONMENT`, filtering `loadTriggerBindingsFromDeployments` — the function's own `list()` call already accepts an `{ environmentId }` filter; unset stays today's all-environments behavior), item 3 (add a `"./sqlite"`-equivalent export — A19's own resolution already did this for `@aart/store`'s package.json; `@aart/store`'s root `index.ts` re-export plus a CLI `--store fs|sqlite` flag is what's left — the adapter itself needs no rework, its conformance suite already ran clean as part of this session's own full-suite run), item 4 (`--root <dir>` / `AART_ROOT` exposing `CreateAartContextOptions.root`, already threaded internally end-to-end, just never surfaced as a flag). None of the three has any dependency on what this session built — `--bundle` doesn't need any of them, and they don't need `--bundle` either.

## 2026-07-11 — S13 Deploy-story hardening

### A45 — Webhook secrets wired for real, `aart request-approval` closes the CLI-approval gap, `--environment`/`--store`/`--root` all built, and the known `flushAsync` flake is fixed — plus a genuine new finding: no gate but `humanReview` has ever had a real write path

Founder-approved scope, offered as "Everything (Recommended)" and selected verbatim: the two real findings A44 surfaced (no `secretResolver` wiring; no CLI path to create an `ApprovalTask`) plus the three items A44 scoped out (`--environment`, `--store`, `--root`), plus deflaking the one known-flaky test, plus this wrap-up. Local only — commits, no push, no publish, no LICENSE, no retagging.

**F-A: webhook/github/slack HMAC secrets now resolve for real.** `packages/cli/src/real-server-port.ts`'s `startServer` now passes a real `secretResolver` (new file, `packages/cli/src/secrets.ts`) to `@aart/server`'s `startServer` — the exact call A44 found missing. Verified first that no real secret-resolution convention already existed ANYWHERE to match instead (grepped clean: no `AART_SECRET_*` anywhere, `resolveSecret` falls back to `throwingSecretResolver` at every real call site including `@aart/mcp`'s own `real-context.ts`; `Environment.secretSource` is a real, separate, already-wired concept — an operator-supplied vault-path/provider descriptor the dashboard already renders read-only, `packages/dashboard/src/views/production.ts` — but nothing anywhere FETCHES a live value from it, no vault client exists, and no CLI/MCP command even sets it) — so this is a new, minimal implementation, not a rewiring: `AART_SECRET_<NAME>` env var first, falling back to a store-adjacent `<root>/secrets.json` (`{"<NAME>": "value"}`), accepting a ref in either the stored `secrets.<NAME>` form (verified against `server.test.ts`'s own fixtures) or a bare `<NAME>`. `createRealServerPort` now takes a third `root` parameter (`cli-context.ts` resolves it once, the same default `createAartContext`/`createRealAartContextWithEngine` themselves use) so the secrets-file fallback reads from the SAME root this invocation's store is actually rooted at.

**F-A proof — live, from the installed binary, not a unit test standing in for it** (full transcript in this session's final report): registered `lifecycle-demo`, deployed to a real `production` Environment, `aart trigger add ... --webhook-hmac-secret-ref secrets.DEMO_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, started `AART_SECRET_DEMO_WEBHOOK_SECRET=<value> aart server --port 8099 --environment production`. `curl` with a correctly-HMAC-signed body → `HTTP 200 {"kind":"started","runId":"..."}`; a real `aart worker` then claimed and completed that exact run (`GET /runs/<id>` → `"status":"completed"`). `curl` with a wrong signature → `HTTP 401 {"error":"bad_hmac"}`, and `GET /rejected-triggers` shows the durable record (`"reason":"bad_hmac"`). Also added as permanent regression coverage (`packages/cli/src/real-server-port.test.ts`, new — 2 tests) and `packages/cli/src/secrets.test.ts` (new — 7 tests, resolver logic/precedence/normalization in isolation).

**F-B: `aart request-approval <workflowId> [--version <v>]`** (`packages/cli/src/commands/governance.ts`) calls the exact same `requestApprovalHandler` `aart_request_approval` (MCP) calls — already exported from `@aart/mcp`'s top level, so no package-export change was needed, only the CLI command + `cli.ts` dispatch/USAGE wiring. Matches `promoteCommand`/`deployCommand`'s own `workflowId [--version]` shape (defaults to latest registered version).

**F-B proof, and a genuine new finding it surfaced.** Ran the literal 6-step sequence — register, validate, request-approval, approve, promote, deploy — live, from the installed binary, no MCP client, no direct store access:
```
register        -> ok:true, approval:"draft"
validate         -> ok:true, valid:true, findings:[]
request-approval -> ok:true, taskId:"task_...", stepId:"__gate:humanReview__"
approve          -> ok:true, gates.humanReview:"passed", approval:"draft"
promote          -> ok:false, unmetGates:["validate"]
deploy            -> ok:false, error:"Deployment refused: required gates unmet for environment \"production\".", unmetGates:["validate"]
```
`register`/`validate`/`request-approval`/`approve` all genuinely work — `approve` really does record the decision and really does set `humanReview`. `promote`/`deploy` then genuinely refuse. Root cause, verified by reading every real write path in `packages/mcp/src/handlers/{authoring,governance}.ts` and `packages/governance/src/{gates,approval}.ts`, not assumed: **`validateWorkflowHandler` never persists anything** — both its input shapes (a draft string, or `workflowId`+`workflowVersion` "validate an already-registered version by reference") return `{ok, valid, findings}` and stop; `humanReview` is the ONLY gate any real CLI/MCP write path ever touches. `REQUIRED_GATES_BY_MODE.governed`/`.strict` = `["validate","humanReview"]` (`packages/governance/src/gates.ts`), so `validate` stuck at `"pending"` blocks promotion under every mode that isn't `dev` — and `dev`'s own `requiredGatesForMode.length === 0` rule makes `computeApprovalState` return `"draft"` UNCONDITIONALLY (A21's deliberate "never vacuously approve" rule, `packages/governance/src/approval.ts:33-37`), so `dev` mode doesn't help either. Confirmed this is exactly how the codebase's OWN existing test suite already worked around it — `cli.test.ts`'s pre-existing "aart promote / aart deploy" test reaches a promotable state by writing `gates` directly into the store, not through any command, because none exists.

This is a real, separate, pre-existing gap — not something F-B's own addition causes, and not fixable by anything F-B's brief scoped. **Deliberately not fixed here**, for the same reason A44 didn't fix ITS discovered gaps: it's a governance-semantics call (does a successful `aart validate` against an already-registered version automatically satisfy the `validate` gate, or does that gate mean something stricter — e.g. "an external CI pipeline ran and passed"? `validateWorkflowHandler`'s own doc comment already frames a `workflowId`+`workflowVersion` call as "validate an already-registered version by reference," which reads like it was designed for exactly this, but that's this session's inference, not a founder decision) outside a developer's mandate to make unilaterally. Recorded here for explicit confirmation before whoever picks it up next wires it. Encoded as a permanent, precisely-named regression test (`cli.test.ts`'s new "aart request-approval" describe block, 4 tests, including one asserting this EXACT current behavior — `unmetGates` still `["validate"]` after a real approve) so a future fix is a deliberate, acknowledged change to that test, not a silent behavior drift.

The webhook proof above needed a deployed workflow, so it hit this same wall — worked around there (documented inline, both in `TEST-DRIVE.md` and the final report) using the installed package's own exported `createCliContext` to set `gates.validate` directly, the identical mechanism `cli.test.ts` already used, before continuing with genuinely-real `promote`/`deploy`/`trigger add`/`server` commands from that point on.

**F-C: `aart server --environment <name>` (+ `AART_ENVIRONMENT`)** — `loadTriggerBindingsFromDeployments` (`packages/server/src/triggers/registry.ts`) takes an optional `{ environmentId }` filter, a pass-through to `DeploymentStore.list`'s own already-existing filter (both adapters already supported it; nothing there needed changing). Threaded into `ServerConfig.environmentId` (`config.ts`), both call sites that load bindings (`http/server.ts`'s `findBinding`, for webhook/github/slack ingress; `ticker.ts`'s `checkPollTriggers`, for poll ingress) — schedule/cron firing is deliberately NOT scoped, since `Schedule` records carry no `environmentId` of their own to filter by (not a gap, a real data-model boundary). `ServerPort.startServer` (`packages/mcp/src/types.ts`) gained `environment?: string`; `real-server-port.ts` resolves the name to an id (`store.environments.getByName`, throwing a clear error if it doesn't exist — same discipline `resolveDeployment`/`aart bundle --environment` already established). `serverCommand` (`process.ts`) reads `--environment` / `AART_ENVIRONMENT` (flag wins).

Proof: `packages/server/src/http/server.test.ts`'s new describe block, exact test name **"two envs, two deployments, scoped server activates exactly one"** — plus a live rerun against the installed binary (deployed `lifecycle-demo` to both `production` and `staging`, `aart server --environment production` served `production`'s binding, 404'd `staging`'s). `packages/server/src/triggers/registry.test.ts` (new — 3 tests) covers the filter itself in isolation; `cli-context.test.ts` gained the environment-not-found error case, mirroring `produceBundle`'s existing test.

**F-D: `--store fs|sqlite`.** Discovered mid-implementation: `@aart/store`'s `"./sqlite"` package.json export subpath was ALREADY added (commit `274b13d`, A19's resolution) — `packages/store/src/index.ts` correctly still exports only `fs` (a deliberate design: `node:sqlite` stays out of the default import graph). What was actually missing was CLI-side consumption. `cli.ts`'s `run()` now resolves `--store fs|sqlite` (flag only, no env var — none was asked for) before constructing the CLI context: `sqlite` constructs a real store via `createSqliteStore` (`@aart/store/sqlite`) at `<root>/aart.db` and hands it in via the existing `options.store` override seam, so `createCliContext` itself stayed synchronous — the ONE async construction step lives entirely in `cli.ts`'s new `resolveCliContext`. An invalid `--store` value fails with a clear `{ok:false, error}`, not a crash (context construction is now inside `run()`'s own `try`/`catch`, a small deliberate improvement — it wasn't before this session, so any context-construction error, including the pre-existing "real ServerPort needs a real Engine instance" one, used to crash the process instead of returning a clean CLI outcome).

One build-time gap found and fixed along the way: `packages/cli/scripts/build-publish.mjs`'s empirical external-package cross-check demanded `"sqlite"` be added to `package.json` `"dependencies"` — WRONG, there is no installable npm package by that name; `node:module`'s own `builtinModules` list doesn't yet carry `"sqlite"` on this workspace's Node floor (verified directly: `v22.22.2`), since it's still experimental. Fixed by explicitly listing `node:sqlite` in the script's own recognized-builtins set, with a comment explaining why — `pnpm run build:publish` now succeeds and reports the IDENTICAL 8-package external list A44/A42/A35 already established (no new real dependency leaked in; `node:sqlite` correctly stays external as a Node builtin, not a "dependency").

Proof: sqlite's own conformance suite (the same shared suite the fs adapter runs) re-ran clean as part of every full-suite run below — not reworked, per the brief. Live: `--root ./sqlite-root/.aart --store sqlite` created a real `aart.db` file, round-tripped a registered workflow through it, and the SAME root read back with the default `fs` adapter saw nothing — genuinely a different backend, not just a different directory. `cli.test.ts`'s new "--store fs|sqlite" describe block (3 tests) covers this as permanent regression coverage.

**F-E: `--root <dir>` (+ `AART_ROOT`; flag > env > `./.aart`).** `CreateAartContextOptions.root` already existed and was already threaded end-to-end internally (context.ts:34/78/125) — genuinely nothing left to build there except surfacing it as a flag, exactly as A44 said. Resolved once, centrally, in `cli.ts`'s new `resolveCliContext` (shared with `--store`'s own resolution) — applies uniformly to every command, a strict superset of the brief's named five (`server`/`worker`/`run`/`mcp`/`init-agent`) since `run()` only ever builds one context per invocation regardless of which command dispatches. `init-agent` accepts the flag without erroring but has no behavior tied to it — `initAgentCommand` never touches `cli.aart.store` at all, so there's genuinely nothing for `--root` to change there; noted rather than forcing an unasked-for coupling.

Proof: `cli.test.ts`'s new "--root / AART_ROOT" describe block (3 tests: flag isolation, env var honored, flag-over-env precedence) plus a live rerun (registering into `--root ./custom-root/.aart` produced a genuinely separate record from the default root's copy — different `approval` states proved the isolation directly, since this session's own scratch directory had already promoted the default root's copy by that point).

**F-F: the known `flushAsync` flake, fixed properly — poll-until-condition, not a longer sleep.** `packages/server/src/test-helpers.ts` gained `waitFor(condition, {timeoutMs, intervalMs})`, polling the real condition on a 5ms interval (2s default timeout) instead of guessing a fixed duration is long enough. Applied to `worker.test.ts`'s poison-flag test (the one this session's brief named as the confirmed flake) — the specific `flushAsync()` immediately before the flag assertion, replaced with `waitFor(async () => (await fx.store.runs.get(...))?.flag?.kind === "poison")`. Every OTHER `flushAsync()` call site in that file (and elsewhere) was left as-is — not proven flaky, and this session found no evidence of flakiness at any of them across the 5 runs below, so broadening the change stayed out of scope rather than becoming an unproven, speculative rewrite.

**Verification — gates from clean, five times, then the founder's own methodology (A33/A35), then every proof above live.** `pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`: all green — **220 files / 2126 tests** (2101 baseline + 25 new: 3 in new `registry.test.ts`, 7 in new `secrets.test.ts`, 2 in new `real-server-port.test.ts`, 2 in `server.test.ts`'s new `--environment` block, 1 in `cli-context.test.ts`, 10 in `cli.test.ts`'s new `request-approval`/`--root`/`--store` blocks), 191 redaction-lint suppressions (186 baseline, 0 new — this session's changes needed none), 0 kept findings, 0 stale. **Full suite re-run 5 times consecutively from the same build, clean all five** (220/2126 every time) — specifically to prove F-F's fix, not merely asserted: the poison-flag test that used to fail ~1/5 runs under contention passed all 5. `pnpm --filter @team-monet/aart run build:publish` — after the `node:sqlite` builtin-detection fix above — reports the identical 8-package external list every prior session established. `pnpm pack`, installed via `npm install <tarball>` in a directory with zero access to this workspace (`find node_modules -iname "@aart*"` → zero matches): `aart list` on a fresh store returns `{"workflows":[]}`; a `--bundle <dir>` hydration (this session's own `lifecycle-demo`, bundled bare) hydrates cleanly into a second fresh install; the full F-A webhook signed/unsigned proof and the F-B/F-C/F-D/F-E live proofs above were ALL run against this installed binary, not simulated. Verbatim transcripts for all of the above are in this session's final report.

**What's left, explicitly:** the validate/readiness/evals/riskReview gate-satisfaction gap (F-B's finding above) — recommend a narrow, scoped follow-up: wire `validateWorkflowHandler`'s existing `workflowId`+`workflowVersion` branch to persist `gates.validate` (verified this wouldn't break any existing test — `authoring.test.ts`'s own coverage of that branch asserts nothing about gates staying unchanged), which would need `commands/authoring.ts`'s `validateCommand` extended to reach that branch too (today it always reads a file). Deliberately not built here — a governance-semantics call, not a mechanical completion, per this session's own scope discipline. `readiness`/`evals`/`riskReview` have no real write path either, but nothing in this session's own proofs needed them (only `governed`/`strict` mode's `validate`+`humanReview` pair was in play) — not independently investigated further.

### A46 — S14 "Gate write paths": the last product gap closed — `validate`/`readiness`/`evals`/`riskReview` all get real writers, and the full CLI-only governed lifecycle (register → validate → run → request-approval → approve → promote → deploy → bundle → webhook) genuinely completes for the first time

Orchestrator-directed scope under the founder's standing build-program approval and established veto-on-read mode: the gate-semantics decisions below (§17.1's own rule — each gate advanced ONLY by its own mechanism — checked against `/Users/johnlee/Obsidian/03_Projects/Agentic-Automation/aart_product_spec_v2.md` §17 before implementing; no conflict found) were specified in the brief, not offered as a menu. Local only — commits, no push, no publish, no LICENSE, no retagging. Nothing in the brief's premises needed correcting on contact.

**The four writers, each through its own mechanism, all sharing ONE gate-update path.** A45 found that `applyVersionReviewDecision` (`packages/mcp/src/handlers/governance.ts`, the `humanReview` writer behind `aart approve`) was the only place any gate got persisted: read the workflow, merge one gate's new status, recompute `approval` via `ctx.governance.computeApprovalState` (architecture §7.1's sole writer of that field, untouched by this session), persist both together. That inline body is now `applyGateResult(ctx, workflowId, workflowVersion, gate, status)`, exported and shared by all four writers below — not a new abstraction per gate, the SAME one generalized:

- **`validate`** (`packages/mcp/src/handlers/authoring.ts`) — A45's dead branch, wired: `validateWorkflowHandler`'s `workflowId`+`workflowVersion` shape now calls `applyGateResult(..., "validate", result.valid ? "passed" : "failed")` after `ctx.governance.validateWorkflow`. `result.valid` already means exactly "zero error-class findings" (`isValid`, `validation/types.ts`: `findings.every(f => f.severity !== "error")`) — the brief's own "warnings don't block" requirement, for free, not re-derived. **Semantics decision** (the brief left this open, "pick what the gates model already supports and state it"): an errored run writes `"failed"`, not a revert to `"pending"` — strictly more informative (records that a check genuinely ran and did not pass, not merely "never checked"), and symmetric with the evals writer's below-threshold behavior. The draft/in-memory `{workflow: ...}` shape is UNCHANGED — never writes gates, since validating a file is pre-registration and `validate` is a fact about a stored version. `commands/authoring.ts`'s `validateCommand` gained `--registered` (boolean flag: treat the positional as a `workflowId`, not a path, with the sibling commands' own `[--version <v>]`-defaults-to-latest convention) — default behavior (no flag) is byte-for-byte unchanged.
- **`readiness`** (`packages/mcp/src/handlers/execution.ts`) — `runWorkflowHandler` (shared by `aart run`/`aart_run_workflow`, and `aart_verify` indirectly, since `verifyHandler` calls it internally) now calls `applyGateResult(..., "readiness", "passed")` when `finished.status === "completed" && !input.dryRun`. Verified, not assumed: there is no "run a local file directly" path anywhere in this handler or in `aart run` (`resolveWorkflow` always reads from the store; the CLI command only ever takes a `workflowId`) — the brief's "a local file-based run doesn't count" holds by construction. A dry run is excluded explicitly and is the one exclusion that DOES need an explicit check: `params.dryRun` fakes every capability-gated block's dispatch (`step-executor.ts`'s `isDryRun` branch) and still reports `status: "completed"`, so without the check a simulated run would wrongly satisfy a gate whose entire point is "this was proven for real." No waived-guard (deliberately, considered and rejected): kept consistent with the pre-existing `humanReview` writer, which has never special-cased a prior `"waived"` state either, and `"waived"`/`"passed"` are behaviorally identical for `computeApprovalState` — `aart_verify`'s own synthetic workflow (`execution.ts`'s `ensureVerifyWorkflow`, gates pre-`"waived"`) runs cleanly through this same path, just relabels its own readiness gate `"passed"` the first time it's actually run.
- **`evals`** (`packages/mcp/src/handlers/evals.ts`) — reuses `@aart/evidence`'s existing `computeEvalsGateStatus` (`evals/promotion-gate.ts`, S6's exact `evalRun.score >= minScore` threshold comparison, architecture §9.6) rather than re-deriving it: `runEvalHandler` gained an optional `minScore` input; when supplied, `ctx.evidence.computeEvalsGateStatus(evalRun, minScore)` decides passed/failed and `applyGateResult` persists it (+ recomputes `approval`, which the evidence package's own `applyEvalsGate` never did — deliberately NOT the call site used here, see Quality Notes below). Omitted (every pre-S14 caller), behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged — purely informational, no gate write. `computeEvalsGateStatus` is now a real `EvidencePort` method (`packages/mcp/src/types.ts`), mirrored trivially on the stub side (`stubs/evidence.ts` — a fully-specified one-line algorithm has nothing to "fake," same treatment `GovernancePort.computeApprovalState` already got) and wired to the real function on the real side (`real-context.ts`) — the layering every other handler in this package already respects (ports, never a concrete sibling-package import from a handler file). `commands/evals.ts`'s `aart eval run` gained `--min-score <n>`.
- **`riskReview`** (`packages/mcp/src/handlers/governance.ts`) — "no new mechanism, the approval-task machinery is the human-gate machinery": `packages/governance/src/approval-tasks.ts`'s `workflowVersionApprovalSubject`/`decodeWorkflowVersionApprovalSubject` sentinel (`runId: "workflow-version:<id>@<version>"`, `stepId: "__gate:humanReview__"`) is now gate-parameterized (`stepId: "__gate:<gate>__"`, `gate` defaulting to `"humanReview"` — this sentinel's sole use before today). `requestApprovalHandler` gained a `gate?: GateName` input (validated against a 2-element `APPROVAL_TASK_GATES = ["humanReview", "riskReview"]` allowlist — `validate`/`readiness`/`evals` each have their OWN dedicated writer above and must never be settled by a bare human click); `approveHandler` now decodes the gate from the task's own `stepId` (`decodeWorkflowVersionApprovalSubject(task.runId, task.stepId)`, `stepId` a new optional 2nd parameter) instead of hardcoding `humanReview`. `commands/governance.ts`'s `aart request-approval` gained `--gate humanReview|riskReview`, validated at the CLI layer too (mirroring `approveCommand`'s own `--decision` check) before any store lookup. Backward compatibility verified, not assumed: every existing 2-arg `workflowVersionApprovalSubject` call (`recordPrMergeApproval`, `recordStandingApprovalDecision`) and every existing 1-arg `decodeWorkflowVersionApprovalSubject` call (the dashboard's `packages/dashboard/src/views/approvals.ts:31/75` — NOT touched this session, see Quality Notes) still decode `"humanReview"` by default.
- **Defense-in-depth, closing a bypass the request-time allowlist alone leaves open**: `requestApprovalHandler`'s OTHER input shape (raw `runId`+`stepId`, meant for a genuine per-run `human.approval` wait) has no way to recognize a caller is spoofing the workflow-version sentinel with a disallowed gate (e.g. `stepId: "__gate:validate__"`) — so `applyVersionReviewDecision` re-checks the DECODED gate against the same allowlist immediately before writing, independent of how the task was created. Regression-tested directly (`governance.test.ts`'s "a hand-crafted approval task targeting a non-approval-task gate... is refused at approve time").

**Observability, additive only:** `validateWorkflowHandler`'s registered-version branch, `runWorkflowHandler`, `runEvalHandler`, and `promoteWorkflowHandler` now all include `gates`/`approval` in their return payloads (the latter three previously omitted `gates` entirely, `runWorkflowHandler`/`runEvalHandler` omitted both) — needed to make each write actually OBSERVABLE from the CLI's own JSON output for the live proof below, not inferred. Verified no existing test asserts an exact full-object shape anywhere these fields were added (`toEqual` scans came back clean) before adding them.

**Proof — the full CLI-only lifecycle, live, from the installed binary (`packages/cli`'s `team-monet-aart-0.1.0.tgz`), `governed` mode, zero direct store access.** Three independent isolated installs (`~/aart-test-drive-s14`, `~/aart-test-drive-s14-server`, plus a final rebuild re-verified in a third scratch dir), each `npm install`ed from a directory with zero access to this workspace's `node_modules` (`find node_modules -iname "@aart*"` → zero matches, all three). The exact sequence S13's transcript (cited in this session's brief) and A45's own F-B proof showed refusing:

```
register                                  -> ok:true, approval:"draft"
validate <id>.yaml           (file path)  -> ok:true, valid:true            (no gates field -- confirms it never writes)
validate <id> --registered                -> ok:true, gates.validate:"passed"
run <id> --input '...'                    -> ok:true, status:"completed", gates.readiness:"passed"
request-approval <id>                     -> ok:true, taskId, stepId:"__gate:humanReview__"
approve <taskId> --decision approved      -> ok:true, gates.humanReview:"passed", approval:"approved"   (governed's OTHER required gate, validate, already passed above)
promote <id>                              -> ok:true, approval:"approved", unmetGates:[]      <-- THE GAP: A45/S13 showed this ok:false, unmetGates:["validate"]
deploy <id> --target production           -> ok:true, deployment:{...}, environment:{...}
```

Then, beyond governed's own two required gates (confirmed against `gates.ts`'s `REQUIRED_GATES_BY_MODE.governed = ["validate","humanReview"]` before writing anything — `riskReview` is NOT required in `governed`, so it's demonstrated but not load-bearing for the promote/deploy above): `request-approval <id> --gate riskReview` → `stepId:"__gate:riskReview__"`, distinct from the default; `approve` → `gates.riskReview:"passed"`, `gates.humanReview` untouched (still `"passed"`); `eval create` + `eval run <suite> --workflow <id> --min-score 1.5` (score 1, empty suite) → `gates.evals:"failed"`; re-run `--min-score 1` → `gates.evals:"passed"`. With all five gates genuinely `"passed"`, the SAME workflow version was then re-promoted under `AART_TRUST_MODE=production` (`REQUIRED_GATES_BY_MODE.production` = all five) — `ok:true`, `unmetGates:[]` — proving all four writers together, not just governed's pair.

**Bundle → second server → signed webhook**, continuing the deployed version: `aart trigger add --type webhook --webhook-hmac-secret-ref secrets.S14_DEMO_SECRET`, `aart bundle <id> --version 0.1.0 --out ./s14-bundle --environment production` (version pinned explicitly — a bug in MY OWN proof session caught before it mattered: a `--version`-less bundle call would have silently grabbed the newer, unapproved `0.2.0` registered moments earlier for the per-version negative test below, since `bundleCommand` defaults to latest; the deployment itself was unaffected, only the about-to-be-produced bundle would have been wrong). Copied to a SECOND independent isolated install; `AART_SECRET_S14_DEMO_SECRET=... aart server --port 8099 --bundle ./prod-bundle` + `aart worker --bundle ./prod-bundle` (two processes, one hydrated store) — `GET /workflows`/`GET /deployments` show exactly what the bundle carried and nothing else. A correctly HMAC-signed `POST /webhooks/bundle:s14-lifecycle-demo@0.1.0` → `HTTP 200 {"kind":"started","runId":"..."}`; `GET /runs/<id>` → `"status":"completed"`, both steps completed, and the run's own captured `ExecutionSnapshot.definitions.gates` shows all five gates `"passed"` at trigger time. A wrong signature → `HTTP 401 {"error":"bad_hmac"}`, durably recorded on `GET /rejected-triggers`.

**Negative tests, proven BOTH live and as permanent regression coverage:**
- *A workflow with a validation ERROR cannot pass the validate gate* — registered a workflow with an unresolvable block reference (`uses: totally.not.a.real.block`), validated `--registered` against the REAL governance engine (`createRealAartContext` — the stub's class-1-only check can never produce an error finding on an already-schema-valid stored workflow, so this needed the real 5-class engine, same fixture pattern as `real-context.test.ts`'s own "real reference-validation finding" test): `ok:false`, a genuine `class: "reference"` finding, `gates.validate:"failed"` — and `aart promote` then refuses it. Live AND `authoring.test.ts`.
- *An unrun version lacks readiness* — a freshly-registered version's `gates.readiness` stays `"pending"`; a SIBLING version of the same workflow that WAS run keeps its readiness independently. Live (the `0.2.0` promote-refusal transcript below doubles as this) AND `execution.test.ts`.
- *Gates are per-VERSION* — registering `s14-lifecycle-demo@0.2.0` after `@0.1.0` was fully approved/deployed/promoted-under-production starts `0.2.0` completely fresh: `aart promote s14-lifecycle-demo --version 0.2.0` → `ok:false`, every gate `"pending"`, `unmetGates:["validate","humanReview"]`, despite `0.1.0` sitting at full `approval:"approved"` moments earlier in the SAME store. Live AND `authoring.test.ts`/`cli.test.ts`.

**Tests — 29 new** (governance's own `sampleWorkflowYaml`/`createTestContext`/`createTestCli` fixtures throughout, `createRealAartContext` where real 5-class validation was the actual thing under test): `approval-tasks.test.ts` +6 (gate-parameterized sentinel encode/decode, default/explicit/malformed/non-sentinel cases), `authoring.test.ts` +4 (clean→passed, draft-never-writes, per-version isolation, real-governance error→failed), `execution.test.ts` +5 (completed→passed, dry-run-doesn't, failed-run-doesn't, pinned-version isolation, `aart_verify`-waived-compatibility), `evals.test.ts` +3 (meets-threshold→passed, below-threshold→failed, omitted-minScore-untouched), `governance.test.ts` +5 (`--gate riskReview` shape, default-humanReview regression, invalid-gate rejected, approve-writes-riskReview-not-humanReview, the hand-crafted-bypass defense-in-depth negative), `cli.test.ts` +6 (file-path-never-writes, `--registered` clean, `--registered --version` per-version, `--gate riskReview` CLI round-trip, invalid `--gate` CLI error, `--min-score` CLI round-trip) **plus one existing test deliberately rewritten**, exactly as A45's own comment on it anticipated ("a future fix is a deliberate, acknowledged change to that test, not a silent behavior drift") — `cli.test.ts`'s "the full CLI-only lifecycle" test's tail, from asserting `promote` refuses with `unmetGates:["validate"]` to asserting it now succeeds with `unmetGates:[]`, `approval:"approved"`, after inserting the new `validate --registered` step the old test never had.

**Verification — gates from clean, then the full suite three times, then every proof above live.** `pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`: all green — **220 files / 2155 tests** (2126 baseline + 29 new, itemized above). Redaction-lint: **198 findings suppressed** against **167 reviewed entries** (A45's baseline: 191 findings / 161 entries, the same relationship — one entry legitimately matches multiple physical occurrences, e.g. the shared bare `"return {"` lines) — this session's own gate-write returns (`gates`/`approval`-enriched or newly-added `return` lines in `authoring.ts`/`evals.ts`/`governance.ts`/`stubs/governance.ts`/`stubs/evidence.ts`) needed 7 new reviewed entries, 3 pre-existing entries' snippets updated in place to match their (unchanged-shape, added-field) new exact text, and 1 entry removed as superseded (`workflowVersionApprovalSubject`'s now-multi-line return is covered by `stubs/governance.ts`'s pre-existing generic 5-site `"return {"` entry, updated to say 5, not 4) — 0 kept findings, 0 stale, every one individually reviewed, none blanket-suppressed. **Full suite re-run 3 times consecutively from the same clean build, before the live proof below started any process** — 220/2155 every time. `pnpm --filter @team-monet/aart run build:publish` reports the identical 8-package external list every prior session established; `pnpm pack` + isolated `npm install` (three independent times across this session, detailed above) never leaked a single `@aart/*` package. Honest addendum, not smoothed over: ONE post-proof re-run (after the live server/worker session below) briefly showed 3 failures in `cli.test.ts`'s own `--bundle`/worker section — root-caused immediately to this session's OWN leftover `aart worker --bundle` process from the live proof still holding port 8787 (`ps`/`lsof` confirmed it, PID from the exact live-proof timestamp), not a code defect; killed, re-ran clean (220/2155) twice more. A process-hygiene lapse in this session's own manual testing, not a product bug — recorded here rather than quietly re-run past.

**Docs**: `TEST-DRIVE.md` — part (f)'s webhook walkthrough now includes the `validate --registered` step and genuinely succeeds through `promote`/`deploy` (no more "skips past it" workaround language); the "CLI-only-lifecycle gap" paragraph rewritten to describe the four writers and cite this entry; "What doesn't work yet" pruned of the closed gate-satisfaction bullet, replaced with the one genuine residual noted below.

**Quality Notes / flagged, not fixed (deliberately out of this session's CLI-only-proof scope):**
- `packages/evidence/src/evals/promotion-gate.ts`'s own `applyEvalsGate` (S6's store-integrated wrapper around `computeEvalsGateStatus`) was deliberately NOT the call site wired into `runEvalHandler` — it persists `gates.evals` but never recomputes `approval`, unlike every other writer here (including the pre-existing `humanReview` one). Reusing it would mean either a second, inconsistent write (call it, then separately recompute approval anyway) or `gates.evals` silently drifting out of sync with `approval` until the next `promote`/`approve` call happened to reconcile it. Wiring `computeEvalsGateStatus` (the actual "threshold logic" the brief said reuse, not duplicate) directly into `applyGateResult` keeps exactly one gate-update path for all four writers, with zero duplication of the score comparison itself. `applyEvalsGate` is untouched, still exported, still tested (`promotion-gate.test.ts`) — just not this session's chosen writer.
- `packages/dashboard/src/views/approvals.ts`'s `decideApprovalAction` has its own SEPARATE, pre-existing reimplementation of the humanReview gate-write pattern (a `DashboardDeps`-based mirror of the old `applyVersionReviewDecision`, not a caller of it) that hardcodes `gates.humanReview` regardless of which gate a task's `stepId` actually decodes to. A `riskReview` task decided through the dashboard UI (as opposed to `aart approve`) would currently misattribute the decision to `humanReview`. Not fixed — the dashboard's write actions are an already-flagged, separately-tracked partial stub (TEST-DRIVE.md part (e)'s note, unrelated to this session), and this session's mandate/proof were explicitly CLI-only. Noted in TEST-DRIVE.md's "What doesn't work yet" for the next session that touches the dashboard.
- Nothing in the brief's premises was untenable on contact — §17.1 was checked against the spec before implementing (as instructed) and matched the brief's own framing exactly; `REQUIRED_GATES_BY_MODE.governed` was verified (`gates.ts`) to be `["validate","humanReview"]` before assuming `riskReview` didn't gate the required proof sequence.

**What's left, explicitly:** none of this session's own mandate. The dashboard riskReview-misattribution edge case immediately above is the one genuine residual gap this session's own work surfaced.

## 2026-07-11 — B1 Dashboard bug sweep

### A47 — Every dashboard route (read AND write) now goes through `@aart/server`'s HTTP API alone, closing the store-divergence bug class (root A43) end-to-end; the riskReview-misattribution bug A46 flagged is fixed at its source; `aart server`/`aart worker` now refuse to start against a missing store root instead of silently serving an empty one

Founder-directed scope: the founder reported the bug class verbatim ("dashboard that are not reading from server") ahead of his redacted-legacy-a production deployment and directed "we need to fix bugs." Local only — commits, no push, no publish, no LICENSE, no retagging. A concurrent session worked Docker/governance in parallel; this session owned `packages/dashboard` and `packages/server/src/http/**`.

**The class, precisely, and why A43 only closed one instance of it.** A43 fixed workflow/block detail (root AMENDMENTS.md A43) but explicitly flagged the rest: "every OTHER dashboard route that reads `store` directly — risk-diff, corrections, evals, approve/promote — is unaffected... and remains exactly as fragile to the same class of store-misconfiguration." A46 found one more instance while building the CLI-only gate-write lifecycle: the dashboard's own approval-decision path hardcodes `gates.humanReview` regardless of which gate a task's `stepId` actually decodes to. This session enumerated every remaining instance and closed all of them the same way A43 closed the first one — not by patching each symptom, but by making the dashboard's read/write boundary (`ApiClient`) the ONLY thing that ever touches data, so a dashboard process's own store configuration (right, wrong, or entirely absent) can no longer matter.

**Route classification — BEFORE.** `packages/dashboard/src/server.ts`'s 40 routes, by data source:

| Class | Routes |
|---|---|
| Already API-routed (unaffected) | `GET /runs`, `/runs/:id`, `/workflows`, `/workflows/:id`, `/artifacts`, `/waiting-runs`, `/environments`, `/deployments`, `/trigger-configs`, `/secrets`, `/worker-health`, `/flagged-runs`, `/runs/trigger` (form only) |
| Store-direct reads | `GET /approvals`, `/corrections`, `/evals` |
| Store-direct writes | `POST /runs/trigger`, `/workflows/:id/approve`, `/promote`, `/risk-diff` (workflow fetch only — the diff computation itself was already real), `/block-promotion`, `/unblock-promotion`, `/mark-needs-review`, `/clear-needs-review`, `/trigger-improvement`, `/approvals/:id/decision` (**+ the riskReview-misattribution bug**), `/corrections`, `/corrections/:key/update-run-output`, `/create-eval-example`, `/create-issue`, `/evals/suites`, `/evals/runs`, `/flagged-runs/:runId/clear` |
| Store-param-but-inert | `GET /blocks`, `/blocks/:id` (take `store` structurally for `@aart/llm`'s `createLlmPack`, never perform I/O through it — capability-catalog.ts's own doc comment, reconfirmed by reading `createLlmPack`'s body directly) |
| No data source | `/`, `/packs`, `/health`, `/corrections/new`, `/evals/new` |

**Route classification — AFTER.** Every "store-direct" row above now reads/writes through `ApiClient`, extended with 19 new methods (`listApprovals`, `decideApproval`, `triggerRun`, `approveOrDeprecateWorkflow`, `promoteWorkflow`, `blockPromotion`, `unblockPromotion`, `markNeedsReview`, `clearNeedsReview`, `triggerImprovementProposal`, `listCorrections`, `recordCorrection`, `updateCorrectionRunOutput`, `createEvalExampleFromCorrection`, `createIssueForCorrection`, `listEvals`, `createEvalSuite`, `runEvalSuite`, `clearRunFlag`), each implemented in BOTH `createHttpApiClient` (real HTTP) and `createFakeApiClient` (store-backed, for the local/`aart dev` topology — calling the exact same real functions the HTTP endpoints call, not a third reimplementation). "Store-param-but-inert" is unchanged (confirmed, not just assumed, still inert) — see the loud-failure section below for why this doesn't need a check of its own.

**Endpoints added to `packages/server/src/http/server.ts`** (append-style, matching the existing router idiom — the A43-added `GET /workflows/:id` pattern): `GET /approvals[?status=]`, `GET /corrections[?runId=&stepId=]`, `GET /evals` (reads); `POST /runs/trigger`, `/workflows/:id/approve`, `/promote`, `/block-promotion`, `/unblock-promotion`, `/mark-needs-review`, `/clear-needs-review`, `/trigger-improvement`, `/corrections`, `/corrections/:key/update-run-output`, `/create-eval-example`, `/create-issue`, `/evals/suites`, `/evals/runs` (writes); `POST /approvals/:id/decision` extended (see below). Backed by four new small domain modules, mirroring this package's existing one-file-per-domain convention (`flags.ts`, `promotion.ts`):

- **`packages/server/src/approvals.ts`** (new) — `decideApprovalTask`, the ONE real implementation `POST /approvals/:id/decision` now calls (see riskReview fix below).
- **`packages/server/src/workflow-actions.ts`** (new) — `approveOrDeprecateWorkflow`, relocated from a dashboard-local implementation (`stub-deps.ts`'s pre-A47 `approveOrDeprecateWorkflow`) with identical logic.
- **`packages/server/src/corrections.ts`** (new) — `findCorrectionByKey`, relocated identically from dashboard's pre-A47 local version.
- **`packages/server/src/evals.ts`** (new) — `createEvalSuite` (relocated identically) and `runEvalSuiteForWorkflow`, which now uses `@aart/evidence`'s REAL 12-kind scorer registry (`createScorerRegistry`/`runEvalSuite`) instead of the dashboard's former 1-kind (`exact_match`-only) local stub — a genuine fidelity improvement, not just a relocation, that cost nothing extra to wire.
- **`packages/server/package.json`/`tsconfig.json`** — added `@aart/evidence` as a new dependency (it only depends on `@aart/store`+`@aart/types` — no heavier dependency graph than `@aart/governance`, which `@aart/server` already had). The correction-outcome writes (`recordCorrection`, `updateRunOutput`, `createEvalExampleFromCorrection`, `createIssueForAgent`, `triggerImprovementProposal`, `blockPromotion`, `unblockPromotion`, `markNeedsReview`, `clearNeedsReview`) call `@aart/evidence`'s real, already-published functions directly (the same ones `packages/mcp`'s handlers already call via `ctx.evidence`) rather than re-deriving dashboard's former local mirrors — matching architecture's own three-client principle ("every writable action... calls the SAME underlying functions") for real, for the first time, on this axis.
- **`packages/server/src/engine/boundary.ts`** — `StartRunParams` gained an optional `environment?: string`, threaded into `createRealEngineBoundary`'s `engine.triggerRun` call — the dashboard's pre-existing "trigger workflow" form has always had an optional Environment field; without this the new `POST /runs/trigger` endpoint would have silently dropped it. Purely additive (optional field, no existing caller affected).

**Two behavior refinements surfaced by relocating to the real implementations (not scope creep — surfaced by the move itself, not separately sought):** (1) `@aart/evidence`'s real `updateRunOutput` patches the target STEP's own `trace.outputs` (spec §23.3's `(runId, stepId, fieldPath)` scoping applied literally), not `RunRecord.outputs` directly the way the dashboard's former "outputs."-prefix special case did — the pre-A47 dashboard behavior didn't match the spec's own documented semantics. (2) `runEvalSuiteForWorkflow` now verifies the target workflow version exists (matching `packages/mcp/src/handlers/evals.ts`'s real `runEvalHandler`) and reads `store.evals.listExamples` as authoritative over the suite's own embedded `examples` array when non-empty (same file's own documented store-shape note) — the pre-A47 dashboard action checked neither.

**The riskReview-misattribution fix (root AMENDMENTS.md A46).** `packages/server/src/approvals.ts`'s `decideApprovalTask` decodes `decodeWorkflowVersionApprovalSubject(task.runId, task.stepId)` — BOTH arguments, unlike the deleted dashboard-local `decideApprovalAction` (`packages/dashboard/src/views/approvals.ts`, pre-A47), which called the 1-arg form (`t.runId` alone) and then hardcoded `gates.humanReview` in the write regardless of what the (never-consulted) `stepId` actually encoded. The new function writes to `gates[decoded.gate]`, never a fixed key, and repeats the SAME defense-in-depth `@aart/mcp`'s `applyVersionReviewDecision` already established (a hand-crafted task whose `stepId` decodes to a gate outside `["humanReview", "riskReview"]` — e.g. `validate` — is refused with `invalid_gate`, task decision still recorded, workflow gates untouched). `@aart/server` cannot import `packages/mcp/src/handlers/governance.ts` directly (dependency direction runs the other way — `@aart/mcp` depends on `@aart/server`, confirmed via package.json before writing this), so the small `APPROVAL_TASK_GATES` allowlist constant is a deliberate, narrow duplication of policy data, not a divergent reimplementation of the decode mechanism itself (both read the same `GateName`s off the same governance-owned decode function). `packages/dashboard/src/views/approvals.ts`'s `renderApprovalQueuePage` (still real, read-only) now ALSO decodes with `t.stepId` and labels each pending row `"(humanReview review)"` / `"(riskReview review)"` distinctly — previously every version-review row said only `"(version review)"`, giving no visual signal a `riskReview` request even existed.

**Composition-time loud failure (`packages/cli/src/cli.ts`).** `aart server`/`aart worker`, when NOT given `--cliContext` (i.e. the real `--root`/`AART_ROOT`/default resolution path, not a test override) and NOT given `--bundle` (hydrating a fresh root is the entire point of that flag, AMENDMENTS.md A44/A45 — a missing root there is expected, not a misconfiguration), now refuse to start if the resolved store root doesn't exist on disk, with a clear, actionable error, BEFORE the port ever binds. This is deliberately scoped to the composition/launch layer only — `@aart/store`'s fs adapter's own lazy ENOENT-as-empty read semantics are UNCHANGED (the conformance suite and a genuinely-fresh `aart register`/`aart init` flow both depend on them). `--store sqlite`'s own existing `mkdir -p` behavior is unaffected (by the time this check runs, that path already exists). Scoped to `server`/`worker` specifically (not `mcp`, not authoring commands like `register`/`run`, which legitimately start against a not-yet-existing root) — these are the two long-running, serve-existing-data processes root AMENDMENTS.md A43's actual founder-visible bug was about. The dashboard's own equivalent risk is closed differently and, arguably, more strongly: since NOTHING in the dashboard's router touches a store for data anymore (only the inert Blocks/Packs structural parameter, confirmed above), `startDashboard` makes `DashboardConfig.store`/`.deps` fully OPTIONAL, defaulting internally to an always-valid, never-read `createFsStore(os.tmpdir()/...)` placeholder when omitted — "the bug can't happen" rather than "we'll detect it," and it's what the founder-facing `TEST-DRIVE.md` script now does by default (no store construction at all).

**Live verification, as the founder — tarball install, zero local store, two-gate approval walk.** Built `packages/cli`'s real publishable bundle (`pnpm --filter @team-monet/aart run build:publish` — identical 8-package external list to every prior session, confirming `@aart/evidence` was correctly inlined as a workspace package, not leaked as a new real dependency), packed it, `npm install`ed into an isolated directory with zero access to this workspace (`find node_modules -iname "@aart*"` → zero matches). From that installed binary: registered/validated/ran `smoke-b1` for real, requested BOTH gates (`aart request-approval smoke-b1 --version 0.1.0` and `... --gate riskReview`), started `aart server --port 8180` against that install's own store. Separately, from THIS repo's `packages/dashboard/dist` (the dashboard is a private, never-published package — SEAMS.md/TEST-DRIVE.md's own established note), started the dashboard in a directory confirmed empty before AND after (`ls -la`, no `.aart` ever appeared) via the new minimal launch script (`{api: createHttpApiClient(url), port}` — no `store`, no path). `curl`-walked all 19 distinct dashboard routes: every one 200 (or the correct 303 for `/`), the approvals queue showing `smoke-b1` labeled `(humanReview review)` AND `(riskReview review)` distinctly. Decided the riskReview task through the dashboard (`POST /approvals/<id>/decision`) — server-side confirmed `gates.riskReview: "pending"→"passed"`, `gates.humanReview` untouched at `"pending"`; decided the humanReview task next — `gates.humanReview→"passed"`, `approval→"approved"` (governed mode). Triggered a run through the dashboard's form — appeared correctly in the server's own `GET /runs` (not lost, not duplicated). Both process logs (`server.log`/`dashboard.log`) clean of errors throughout. Separately confirmed the loud-failure check against this SAME installed binary: `aart server --root ./this-does-not-exist/.aart` → `{"ok":false,"error":"Store root \"./this-does-not-exist/.aart\" does not exist. Refusing to start..."}`, port confirmed never bound (`curl` connection refused).

**Tests.** Four new files (`packages/server/src/approvals.test.ts` — 10 cases, the riskReview-fix coverage; `workflow-actions.test.ts` — 5; `corrections.test.ts` — 5; `evals.test.ts` — 7; 27 new cases total). Extended existing files: `http/server.test.ts` +14 (the new HTTP endpoints, including the riskReview-over-HTTP case and the hand-crafted-bypass 400); `packages/dashboard/src/api-client.test.ts` +11 (every new `ApiClient` method, both the store-backed fake AND a real `@aart/server` instance over real HTTP — not hand-rolled response mocking); `packages/dashboard/src/server.test.ts` +1 (a single test walking EVERY remaining v1/v2/v3 read AND write through a deliberately-divergent local store, mirroring A43's own workflow-detail proof pattern, extended to this session's scope); `packages/cli/src/cli.test.ts` +6 (the composition-time root check: missing root refused, existing root proceeds, `AART_ROOT` honored, `--bundle` bypasses it, an explicit `cliContext` bypasses it). 26 test cases removed from `packages/dashboard/src/views/*.test.ts` (`approvals`/`corrections`/`evals`/`flags`/`runs`/`workflows`), each covering one of the 15 action-delegate functions deleted from those files — that coverage now lives against the real server-side implementations instead of a dashboard-local mirror of them (the riskReview-specific case in particular is now covered MORE thoroughly, server-side, than the deleted dashboard-local test ever was).

**Verification — gates from clean, three times, plus every live proof above.** `pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`: all green — **224 files / 2192 tests** (2155 baseline going in; redaction-lint: 199 reviewed suppressions applied, 0 kept findings, 0 stale — one stale entry from the relocated approval-decision write updated to its new location, one new entry added for `api-client.ts`'s own `clearRunFlag`, mirroring `packages/server/src/flags.ts`'s already-reviewed identical pattern). Full suite re-run 3 times consecutively from the same clean build — 224/2192 every time, no flakiness.

**Found but out of scope, flagged rather than silently absorbed or discarded:** `packages/dashboard/src/http/html.ts` and `http/html.test.ts` were found modified (a dashboard UI/CSS redesign — dark theme, sidebar nav, status badges) by a process OTHER than this session partway through this session's own work (file mtimes ~12 minutes before this session's own first edit; `ps` showed several other concurrent `claude` CLI processes on this machine). Neither reverted nor committed by this session — left exactly as found, excluded from this session's own commits, flagged here for whoever reconciles it (this session's own `page()`-consuming code is unaffected either way: the function's signature and every route's use of it are unchanged, confirmed by this session's own full green test run against the current state of that file).

**What's left, explicitly:** `resumeApproval` (per-run wait continuation, as opposed to a workflow-version gate decision) and report rendering are the two remaining `DashboardDeps` fields that are genuinely still local mirrors rather than the real `@aart/engine`/`@aart/evidence` composition — narrower than it sounds, since neither was ever store-path-dependent, so neither was part of the bug class this session closed (documented in TEST-DRIVE.md's "What doesn't work yet"). Extending the composition-time root check to `aart mcp` would be a small, natural, low-risk follow-up not done here (out of this session's literal "dashboard (or server)" scope). `DashboardDeps`'s now-superseded write-action fields (`triggerRun`, `decideApprovalTask`, `recordCorrection`, etc.) are left in place, unused by `server.ts`'s router but still constructible via `createStubDeps` — a deliberate, smaller-diff choice over also deleting them from the type, flagged for a future cleanup pass rather than silently left unmentioned.
