## 2026-07-11 — S15 Production deploy kit + the S11/A42 governance-permissiveness question, settled

### A48 — The S11/A42 governance-permissiveness finding, settled: NOT spec-faithful, a real bug — the capability-dispatch chokepoint (architecture §4.6) silently used a HARDCODED `"dev"` trust mode (unconditional capability grant) whenever a run carried no `environment`, which is every `aart run`/`aart_run_workflow` call and, independently, every trigger-fired (webhook/github/slack/poll/etc.) run too; both gaps closed, 16 new tests pin the settled semantics per mode × entry point

**The question, and the verdict up front.** S11 (AMENDMENTS A42, finding 3) observed: "A workflow with an unapproved, Medium-risk capability (`file.write` via `artifact.write`) still ran to completion under `aart run` in `governed` mode, despite `aart_validate`'s own finding wording... reading as if it should be hard-blocked" — recorded as an honest, unresolved observation, explicitly not investigated ("renegotiating governance's own risk-tier/trust-mode capability-grant policy is squarely outside a composition-wiring session's mandate"). This session's brief: settle whether spec/architecture require run-time gating here, and either implement the fix or document precisely why current behavior is correct. **Verdict: the spec/architecture DO require it (§17.2, architecture §4.6/ADR-09), and the shipped code was NOT meeting that requirement — a genuine, previously-undiagnosed bug in the DI wiring, not a documented design tradeoff and not "working as intended."** Fixed, per mode × entry point, with regression tests pinning the settled behavior at every one of the four entry points the brief named (CLI `aart run`, MCP `aart_run_workflow`, trigger-fired via server, worker-claimed).

**Spec/architecture grounding, read before touching any code (this session's own veto-on-read discipline, matching A46's).**
- Spec §17.2: trust modes are `dev`/`governed`/`strict`/`production`; "Local development default: **governed**... CI/production default: **production**... Experimental override: **dev**." `dev` is the opt-IN escape hatch for frictionless local iteration ("draft workflows can run with warning") — it is explicitly NOT the default, and nothing in the spec ties it to "no environment was specified for this run."
- Architecture §7.3 (`packages/governance/src/gates.ts`'s `REQUIRED_GATES_BY_MODE`, pre-existing and correct, untouched this session): governs GATE/PROMOTION requirements (draft→approved), not run-time dispatch directly.
- Architecture §4.6 (ADR-09) is the actual RUN-TIME enforcement chokepoint: "Before ANY block executes: `declared = block.capabilities`; `granted = governance.getGrantedCapabilities(workflowVersion, environment)`; assert `declared ⊆ granted`; else: fail step with `CapabilityDeniedError`." `@aart/governance`'s real `getGrantedCapabilities` (`capability.ts`, pre-existing and correct in isolation, untouched this session): `dev` mode grants the full capability closure unconditionally ("dev... is not capability-gated, the warning IS the enforcement" — its own doc comment); every other mode grants the full closure only if the workflow version is globally `"approved"` or a standing approval covers it; otherwise **nothing** is granted — fail-closed by design.
- Architecture §17 (A19's own correction) states trust modes are explicitly "a runtime execution posture," not a deployment-environment concept. Checked for an "attended vs unattended" mechanism as an alternative reading (the brief flagged this as one possible needle-threading) and ruled it out: no such field or concept exists anywhere in `@aart/types`, spec, or architecture (no `RunRecord.attended`, nothing comparable). The mechanism the spec/architecture actually built for authoring-loop freedom is `dev`-vs-`governed+` (a MODE a caller opts into), not a property of which surface issued the call — confirmed by `getGrantedCapabilities`'s own pre-existing, correct `trustMode === "dev"` branch, which is precisely this escape hatch already implemented and already tested (`packages/governance/src/capability.test.ts`) in isolation. The bug is entirely in how a real run's `trustMode` input reaches that already-correct function.

**Where it actually broke — a genuine split-brain between two independently-resolved trust-mode signals, not a single bug in one place.** `getGrantedCapabilities`'s policy function is correct. The bug is in the ADAPTER that resolves its `trustMode` argument for a real run (`packages/mcp/src/real-context.ts`'s `createGetGrantedCapabilities`, before this session):

```ts
let trustMode: TrustMode = "dev";                    // unconditional, un-overridable
if (environment) { /* resolve from Environment.config.trustMode, correctly */ }
```

`environment` here is `TriggerRunInput.environment` (architecture §4.6: "Threaded through to `getGrantedCapabilities` on every step dispatch for this run's lifetime") — and **nothing in the shipped code ever supplied one, anywhere**, for two independent reasons:

1. `RunWorkflowInput` (`packages/mcp/src/handlers/execution.ts`'s `runWorkflowHandler`, shared verbatim by CLI `aart run` — `packages/cli/src/commands/authoring.ts`'s `runCommand` — and MCP `aart_run_workflow`, per this repo's own three-clients principle) has no `environment` field at all; it never existed as a concept on this surface. Every direct run, forever, resolved `trustMode = "dev"` inside the capability chokepoint — full, unconditional grant — **regardless of `ctx.trustMode`**, the SEPARATE, CORRECTLY-resolved ambient trust mode (`context.ts`'s `resolveTrustModeFromEnv()`, defaulting to `"governed"` per spec §17.2, `AART_TRUST_MODE`-overridable) that `runWorkflowHandler` was already passing as `approvalMode: ctx.trustMode` into `triggerRun` the whole time — correctly recorded onto `RunRecord.approvalMode` for audit purposes, but never once consulted by the actual dispatch-time enforcement. `aart_validate`'s finding wording is produced from this SAME correct `ctx.trustMode`, via `createRealGovernancePort(catalog.blocks, trustMode)` feeding `ValidationContext.trustMode` — which is exactly why the finding read as if it should have blocked: validate and dispatch were silently reading two different trust-mode sources that happened to agree everywhere except the one place that mattered.
2. Independently, on the trigger-fired (webhook/github/slack/poll/queue/database/email/file/sdk — every type sourced from `Deployment.triggerConfig`) side: `Deployment.environmentId` (a real, populated field on every deployment, already used to FILTER which deployments load — `loadTriggerBindingsFromDeployments(store, {environmentId})`, A45) was never carried ONTO the resulting `TriggerBinding` (`packages/server/src/triggers/registry.ts`'s `deploymentToBinding` set `deploymentId` but not `environmentId`), and `StartRunParams` (`packages/server/src/engine/boundary.ts`) had no `environment` field for `processTriggerIntake` (`packages/server/src/triggers/intake.ts`) to pass one through even had it been available. An unattended, deployed, webhook-fired execution of an unapproved workflow against a `production`-trust-mode environment got the exact same accidental unconditional-grant bypass as a direct `aart run` — arguably the more serious half of this finding, since unattended/triggered execution is precisely the case the trust-mode design exists to protect (nobody is watching a webhook fire).
3. Worker-claimed execution (`EngineBoundary.executeClaimedRun` → `Engine.executeRun`) needed no separate fix once (1)/(2) closed: `environment` is captured ONCE into `RunRecord.params.environment` at trigger time (`packages/engine/src/run-lifecycle.ts`'s `environmentOf(run)`) and re-read on every step dispatch for that run's ENTIRE lifetime, including worker-resumed continuations after a wait or a worker restart — this is the same chokepoint, not a fourth independently-behaving copy. Verified directly, not assumed: a dedicated test (`boundary.test.ts`) constructs a real capability-policy engine and proves `executeClaimedRun` denies/allows identically to the direct-run path, rather than inferring it from the plumbing alone.

**The fix, per file:**
- `packages/governance/src/capability.ts` (+ `index.ts` export) — new `normalizeEnvironmentTrustMode(raw: unknown): TrustMode`, factoring out the "unrecognized/absent → `governed`" fallback that used to be duplicated inline in exactly the kind of silently-diverging way that produced this bug; now the one place this normalization happens, reused by both fixes below.
- `packages/mcp/src/real-context.ts` — `createGetGrantedCapabilities`/`createRealEngine` both gained a REQUIRED (not defaulted) `trustMode`/`defaultTrustMode` parameter. The bug was a silent, un-overridable literal default; the fix removes the ability to silently default at all, not just changes what the default is — TypeScript now enforces every call site states its trust mode explicitly. `packages/mcp/src/context.ts`'s `createRealAartContextWithEngine` (the actual composition root every CLI/MCP entry point is built through) threads its already-resolved `trustMode` through. Two E2E fixtures that construct a real engine directly and knowingly rely on unconditional dev-mode grants for reasons unrelated to capability-gating (`packages/mcp/src/e2e/redacted-legacy-b-worker.mjs`, `redacted-legacy-a.e2e.test.ts` — both already declare `capabilities: []` on every fixture block AND already document "dev trust mode... grants the full capability closure unconditionally" in their own header comments) now pass `"dev"` explicitly — byte-identical behavior, now honest about it instead of silently inheriting a default.
- `packages/server/src/triggers/types.ts` / `registry.ts` — `TriggerBinding` gained `environmentId?: string`, populated from `Deployment.environmentId` in `deploymentToBinding`.
- `packages/server/src/engine/boundary.ts` — `StartRunParams` gained `environment?: string`; `createRealEngineBoundary`'s `startRun` threads it into `engine.triggerRun`, AND (since this exact call site was already being touched) now captures `RunRecord.approved`/`approvalMode` HONESTLY from the target environment's real `config.trustMode` when one resolves, instead of silently inheriting the engine's own generic `{approved: true, approvalMode: "dev"}` fallback — a webhook-fired run against a `production` environment now correctly records `approvalMode: "production"` in its own audit trail, not `"dev"`.
- `packages/server/src/triggers/intake.ts` — `processTriggerIntake` threads `binding.environmentId` into `engine.startRun`.

**Settled semantics, per mode × entry point (this is exactly what the new tests pin):**

```text
                            dev              governed / strict            production
CLI `aart run` /            full grant,      DENIED unless approved       DENIED unless approved
MCP `aart_run_workflow`     unconditional    or a standing approval       or a standing approval
(no --environment concept                    covers it (fail-closed)      covers it (fail-closed)
exists on this surface)

trigger-fired                full grant,     gated by the DEPLOYED        gated by the DEPLOYED
(webhook/github/slack/       unconditional   target environment's REAL    target environment's
poll/queue/database/email/                   config.trustMode             REAL config.trustMode
file/sdk — every type
sourced from
Deployment.triggerConfig)

worker-claimed                inherits whatever was captured in RunRecord.params.environment at
(EngineBoundary.               trigger time (above), for this run's ENTIRE lifetime, including
executeClaimedRun)             resumed continuations — the SAME chokepoint, not a separate path
```

Authoring-loop freedom is real, was never actually at risk, and remains fully available: a workflow using only zero-capability blocks (pure data/logic/assertion steps — the founder's own A42 test-drive smoke workflow, `data.stringify` + `assert.contains`) runs unconditionally in every mode regardless of approval state, since `declared ⊆ granted` is vacuously true for an empty `declared` set; a capability-bearing draft can still be run for real by explicitly opting into `dev` (`AART_TRUST_MODE=dev`), exactly as spec §17.2 documents it. The fix removes an ACCIDENTAL, un-auditable, mode-independent bypass — not the documented, opt-in one.

**Residual gap, flagged not fixed (scope discipline, not an oversight).** `schedule`-sourced triggers (`packages/server/src/ticker/ticker.ts`, `Schedule` store records) have NO environment concept anywhere in their frozen §5.3 shape (`ScheduleSchema`: id/workflowId/workflowVersion/cron/timezone/missedRunPolicy/inputs/paused — no `environmentId`), unlike the 9 `Deployment.triggerConfig`-sourced trigger types this session fixed. A schedule-fired run today still resolves the capability chokepoint against the ambient default the hosting process's `Engine` was constructed with (`"governed"` in a correctly-configured production deployment — NOT the old unconditional `"dev"` bug, so this is not a re-opening of the S11 hole) rather than a specific target environment's real trust mode. Closing this fully needs a `Schedule`-schema migration (new column, `@aart/types` + fs/sqlite adapters + `aart trigger add`'s schedule-creation surface, a store-schema-version bump) — a genuinely separate, larger change spanning frozen store shapes this session's governance-question mandate doesn't cover. Flagged here for the next session that touches scheduling, same pattern as A44's "runtime environment scoping... deliberately NOT built" and A46's dashboard riskReview-misattribution gap.

**Tests — 16 new**, pinning the settled semantics per mode × entry point named above:
- `packages/governance/src/capability.test.ts` +2 (`normalizeEnvironmentTrustMode`: the four real values pass through unchanged; invalid/absent → `"governed"`, never `"dev"`)
- `packages/mcp/src/real-context.test.ts` +5 (governed mode denies the S11 repro exactly — draft workflow, `artifact.write`'s `file.write` capability, no `--environment`; strict and production also deny, proving the fix is trust-mode-general; dev mode still grants — the preserved, opt-in escape hatch; governed mode runs an ALREADY-APPROVED version's identical step fine — approval, not trust-mode alone, unlocks it; a zero-capability draft always runs fine in governed mode — the authoring loop is never blocked for capability-free iteration)
- `packages/server/src/triggers/registry.test.ts` +1 (`deploymentToBinding` carries `environmentId` onto the binding, not just uses it to filter which deployments load)
- `packages/server/src/triggers/intake.test.ts` +2 (`binding.environmentId` threads through to `engine.startRun`'s `environment` param; a binding with none leaves it unset — unchanged)
- `packages/server/src/engine/boundary.test.ts` +6 (environment threads to the persisted run's `params.environment`; omitted stays unset; `approved`/`approvalMode` captured honestly from a real environment's `config.trustMode`; an environment with unrecognized `config.trustMode` normalizes to `"governed"`; two worker-claimed end-to-end tests — a real `checkCapability`+`getGrantedCapabilities` policy engine wired directly, proving `executeClaimedRun` denies an unapproved draft and allows an approved one exactly like the direct-run path, not a fourth independently-behaving copy)

**Verification — the from-clean gate suite, repeated three times for determinism.** `pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`: all green — **220 files / 2171 tests** (2155 A46 baseline + 16 new). Redaction-lint: **198 findings / 198 reviewed entries** — byte-identical to A46's baseline (this session's new code returns plain strings/booleans/DI-adapter closures, nothing persist/emit-shaped the redaction-bypass check flags). Full suite re-run 3 consecutive times from the same clean build: 220/2171 every time, including both real-subprocess flagship E2E tests (`redacted-legacy-b.e2e.test.ts`'s genuine dual-SIGKILL-and-resume across a real child process, `redacted-legacy-a.e2e.test.ts`'s full branch/wait/correction lifecycle) explicitly re-verified green after their `createRealEngine(..., "dev")` argument was made explicit.

**Quality notes:**
- `createRealEngine`/`createGetGrantedCapabilities`'s new `trustMode` parameter was made REQUIRED, not given a new (different, still-silent) default. A function whose entire bug was "a caller gets dangerously-wrong behavior by omission" should not grow a new omittable parameter — every call site in this repo (the composition root + 2 E2E fixtures) was updated to state its trust mode explicitly; the compiler now refuses any future call site that tries to omit it again.
- The `RunRecord.approved`/`approvalMode` audit-field fix on the trigger-fired path (`boundary.ts`) is scoped to the common, deployment-backed case (`params.environment` resolves to a real `Environment`) — a deployment-less trigger binding (manual/cli/sdk source, or the schedule gap above) still falls back to the engine's pre-existing `{approved: true, approvalMode: "dev"}` default, UNCHANGED behavior for that minority case, not a regression this session introduced.
- Nothing in the brief's premises needed correcting on contact — the brief's own suggested needle-threadings ("attended vs unattended, or dev vs governed+ modes") named both candidate mechanisms; the codebase's actual (pre-existing, correct-in-isolation) `getGrantedCapabilities` design already commits to the second one, which is what made this a wiring fix rather than a design decision.

### A49 — The production deploy kit for the founder's redacted-legacy-a server: Dockerfile (lean/browser/test multi-target), docker-compose.yml, DEPLOY.md, and the first-ever Linux run of this codebase (220 files / 2171 tests, clean) — built and empirically proven end to end, not just written

**What was built.** Root `Dockerfile` (`node:22-bookworm-slim`, multi-stage: `base` → `builder` → `test`/`pruned` → `runtime-base` → `runtime` [default target, lean] / `runtime-browser` [`--target`-only, + Playwright's Chromium]), root `docker-compose.yml` (server+worker+dashboard sharing one sqlite-backed volume, secrets via `.env`), root `DEPLOY.md` (compose path, bare-process/systemd path, store guidance, secret management, backup/restore with consistency caveats per store adapter, upgrade procedure including the real engine-schema-version compatibility constraint, and an honest ops-limits section), `.dockerignore`, `.env.example`, and `deploy/` (`entrypoint.sh` — the role dispatcher; `serve-dashboard.mjs` — the `aart dashboard`-equivalent composition-root wrapper; `build-dashboard-launcher.mjs` — esbuild-bundles it). `packages/dashboard` and `packages/server/src/http/**` were not touched (this session's own boundary — the concurrent session on main owns them); the dashboard-equivalent consumes `@aart/dashboard`'s already-published public API from OUTSIDE that package, exactly the way `TEST-DRIVE.md`'s own pre-existing manual `dashboard-dev.mjs` example already did.

**Everything below was actually run, not assumed** — `docker build`/`docker run`/`docker compose up` against a real Colima-backed Docker Engine, arm64, this session's own machine:

1. **Lean image builds clean** (`docker build -t aart:latest .`, ~1.16GB / 245MB content) — full `pnpm install` → `pnpm run build` → `pnpm --filter @team-monet/aart run build:publish` → `pnpm run build:dashboard-launcher` → `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --prod` (prune) → copy into a non-root-user runtime image. `docker run aart:latest register/validate/run` against a real sqlite-backed volume genuinely dispatches real blocks (verified: `data.stringify`+`assert.contains` completes for real).
2. **The full governed lifecycle, containerized, end to end**: register → validate → request-approval → approve → promote → `trigger add --type webhook` → deploy to a `production` target → bundle → hydrate the bundle into a SECOND, independent `server`+`worker` pair (fresh volume, mirroring `TEST-DRIVE.md` part (f)'s own established methodology) → a correctly HMAC-signed `POST /webhooks/bundle:docker-smoke@0.1.0` → `HTTP 200 {"kind":"started",...}` → the run genuinely completes (`GET /runs/<id>` → `"status":"completed"`, both steps `"completed"`). A wrong signature → `HTTP 401 {"error":"bad_hmac"}`, durably recorded on `GET /rejected-triggers`. **This run's own `RunRecord` doubles as a live, in-production confirmation of A48's fix**: `"approved":true,"approvalMode":"governed","params":{"environment":"env_bundle"}` — the webhook-fired run genuinely captured a real target environment and recorded `"governed"` (not the pre-A48 bug's unconditional `"dev"`), because `normalizeEnvironmentTrustMode` correctly falls back to `"governed"` for a synthetic bundle-hydration environment with no explicit `config.trustMode` — not asserted from code reading, observed directly in a real container's real HTTP response.
3. **`aart dashboard`-equivalent works** — `docker run aart:latest dashboard` serves real HTML (`GET /` → 303 → `Runs — AART Dashboard`, real nav links) reading the same store `server`/`worker` write to. Two real integration bugs found and fixed while building this (both documented in `deploy/build-dashboard-launcher.mjs`'s and `serve-dashboard.mjs`'s own header comments, not silently worked around): (a) pnpm's non-hoisted workspace `node_modules` layout means a bare `@aart/dashboard`/`@aart/store` import fails to resolve from a script living outside any real package directory — fixed by esbuild-bundling with the workspace closure inlined, output placed inside `packages/cli/dist/` specifically to piggyback on that package's own already-correct third-party-dependency resolution; (b) `@aart/store`'s `dist/index.js` barrel re-exports a `vitest`-dependent conformance-suite helper that a naive unbundled `node` import would eagerly resolve (and fail on, since `vitest` is correctly absent from a production install) even though the dashboard launcher never calls into that code path — esbuild's tree-shaking (verified: zero `"vitest"` occurrences in the built bundle) avoids pulling it in at all.
4. **`docker-compose.yml` verified as a real multi-service stack**, not just valid YAML: `docker compose up -d --build` brings all three services to `healthy`/`started`; a workflow registered via `docker compose exec server ...` is immediately visible via `docker compose exec worker ... list` — direct proof the shared named volume is one real store, not three independent ones.
5. **`runtime-browser` target verified with a real Chromium launch AND a real workflow dispatch**: `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium` (~1.9GB added, ~3.04GB total image) succeeds; a raw isolate-free Playwright launch+navigate+read succeeds inside the container; a full `browser.goto` → `web.read` → `assert.contains` workflow against `https://example.com`, run via `AART_TRUST_MODE=dev` (spec §17.2's documented escape hatch — the SAME unapproved-draft-with-a-browser-capability-step scenario is correctly DENIED first under the `governed` default, matching A48's settled semantics, then genuinely runs to `"completed"` once `dev` mode is explicitly opted into), dispatches for real — not a stub.
6. **`isolated-vm`'s native addon verified working in-container directly** (task's own explicit ask: "verify — it's a native addon"): a real `Isolate`/`Context`/`eval("1 + 1")` round-trip, run from a location where the addon actually resolves (`packages/cli/node_modules/isolated-vm` — the repo's own `scripts/smoke/isolated-vm.mjs` can't run standalone from an arbitrary path in a production-pruned image for the same non-hoisting reason item 3 above found, not a defect in the smoke script itself, which is designed to run from the workspace root during development, not inside a pruned deploy image).
7. **The full from-clean gate suite + both platform smoke tests, run for the first time ever on Linux**, inside a dedicated, never-shipped `test` Docker target (`docker build --target test .`): `pnpm run smoke:browser` (OK), `pnpm run smoke:isolated-vm` (OK), then `pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test` — **220 test files / 2171 tests, all green**, byte-for-byte matching the macOS baseline (same counts A48 established), redaction-lint clean at the identical 198/198 findings/entries. Includes both real-subprocess flagship E2E tests (`redacted-legacy-b.e2e.test.ts`'s genuine dual-SIGKILL-and-resume across a real child process; `redacted-legacy-a.e2e.test.ts`'s full branch/wait/correction lifecycle) passing on Linux. Scanned the full build log for hidden failures (`grep -niE "error TS|FAIL|✗"`) — zero, beyond test names that legitimately contain the word "fail" as part of their own description.

**Real platform findings, recorded honestly (this session's own DoD framing, matching A42/A45/A46's precedent of reporting what running things for real actually surfaces):**
1. **`isolated-vm@7.0.0` ships prebuilt native binaries for both `linux-x64` and `linux-arm64` inside the npm package itself** (`node_modules/isolated-vm/prebuilds/`), and its own install script (`node-gyp-build || node-gyp rebuild`) is designed to use a matching prebuild without compiling — but on this session's arm64 Linux container, **it compiled from source anyway** (a full g++ build, ~40s, visible in the build log), not the prebuild path. Not investigated further (the Dockerfile's `g++`/`make`/`python3` fallback layer already covers it, and the compiled result works correctly, verified directly) — recorded as a genuine, unexplained platform surprise per the task's explicit ask to report these, not smoothed over.
2. **`pnpm prune --prod` over-pruned this workspace** — tried first, it removed every top-level `node_modules/<pkg>` entry entirely (only `.pnpm`'s internal store survived), breaking BOTH the CLI bundle's externals and the dashboard's workspace-package resolution. `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --prod` (a second install pass against the same already-resolved lockfile, not a `prune`) does the workspace-aware thing correctly instead — this is what the Dockerfile's `pruned` stage actually uses; recorded in that stage's own comment so a future editor doesn't reintroduce the broken version having forgotten why.
3. **`pnpm prune`/`pnpm install --prod` both need `CI=true`** in a non-interactive Docker `RUN` step — pnpm's own interactive "remove node_modules content, are you sure" confirmation has no TTY to answer inside a build, and aborts (`ERR_PNPM_ABORTED_REMOVE_MODULES_DIR_NO_TTY`) without it.
4. **A `.dockerignore` bare pattern without a `**/` counterpart did not reliably exclude nested files** in this session's own Docker/BuildKit setup — `*.tsbuildinfo` alone left every package's stale, host-built `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` in the build context (`dist` alone worked, empirically, likely only because `**/dist` was ALSO listed), which fooled `tsc -b`'s incremental-build detection into skipping real compilation entirely (a `pnpm run build` step completing in 0.3-0.4s across 14 packages was the tell — confirmed by inspection: `dist/` directories were entirely absent from the container despite `tsc -b` reporting success). Fixed by adding an explicit `**/` variant of every pattern in `.dockerignore`, not relying on a bare pattern's assumed any-depth matching. Recorded because this is exactly the kind of "worked on macOS, silently wrong in the container" class of bug this whole verification exercise exists to catch — caught here, not shipped.
5. **Colima's default host-directory bind-mount allowlist does not include arbitrary paths outside `$HOME`** (this session's own scratchpad, under `/private/tmp/...`, silently mounted as an empty directory with no error) — not an AART-codebase finding, a local Docker-Desktop-alternative environment quirk, noted here only because it cost real debugging time before being isolated; all of this session's own container verification commands were run from `~/`-relative paths once identified.

**Tests / verification, not new automated test files** — this part of the session's mandate is operational/infra, proven by the empirical runs enumerated above (items 1-7), not by new `*.test.ts` files; Part 1's own 16 new tests (A48) are the test-file contribution this session made.

**Quality notes:**
- `packages/cli/dist/serve-dashboard.mjs` is a build-time-generated artifact (gitignored, like every other `dist/` output) — the checked-in source is `deploy/serve-dashboard.mjs` + `deploy/build-dashboard-launcher.mjs`; a fresh clone needs `pnpm run build && pnpm run build:dashboard-launcher` (or the Dockerfile, which does both) before the dashboard command works — documented in `DEPLOY.md`'s bare-process path.
- Root `package.json` gained one new devDependency (`esbuild`, version-pinned identically to `packages/cli`'s own existing pin, `0.27.7`) and one new script (`build:dashboard-launcher`) — needed because `deploy/build-dashboard-launcher.mjs` runs from the repo root, which (per the pnpm non-hoisting finding above) has no `node_modules/esbuild` of its own unless the root package.json declares it. `pnpm-lock.yaml` updated accordingly; full gate suite re-verified green after the change, both on macOS and inside the Linux `test` container.
- The schedule-fired-triggers environment gap (A48's own flagged residual) and the dashboard's partial-write-stub status (pre-existing, A46) are both restated in `DEPLOY.md`'s "Ops limits" section in founder-facing language, not just AMENDMENTS.md's internal one — the founder reads DEPLOY.md, not this file, before deploying.
- `docker-compose.yml`'s `dashboard` service depends on `server`/`worker` reaching `service_healthy` before starting — `server`/`worker` both have real `HEALTHCHECK`s (curl against their real `/health` endpoints); the image itself carries no blanket `HEALTHCHECK` (deliberately — the same image serves three different roles on three different ports, so a single Dockerfile-level healthcheck would be role-agnostic and wrong for at least two of the three; per-service healthchecks in `docker-compose.yml` are the correct altitude for that concern, not the Dockerfile).

**What's left, explicitly:** the schedule-trigger environment-scoping gap and the dashboard write-action partial-stub status (both pre-existing, both restated not re-litigated here) are the two genuine residual gaps this session's own work surfaced or confirmed. No LICENSE file, no publish, no push, no retagging — per this session's own explicit local-only mandate.

## 2026-07-11 — F1 Land the facelift, merge the deploy kit

### A50 — The founder's React SPA facelift (`packages/dashboard/frontend/`) landed with fixes from a two-perspective review — redaction chokepoint restored, five regression tests recovered, real `@aart/types` imports surfaced (and fixed) four genuine field-drift bugs beyond the two named ones, dead code removed, root `pnpm run build` now builds the SPA; `b2-deploy-kit` (A48/A49) merged, and the merge's own Docker verification found and fixed a real bug — the containerized dashboard served a 404, not the SPA

Founder-directed: he built the SPA himself (a full rewrite of `packages/dashboard`'s server-rendered surface into a JSON API + React frontend), asked for it to be reviewed ("I believe dashboard change is done, can you review them?"), and a two-perspective review (briefed + cold-read) returned LANDABLE-WITH-FIXES. This session applied those fixes and landed it, then merged the already-approved `b2-deploy-kit` branch (A48/A49, production deploy kit). Local only — commits, no push, no publish, no LICENSE. His SPA design (component structure, routing, visual design, feature scope) is untouched beyond the named fixes below — this session fixed and completed, did not redesign.

**Phase 1 — the facelift, fixed.**

1. **Five regression tests restored** (`packages/dashboard/src/server.test.ts`), adapted from the pre-facelift versions (`git show HEAD:...`) to the new JSON-API surface, not copy-pasted: both divergent-store proofs (root A43/A47's own class — a misconfigured/empty local dashboard store must never break a read once everything routes through `api`; the comprehensive one additionally proves 3 writes — trigger/decide-approval/record-correction — land in the REAL store, never the divergent local one), `create eval / run eval`, `GET /api/worker-health` (an unreachable worker reported distinctly from a healthy one), and `POST .../clear` on an unflagged run (409, not silently absorbed).
2. **Workspace integrity.** `packages/dashboard/frontend` added to `pnpm-workspace.yaml`; `build:frontend` converted from `cd frontend && npm run build` to `cd frontend && pnpm run build` (no npm shell-out inside a pnpm workspace); `pnpm-lock.yaml` regenerated via `pnpm install`, never hand-edited. Reproducibility PROVEN, not assumed: deleted `frontend/node_modules`, ran `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, confirmed the workspace symlinks (`@aart/types`, `@aart/governance`) and `vite` binary came back from the lockfile alone.
3. **Root `pnpm run build` now builds the SPA.** It previously only ran `tsc -b tsconfig.build.json` — pure TS-project-references compilation, which never invoked any package's own `package.json` "build" script, so `packages/dashboard/frontend`'s vite build was never reached by CI's own "Build" step (`ci.yml` calls `pnpm run build` directly) despite existing. Changed to `tsc -b tsconfig.build.json && pnpm --filter @aart/dashboard run build:frontend`. Proven from a genuinely clean tree (`pnpm run clean`, which also wipes `packages/*/dist` recursively including the nested SPA output), not assumed: real artifacts land at `packages/dashboard/dist/frontend/index.html` + assets, timed at ~5-6s total for the full workspace build.
4. **Redaction chokepoint restored.** `GET /api/runs`, `/api/runs/:id`, `/api/artifacts` now route every `RunRecord` through `deps.redact(run, new Set())` before serialization — the same `new Set()` default `packages/evidence/src/redact.ts`'s own `applyRedaction` and this package's `createReportRenderers` already use for a post-persist read (the engine redacts at write time; this chokepoint is defense-in-depth, matching architecture §7.9's own framing, not the primary scrub). Before this fix, the raw `run` JSON field bypassed `deps.redact` entirely — only `reportHtml` (built via the report renderer, which redacts internally) ever went through it; the old server-rendered surface's "values NEVER shown" invariant had a real gap on the new JSON surface specifically. Regression-tested with a test-local `RedactFn` (not the identity stub, not `@aart/governance`'s real `redactRecord` — this test's only job is proving the ROUTE calls `deps.redact` at all; the real algorithm is `@aart/governance`'s own test suite's job) that redacts a planted marker and leaves a control value untouched, across all three routes.
5. **Dead code removed.** The 8 orphaned `views/*.ts` modules (their `*.test.ts` siblings were already gone in the founder's own diff) — superseded by the JSON API + SPA, `server.ts` no longer calls into any of them. `html.ts` pruned from ~400 lines to just `escapeHtml` (the one export `stub-deps.ts`'s report renderer still calls) — `page()`/`table()`/`form()`/`hiddenField()`/`textField()` deleted along with `html.test.ts`'s assertions against them. Every stale doc comment pointing at the deleted `views/` modules updated (`capability-catalog.ts`, `api-client.ts`, `deps.ts`, `stub-deps.ts`, `server.test.ts`'s own header, which now correctly points at `api-client.test.ts`/`stub-deps.test.ts` as where that unit coverage actually lives now).
6. **SPA field/type fixes — real `@aart/types` (+ `@aart/governance` for `SemanticRiskDiff`) imports replaced every `any` across all 9 pages**, which is what surfaced the bugs, not just a typing nicety. Two were named in the brief; typing the rest of the pages the same way surfaced four more of the identical class, all fixed:
   - `run.completedAt` (doesn't exist on `RunRecord`) → `run.endedAt` (`RunDetailPage.tsx`).
   - `run.environment` (doesn't exist) → `run.params.environment` (where the trigger form's optional Environment field actually lands, per `stub-deps.ts`'s `makeTriggerRun` / server.ts's `/api/runs/trigger`).
   - `workflow.blocked` (doesn't exist on `Workflow`) → `workflow.promotionBlocked` (`WorkflowsPage.tsx` — the block/unblock-promotion button was reading the wrong field, so it likely always rendered "Block spec" regardless of actual state).
   - `riskDiff.addedCapabilities` (doesn't exist on `SemanticRiskDiff`) → `riskDiff.newCapabilities` (`WorkflowsPage.tsx` — the risk-diff tool's "new capabilities requested" callout was silently always empty).
   - `run.summary.{passed,total}` (`EvalRun` has no nested `summary` object) → the real top-level `EvalRun.passed`/`.total` (`EvalsPage.tsx` — every eval run's pass/fail summary rendered "0 / 0").
   - The Approvals decision dialog's Trust Mode dropdown offered `"override"`/`"disabled"` — NEITHER is a real `TrustMode` value (the real enum, `packages/governance/src/gates.ts`, is `dev`/`governed`/`strict`/`production`) — corrected to the four real modes with their actual `REQUIRED_GATES_BY_MODE` semantics in the option labels.
   - `router.tsx`'s `Link` component's `[key: string]: any` passthrough replaced with `AnchorHTMLAttributes<HTMLAnchorElement>` (also fixed a latent `React.MouseEvent` reference with no `React` import in scope — untested until this session's typecheck first ran against this file for real).

**Phase 1 — the SHOULD items, same pass.**

7. **Static handler hardening** (`server.ts`'s SPA static-file serving). Resolve-then-verify-prefix (`path.resolve` + `startsWith` the resolved root) replaces the prior strip-a-leading-`"../"`-prefix regex — robust by construction against the FINAL resolved path, not pattern-matching against one specific attack shape. `path.posix.normalize`, not the OS-generic `path` module, for the URL-decoded segment — confirmed empirically (not assumed from spec text) this actually matters: `new URL()`'s own WHATWG dot-segment removal already collapses literal `".."` AND singly-percent-encoded `"%2e"`/`"%2E"` before `.pathname` is even read, but a percent-encoded BACKSLASH (`%5c`) survives untouched, and `path.win32.normalize` (what the OS-generic `path` module becomes on a Windows host) DOES treat a decoded `"\"` as a separator and resolves a decoded `"\.."` upward — `path.posix.normalize` doesn't, verified directly. Generic 500 body (`"Internal Server Error"`, logged server-side via `console.error` — reviewed and added to `redaction-lint-suppressions.ts`, fs/URI errors only, never run data) — never `err.message` to the client. Two new regression tests, one of which also fixed the EXISTING SPA-fallback test's own setup: it fabricated `frontend/dist` (a directory vite never writes to) instead of the real `packages/dashboard/dist/frontend`, so it was silently exercising a dead `getFrontendDir()` candidate, not the real one — fixed with a backup/restore around the real path so the suite doesn't clobber a real `pnpm run build`'s output if one already ran (CI's own Build step runs before Test).
8. **Minimal SPA test coverage** — vitest + `@testing-library/react`, jsdom environment, wired into the frontend as its own workspace package (`vitest.config.ts`/`vitest.setup.ts` — explicit RTL `cleanup()`/`vi.unstubAllGlobals()` in `afterEach`, since this workspace doesn't use vitest globals anywhere else). All 9 pages render against a `mockFetchJson` fetch stub; `RunDetailPage` additionally gets a regression check for the `endedAt`/`params.environment` fix and an explicit escaping assertion (an attacker-shaped step id renders as inert text — no `<img>` element is ever created, its `onerror` never fires). `vitest.workspace.ts`'s `projects` glob (`packages/*/vitest.config.ts`) only reaches DIRECT children of `packages/` — extended with an explicit second entry for the nested frontend project, or `pnpm run test` would silently never run these.
9. **Pruned unreferenced scaffold assets** (`hero.png`, `react.svg`, `vite.svg`, and — found the same way, zero references, same class — `App.css`) and every trivial oxlint `no-unused-vars` finding (unused imports, unused destructured variables, unused catch bindings — `catch (err) {}` → `catch {}` where genuinely unused, matching this codebase's own established convention elsewhere). Left `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` and `react/only-export-components` warnings alone — structural, not trivial, not flagged as wrong.

**Flagged, not fixed (Phase 1's own scope boundary):** the dashboard still isn't bundled into the npm CLI artifact — `@aart/dashboard` remains a private, workspace-only package (reserved-mount placeholder in `@aart/server`; reachable via the container launcher, `deploy/serve-dashboard.mjs`, not the published CLI). An S8-integration follow-up, not this session's to close.

**Phase 2 — merging `b2-deploy-kit` (A48/A49).** `git merge b2-deploy-kit` — two conflicts, both additive (both branches extended the same seam from different directions), resolved by keeping both sides' intent rather than picking one:
- `AMENDMENTS.md` — both branches appended a new entry after the same A46 ending point. Resolved by keeping both in full, chronological order: A46 → A47 → A48 → A49.
- `packages/server/src/engine/boundary.ts` — both branches extended `StartRunParams.environment` and the same `engine.triggerRun(...)` call site (A47: threaded `environment` through for the dashboard's trigger-workflow action; A48: additionally computes `approvalMode`/`approved` honestly from the target environment's real trust mode — the actual S11/A42 capability-gating fix). `b2-deploy-kit`'s version is a strict superset of A47's (it already threads `environment` through AND adds the new capture) — taken as-is; the merged doc comment credits both A47's and A48's reasons for the field.

Two touch-ups the merge made stale, both corrected: `DEPLOY.md`, `docker-compose.yml`, and (found the same way, same class, not explicitly named but the identical staleness) `deploy/serve-dashboard.mjs`'s own header comment all described the dashboard's write actions as "a documented partial stub" (approve/promote/risk-diff real; trigger/resume/report-render still local mirrors) — the B2 session's own understanding, written against the PRE-A47 codebase. Corrected to: API-complete except two narrower gaps (wait-resume, report rendering — TEST-DRIVE.md's own "what doesn't work yet" language, restated here for consistency), and the riskReview/humanReview misattribution bug (A46) is fixed at its source (A47), no longer a live caveat.

**The Dockerfile verification this touch-up asked for found a real bug, not just stale docs.** A genuine `docker build` + standalone `docker run <image> dashboard` served `GET /` → 404, despite `packages/dashboard/dist/frontend/index.html` genuinely existing in the built image (confirmed by `docker exec`ing in and checking directly, before concluding anything). Root cause: `getFrontendDir()` (`server.ts`) guesses the SPA's location relative to its own `__dirname` — correct when `@aart/dashboard`'s compiled code runs unbundled, wrong once `deploy/build-dashboard-launcher.mjs` esbuild-bundles it into `packages/cli/dist/serve-dashboard.mjs` (a DIFFERENT directory, for reasons unrelated to this file — pnpm's non-hoisted `node_modules` layout, that script's own header comment). None of the 4 guessed candidates resolve correctly from there. Fixed with `AART_DASHBOARD_FRONTEND_DIR`, checked before any `__dirname`-relative guess (backward compatible — unset, every existing caller is unaffected); the Dockerfile's `runtime-base` stage sets it to the one path that's actually correct for that image's fixed layout, matching the existing `AART_ROOT` pattern. Verified fixed, not assumed: the same standalone `docker run` served a genuine 200 (real `index.html`, real JS bundle) after the fix. Flagged, not fixed (a composition-root choice in a DIFFERENT session's file, not this entry's to unilaterally change): `deploy/serve-dashboard.mjs` constructs `DashboardDeps` via `createStubDeps(store)` with no override, so item 4's redaction chokepoint routes through `identityRedact` (a documented no-op) in THIS specific launcher — noted in both `DEPLOY.md` and that script's own header comment, not silently absorbed.

**Live verification, the full 3-service compose stack — not just the standalone container.** `cp .env.example .env && docker compose up -d --build`: `server`/`worker` both reach `healthy` (real `HEALTHCHECK`s against their real `/health` endpoints); `dashboard` starts clean after both (`depends_on: condition: service_healthy`). Registered a real workflow (`docker-compose-smoke`) via the SERVER container's own bundled CLI (`docker compose exec server node .../bin.js register ...`) — immediately visible through the DASHBOARD's `GET /api/workflows` and `GET /api/workflows/:id` (proving the dashboard's `ApiClient` genuinely reaches the real server over HTTP, not a stub or a second store) AND through the WORKER container's own independent CLI read (`docker compose exec worker ... list` — proving the shared named sqlite volume is one real store, not three independent ones, matching docker-compose.yml's own header claim). ## 2026-07-11 — F2 Close A50's redaction-wiring residual

### A51 — `deploy/serve-dashboard.mjs` now wires `@aart/governance`'s real `redactRecord` into `DashboardDeps.redact`, closing A50's own second flagged residual — proven in a rebuilt lean Docker image, not just typechecked

Standing build-program approval: closes a flagged gap ahead of the founder's redacted-legacy-a deployment. Local only — commits, no push, no publish, no LICENSE.

**The fix, exactly as flagged.** A50 documented (in this file, `DEPLOY.md`, and the launcher's own header comment) that `deploy/serve-dashboard.mjs` constructed `DashboardDeps` via `createStubDeps(store)` with no override, so `deps.redact` was `identityRedact` — `stub-deps.ts`'s own words, "never-invoked-in-production." Fixed with the same one-line pattern every other real composition root in this codebase already uses (`packages/mcp/src/real-context.ts`'s `createRealEngine`/`createRealGovernancePort`: `redact: redactRecord`, no adapter, since `redactRecord` already matches `DashboardDeps.redact`'s `RedactFn` signature exactly): `deps: { ...createStubDeps(store), redact: redactRecord }`. `deploy/build-dashboard-launcher.mjs`'s esbuild `alias` map gained a matching `"@aart/governance"` entry (`packages/governance/dist/index.js`, identical pattern to the existing `@aart/store`/`@aart/dashboard` entries) — genuinely required, not defensive: verified directly by temporarily removing it and re-running the build, which fails with `Could not resolve "@aart/governance"` (`deploy/`'s own missing `node_modules`, this script's pre-existing documented reason for aliasing anything at all); restored, it builds clean. Both places A50 flagged the gap (this launcher's header comment, `DEPLOY.md`'s "Ops limits") now describe the fix instead.

**A genuine nuance this session's own verification surfaced, not assumed.** `packages/dashboard/src/server.ts`'s `redactRun` chokepoint always calls `deps.redact(run, new Set())` — an EMPTY resolved-secrets set, by that file's own explicit design (defense-in-depth over a record the engine already redacted at write time, not the primary scrub). `redactRecord` only replaces values it's handed via that second argument, so at THIS call site it degenerates to the exact same no-op `identityRedact` always was — confirmed empirically, not just read off the source: seeded a `RunRecord` fixture with a planted marker directly into an fs store (bypassing the engine, same technique A50's own `server.test.ts` regression test uses), ran the real built launcher (`packages/cli/dist/serve-dashboard.mjs`) against a real `aart server` sharing that store, and `GET /api/runs/:id` returned the marker verbatim, both before and after this fix — an inherent property of `server.ts`'s own call site, not a defect in this wiring. Wiring in the real function was still the correct fix regardless (a production composition root has no business carrying a stub documented as never-invoked-in-production, and this closes the gap for any future caller that DOES thread a real resolved-secrets set through) — the decisive proof the wiring itself is correct came from calling `deps.redact` directly with a realistic non-empty set (mirroring the exact override expression, against the real `packages/dashboard/dist`/`packages/governance/dist` artifacts): the post-fix function scrubbed a planted marker while preserving a control value; `identityRedact` given the identical input did not. Reproduced a second time inside a freshly built lean Docker image (`docker build` default `runtime` target; `docker run <image> dashboard` started clean; `docker exec`ing in and importing `packages/governance/dist/index.js` directly confirmed `redactRecord` is present, resolvable, and functional in the pruned production `node_modules`, not just on the host).

**Gates.** `pnpm run build`, `pnpm run typecheck` — both clean. `@aart/dashboard`'s own vitest project — 5 files / 90 tests, all passing (unaffected — this file lives outside that package, per its own stated boundary). Full suite: **225 files / 2177 tests, all green** — byte-identical to the pre-change baseline (HEAD `0caf0fe`), zero regressions. `lint:redaction` (bonus, directly relevant to this change): clean, 200 reviewed suppressions, 0 new findings.

**Flagged, not fixed (out of this entry's own scope):** `deploy/serve-dashboard.mjs`'s header comment still says its bundled output is `deploy/dist/serve-dashboard.mjs` — it's actually `packages/cli/dist/serve-dashboard.mjs` (`build-dashboard-launcher.mjs`'s own `OUT_FILE`, `entrypoint.sh`'s own invocation path). ## 2026-07-11 — F4 First public push, first Actions run: the gitleaks pre-flight recorded, and the fresh main's own CI gate fixed

### A53 — Gitleaks push pre-flight recorded (5 findings, all synthetic redaction-test fixtures, allowlisted); the first GitHub Actions run's 30+-minute hang, fixed at the root — `@aart/familiarity-evals`'s own composition root never closed the real headless Chromium its CI gate leaves running

Founder-directed, urgent: the repo's first push to `github.com/team-monet/aart` (HEAD `b988a04`) triggered the first-ever real GitHub Actions run (29148548882) — it hung 30+ minutes and was cancelled, blocking the founder's own planned deploy. Local only — commits, no push (the orchestrator pushes after independently verifying this fix).

**Gitleaks pre-flight, folded in — `b988a04`'s own commit message named this record and it didn't exist yet.** Ahead of that push, a gitleaks history scan surfaced 5 findings, all in redaction-test fixtures that deliberately contain secret-*shaped* synthetic markers (the whole point of those tests is proving the redaction machinery scrubs values of that shape) — spot-verified directly, not just trusted: `packages/governance/src/redact.test.ts`'s `SECRET = "sk-live-9f8a7b6c5d4e3f2a1b0c"`, `packages/dashboard/src/server.test.ts`'s `SECRET = "sk-test-planted-secret-9f2a"` (the name says it outright). All 5 (those two plus `packages/governance/src/redact-adversarial.test.ts`, `packages/evidence/src/redact.test.ts`, `packages/mcp/src/redaction-adversarial.test.ts`) allowlisted by path in `.gitleaks.toml`; any new/other finding still reports.

**Root cause of the hang, file:line.** GitHub Actions run 29148548882: every step through `Lint — redaction bypass scan` passed for real, then **"Familiarity-evals CI gate (fake model)"** (`pnpm run eval:familiarity` → `node packages/familiarity-evals/dist/ci-gate.js`) never exited. This is that step's first-ever real execution — the Docker `test` stage runs the pnpm gate chain but not the workflow's own extra steps, so this SEAM sat latent since S9. `packages/familiarity-evals/src/real-checks.ts`'s `createRealRunSuccessFn` builds a REAL `@aart/engine` + real `@aart/blocks-core` catalog; 2 of the suite's 8 tasks (`verify-page-renders`, `fill-web-form-and-screenshot`) author `browser.*` steps, which lazily launch a real, shared headless Chromium (`browser-session.ts`). This is SEAMS.md's own **S3-E1**, "RESOLVED" for `@aart/engine`/`@aart/server`'s real composition root (`packages/mcp/src/real-context.ts:259`'s `onRunTerminal: (runId) => closeBrowserSession(runId)`, `packages/server/src/config.ts`'s `onShutdown: () => closeAllBrowserSessions()`) — but `real-checks.ts` is a THIRD, separate composition root (its own header comment already says it mirrors `real-context.ts`'s pattern) that built `createEngine(...)` without the same hook, and `ci-gate.ts`'s `main()` never called `closeAllBrowserSessions()` either. Reproduced directly: backgrounded `CI=true node dist/ci-gate.js < /dev/null`, confirmed via `ps`/`lsof` that the process was still alive 30s after printing its own "all 8 tasks passed" line — exactly one leaked `chrome-headless-shell` child (`--remote-debugging-pipe`) plus the two open CDP pipe fd pairs in the node process, nothing else. Those open pipes are real libuv handles (unlike a bare unresolved `await new Promise(() => {})` — see `packages/mcp/src/e2e/redacted-legacy-b-worker.mjs`'s own `hangForever()` comment on that exact distinction), so the event loop never emptied and Node never exited on its own.

**The fix, both tiers of S3-E1's own already-established pattern.** `real-checks.ts`: `createRealRunSuccessFn`'s `createEngine({...})` now passes `onRunTerminal: (runId) => closeBrowserSession(runId)` (imported from `@aart/blocks-core`) — the per-run primary mechanism, identical to `real-context.ts`'s. `ci-gate.ts`: `main()`'s suite-run/print/decide-exit-code body is now wrapped in `try { ... } finally { await closeAllBrowserSessions(); }` (also imported from `@aart/blocks-core`) — the coarser safety net, standing in for `onShutdown` since this is a one-shot script with no SIGTERM lifecycle of its own; runs on the pass path, the "some tasks failed" path, and if the suite itself throws. Matches every `@aart/blocks-core` browser-block test's own established `afterAll(() => closeAllBrowserSessions())` teardown (e.g. `browser/goto.test.ts`).

**Proof.** `CI=true pnpm run eval:familiarity < /dev/null`, twice consecutively: exit 0, ~2s each (was: never exits), zero leftover `node`/`chrome-headless-shell` processes after either run. Collateral: `@aart/familiarity-evals` + `@aart/dashboard`'s own vitest projects (12 files / 128 tests), full suite (**225 files / 2179 tests**, matching A52's own baseline byte-for-byte), `typecheck`, `typecheck:tests`, `check:tsconfig-refs`, `lint:redaction` (201 suppressions, 0 new/kept/stale) — all green.

**`ci.yml` hardening.** Confirmed the familiarity-evals gate is the job's own LAST step (14 steps total, parsed not eyeballed) — no step sits downstream of it that could share today's "added by an earlier session, never actually executed on real Actions" blind spot. Added `timeout-minutes: 20` at the job level (GitHub's own unset default is 360 — 6 hours) so a future regression of this exact shape fails fast and visibly instead of silently burning hours.

**Commit:** one, local only. No push (orchestrator verifies then pushes).

## 2026-07-11 — F5 The authoring-machine story: a genuine fresh-clone verification, `aart init-agent`'s npx-registry trap fixed, `AUTHORING.md` + root `README.md`

### A54 — Founder-directed: verified the fresh-clone install path for real (nothing in this repo had ever been cloned via `https` into a directory with zero access to any other checkout before this session), found and fixed a real defect in `aart init-agent`'s generated MCP config, and wrote the authoring-machine + root-README docs this exposed as missing

Founder-directed: configuring a second Mac to author workflows with a coding agent and ship them to the already-running server ("Can you help to configure my another test pc to build some of workflow and deploy to the server?"). Local only — commits, no push (the orchestrator pushes after independently verifying this).

**What "verified for real" means here, precisely.** Every prior from-clone install claim in this repo (A33/A35/A42/A44/A46, `DEPLOY.md`'s own Path B) was proven from a directory with "zero access to this repo's own `node_modules`" — but always ultimately sourced from files already sitting on this same checked-out working tree (`pnpm pack`'s tarball, or a copy of it). This session's own first step was different in kind: `git clone https://github.com/team-monet/aart.git` — the real public URL, `https`, no SSH key required — into a directory with no relationship to this working tree at all, then corepack/pnpm/build/pack/install and the full authoring lifecycle from there, exactly as a genuinely new machine would experience it. Two independent repeats of this (a first pass that found the defect below; a second, completely separate clone that re-verified both the bug's presence pre-fix and the fix post-fix in isolation), plus this actual working tree (the patch applied here directly, then re-proven by the automated test suite below rather than a third manual CLI walk) — three independent trees in total, all agreeing.

**The defect this found: `aart init-agent`'s generated `.mcp.json` pointed a coding agent at the WRONG binary.** `generateInitAgentOutputs` (`packages/mcp/src/init-agent.ts`) unconditionally emitted `{"command": "npx", "args": ["-y", "@team-monet/aart", "mcp"]}`. `@team-monet/aart` is a real, already-claimed npm package name — currently at `0.9.0` (published 2026-06-24, verified via `npm view @team-monet/aart`), a `commander`-based CLI from before this repo's current architecture (`-w/--workspace`, `aart block`/`pack`/`schedule`/`dashboard`/`doctor` — none of which exist in this repo's own command surface). Proven empirically, not assumed: with a freshly-built `aart` from this exact repo already correctly installed and resolving on `PATH`, `npx -y @team-monet/aart --help` still fetched and ran the OLD registry package instead of it — `npx <package-name>` (as opposed to a bare command already on `PATH`) resolves against the registry first for a scoped spec like this, regardless of what else is installed locally. A coding agent spawning its MCP server from a freshly-`init-agent`'d `.mcp.json` would silently get the wrong, incompatible `aart mcp` — no error, just a stdio server that doesn't understand this repo's tools.

**The fix.** `generateInitAgentOutputs` gained a `binPath?: string` option (`packages/mcp/src/init-agent.ts`): given, the config becomes `{"command": "node", "args": [binPath, "mcp"]}` — pointing straight at the file that's actually running, never touching the registry. Omitted, the function's own zero-argument behavior is unchanged (existing unit tests' contract preserved deliberately — this is a pure function; it doesn't know how it was invoked, and shouldn't guess). The real fix lives one layer up, where that's actually knowable: `packages/cli/src/commands/authoring.ts`'s `initAgentCommand` now always resolves `binPath` from `process.argv[1]` (empirically confirmed stable via a standalone shebang/symlink probe: Node preserves the AS-INVOKED path — e.g. a global install's `node_modules/.bin/aart` symlink — not a dereferenced target, so it survives an in-place `npm install -g` upgrade) — the one place in this codebase that legitimately knows which binary is running `init-agent`. `--npx` opts back into the original registry-resolved form (correct once `1.0.0` is genuinely published); `--bin-path <path>` overrides explicitly (tests; also usable by hand). Verified three ways, not just typechecked: (1) unit tests on `generateInitAgentOutputs`'s new `binPath` branch directly; (2) CLI-level tests asserting a real `aart init-agent` invocation's default output, `--bin-path`, and `--npx`; (3) a live MCP protocol handshake (`initialize` + `tools/list`) spoken over stdio to exactly the `command`/`args` a fixed `.mcp.json` names, against the first fresh-clone build — got back `serverInfo: {"name":"aart","version":"0.1.0"}` and the real 16-tool catalog (`aart_find_blocks` first, matching `TEST-DRIVE.md` part (d)'s own documented count), with a clean `"MCP server stopped."` on stdin close. A second, fully independent fresh clone re-confirmed the generated `.mcp.json`'s CONTENT (`command`/`args` matching that install's own `which aart`) both pre-fix (still the old `npx` form — the bug reproduces on a genuinely untouched clone too, not an artifact of the first tree) and post-fix, without repeating the full protocol handshake a second time.

**Two more doc gaps this same fresh-clone pass surfaced and fixed.** `packages/cli/README.md`'s Command reference had drifted from the CLI's real usage block — missing `aart request-approval` entirely (a core command in the governed lifecycle: this session's own authoring-loop walk used it directly), the `--registered` `validate` variant, and every `worker`/`server`/`mcp`/`eval run` flag added since; corrected to match `cli.ts`'s own `USAGE` string verbatim, plus a prominent install-section warning (see `AUTHORING.md` part (b)) since this README ships as the one thing `npm install -g` and any GitHub browsing lands on. `TEST-DRIVE.md`'s "What doesn't work yet" list still claimed "No `LICENSE` file" — stale since `b988a04` adopted Apache-2.0; removed (resolved, not a residual gap to keep restating).

**A genuine product rough edge found and documented, deliberately NOT code-fixed.** `aart server --bundle <dir> --environment production ...` (exactly the sequence this session's own brief specified, mirroring `aart bundle --environment production`'s own earlier step) fails on a never-before-hydrated store: `environment "production" not found`. Root cause, read from `packages/server/src/bundle/load.ts`'s own doc comment: hydration always creates one synthetic `Environment` named literally `"bundle"` (`BUNDLE_ENVIRONMENT`, id `env_bundle`) — a bundle's manifest never records the name it was produced under, by deliberate design (`--environment` at PRODUCE time only selects which deployment's `triggerConfig` gets embedded into `triggers.json`, not a name to restore later). This is an already-reasoned architectural tradeoff from an earlier session (S12 "deploy story"), not an oversight — re-litigating whether bundles should carry an environment name is a real product question, not this entry's call to make unilaterally. Documented instead, prominently, in `AUTHORING.md` part (e) and its part (g) limits list: omit `--environment` on a bundle-hydrated store, or pass `--environment bundle` explicitly. Verified directly (both the failure and the fix, against the SAME store): a fresh sqlite store, `aart server --bundle ... --environment production` refused before binding a port (though hydration itself had already committed by then — confirmed by inspecting the sqlite store directly); the SAME now-hydrated store, `aart server --bundle ... --environment bundle`, started clean, and a correctly-HMAC-signed webhook against it ran a real workflow to `"completed"` (an incorrectly-signed one got `401 {"error":"bad_hmac"}`, durably recorded in `GET /rejected-triggers`) — the exact artifact/handoff a real authoring-machine-to-server deploy would exercise, including graceful `SIGTERM` shutdown of both `server` and `worker` afterward.

**`AUTHORING.md` (new, repo root) + `README.md` (new, repo root — this repo had none before this entry).** `AUTHORING.md`: prerequisites (git/Node-via-nvm-or-brew/corepack — no doc anywhere mentioned corepack before this one, verified by grep across every `*.md` in this repo), the npm trap above made prominent with a reproduced verbatim proof, wiring the coding agent (`init-agent`, the fix above, the 16-tool checkpoint), the authoring lifecycle (register → validate --registered → run → request-approval → approve → promote → deploy → trigger add → bundle, one line per gate advanced), deploying to the server (the bundle/environment gotcha above, the `AART_SECRET_<NAME>` handshake stated plainly as the most likely real confusion — the authoring machine only ever records a secret's NAME, never its value), updating the install (including an nvm-specific gotcha: npm's global prefix is per-Node-version, so switching versions silently orphans a previously-`npm install -g`'d `aart` and any `.mcp.json` pointing at it), and an honest limits section matching this repo's own established "not glossed over" convention (`TEST-DRIVE.md`/`DEPLOY.md`). Every command block in parts (b)/(c)/(d)/(e) was executed verbatim (not just written from memory) against a genuinely independent fresh clone as part of this entry's own verification, including a byte-for-byte extraction-and-run of part (b)'s own code block straight out of the committed file. `README.md`: minimal per this session's own brief (one-liner, `1.0.0`-pending/install-from-source note, links to `AUTHORING.md`/`DEPLOY.md`/`TEST-DRIVE.md`/`LICENSE`) — the R1 release pass owns polishing it further. `DEPLOY.md` and `TEST-DRIVE.md` both gained a cross-reference to `AUTHORING.md`.

**Gates, from clean.** `pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`: **225 files / 2187 tests, all green** (A53's own 225/2179 baseline + this session's 8 new tests — 4 on `generateInitAgentOutputs`'s `binPath` branch, 4 on `aart init-agent`'s real CLI-level default/`--bin-path`/`--npx` behavior). `lint:redaction`: clean, 201 reviewed suppressions, 0 new/kept/stale. `check:tsconfig-refs`: OK, same 5 non-blocking informational notes as every prior session. Re-run for stability, not just once: the full suite ran green in an independent fresh-clone tree first (225/2187) and again in this working tree (225/2187); the two touched test files specifically ran green two further times back to back (65/65 both times).

**What would differ on the real target machine, stated plainly.** This session's verification used `npm install --prefix <tmp> <tarball>` (an isolated prefix) instead of a literal `npm install -g`, specifically to avoid mutating THIS machine's real global npm state — deliberately never run with a literal `-g` flag this session. Both are a plain `npm install` of a local tarball differing only in the prefix/bin-linking destination (standard, well-documented npm behavior, not independently re-verified against a real global prefix this session). `AUTHORING.md` documents the real `-g` form throughout, since that's what the actual second Mac should run. Everything else — the clone, corepack/pnpm, the build/pack sequence, `init-agent`'s fix, the full authoring lifecycle, the bundle/server handshake including the live signed webhook — ran against the real mechanism, not a stand-in.

**Commit:** one, local only. No push (orchestrator verifies then pushes).
