## 2026-07-11 — with-aart

### A55 — `with-aart/`: an agent-first onboarding layer for AART, modeled on the founder's own `with-monet` pattern — adapted, not transplanted, to AART's per-workspace-store model; a real `.mcp.json`-clobbering defect in `aart init-agent` found and fixed along the way

Founder-directed: "for Wire your coding agent can we make similar to how we do for monet?" — build AART's own agent-adoption layer, studying `/Users/johnlee/code/with-monet` (the founder's separate monet-adoption harness repo) as the fidelity reference. Local only — commits, no push (the orchestrator pushes after independently verifying this).

**What `with-monet`'s pattern actually is, extracted from the reference repo (`agents/*.md`, `bootstrap/install.md`, `mcp/monet.json`, `roster.json`, `package.json`, `README.md`).** An agent-first, self-installing harness: the user pastes one line into their coding agent, and the agent — not a script — reads `bootstrap/install.md` and executes it as a playbook: **orient** (identify host, confirm MCP support AND a real isolated-subagent primitive — both gate the install, either missing stops it), **get the substrate** (`npm i -g @team-monet/monet`, already published), **configure the MCP server at USER scope** (one server, `~/.monet`, organized per-project by "circles" — genuinely global, not per-repo), **install the team** (`stig`, the lead, written into the host's always-on global persona file wrapped in `<!-- BEGIN with-monet:stig --> / <!-- END with-monet:stig -->` markers, ask-first because it's the install's highest-impact write; nine workers, each written as its own file carrying a `<!-- with-monet:agent -->` marker, with `disallowedTools: mcp__monet` denying them Monet access on hosts that support a denylist), **offer memory ingest**, **offer to start**, then **verify** (a live `agent_context`/`memory_search` call proves the wiring; each worker actually launches). Every write is non-destructive by discipline written into the prose, not the tooling: back up before overwriting, merge into existing config rather than replacing it, never clobber the user's own content — because `monet` itself has no mechanical config-writer of its own; the *installing agent* does the read-merge-write by hand, every time, per `install.md`'s own instructions.

**with-aart, adapted rather than transplanted — the structural differences are load-bearing, not cosmetic.** AART's domain model is fundamentally different from monet's, and the harness reflects that rather than copying `with-monet`'s file shapes blindly:

- **No team to install.** monet's product is "a lead (`stig`) that delegates to a worker team" — `with-monet` ships `agents/*.md` + `roster.json` mapping each worker into host-specific subagent frontmatter. AART's product is "one coding agent, calling `aart_*` MCP tools directly" — there is no team, so `with-aart/` has no `agents/` directory and no `roster.json`. The single piece of standing content monet's `agents/stig.md` and AART's working-instructions text both play the same structural role (an always-on behavioral overlay), so that's the one thing `with-aart` installs globally — everything else in monet's file layout (`glama.json`, per-worker frontmatter mapping) has no AART analog and was deliberately not manufactured.
- **MCP wiring is per-workspace, not user-scope — a structural fact, not a preference.** monet's store is one global `~/.monet`, organized per-project by circle, so its MCP server registers once at user scope and covers every project. AART's store (`.aart/`, like a project's own `.git/`) is workspace-scoped by design — there is no "install once for every project" step for the MCP server itself. `with-aart/bootstrap/install.md`'s Phase 3 states this explicitly and repeats per workspace (`aart init-agent`, unchanged mechanism); only Phase 4 (the working-instructions text) installs globally, mirroring monet's Tier-A gate treatment (ask first, back up, idempotent markers) for that one write.
- **One source of truth by construction, not by convention.** monet has no mechanical writer for its Stig persona — `agents/stig.md` IS the one and only copy, hand-authored, installed verbatim. AART already had a mechanical writer (`aart init-agent`, generating `AGENTS.md` from `generateInitAgentOutputs`, `packages/mcp/src/init-agent.ts`) before this session. The risk `with-aart` had to design against was different: hand-writing a *second*, independently-maintained copy of that same "verify reflex / authoring loop" prose inside `with-aart/agents/aart.md` — exactly the fork the brief warned against. Resolved by never writing one: `install.md`'s Phase 4 instructs the installing agent to *generate* the canonical text by actually running `aart init-agent --cwd <scratch dir>` and reading the `AGENTS.md` it writes, then discarding the scratch dir — never fetching or hand-copying static prose. `init-agent.ts` gained a documentation-only header addition (no behavior change) naming both call sites explicitly, so a future edit to the instructions template can't accidentally leave one consumer stale. No `--host` flag was added to `init-agent` — evaluated and rejected: the existing `--instructions-out`/`--mcp-config-out` redirection flags already let an agent generate just the instructions text via a scratch directory without touching a real workspace, and all host-specific knowledge (which global file, how to wrap it) is genuinely `install.md`'s prose to own, not `init-agent`'s code — adding a flag would have duplicated that reasoning into the mechanical writer for no behavioral gain.
- **No "isolated subagents" gate.** monet's Phase 1 stops the install entirely if the host lacks a true named-subagent primitive (Stig has nothing to orchestrate without one). AART needs only MCP support — one agent, calling tools directly, is the whole model — so `with-aart`'s Phase 1 has no equivalent hard stop; a non-MCP host is told the CLI still works standalone instead.

**The real defect this session's own "verify like the consumer" instruction surfaced: `aart init-agent` clobbered `.mcp.json`.** Before this session, `initAgentCommand` (`packages/cli/src/commands/authoring.ts`) wrote `outputs.mcpConfigJson` straight over whatever `.mcp.json` already contained — no read, no merge. `.mcp.json` is a shared, multi-tenant registry by Claude-Code convention (this founder's own real setup is the concrete case: a workspace running both `with-monet` and, now, `with-aart` would have a `monet` entry already present when `aart init-agent` runs). Re-running `init-agent` — the documented refresh mechanism, `AUTHORING.md` part (f) — would have silently deleted that sibling entry every time, exactly the "clobber" failure mode `with-monet`'s own `install.md` explicitly guards against by hand for every host config it touches. Fixed at the one place that performs the file I/O (`initAgentCommand`, not the pure `generateInitAgentOutputs`): a new `mergeMcpConfig` reads any existing `.mcp.json`, replaces only the `aart` key, and writes the rest back untouched; a file that exists but fails to parse as JSON is left on disk and reported as an error rather than silently replaced (the same "fail loudly" tradeoff `aart server`'s missing-store-root check and A54's own `binPath` fix already established in this codebase). No existing file behaves exactly as before — byte-identical output, confirmed by construction (the no-file branch returns a fresh spread of the same `outputs.mcpConfig` `generateInitAgentOutputs` already produced).

**Verification — real, live, not just unit tests.** Beyond the four new `cli.test.ts` cases (sibling-preservation, idempotent in-place update, other-top-level-key preservation, invalid-JSON fails loudly without touching the file), this session ran `with-aart/bootstrap/install.md`'s own phases end to end against the freshly-built `packages/cli/dist/bin.js`, entirely inside scratch paths — **the real `~/.claude` on this machine was never touched**, only a synthetic stand-in built from `with-monet/agents/stig.md`'s own public content:
- **Phase 3, live:** seeded a scratch workspace's `.mcp.json` with a `monet` entry, ran the real `aart init-agent --cwd <scratch>` — resulting file had *both* `monet` (byte-identical) and a correctly self-consistent `aart` entry. Re-ran with a different `--bin-path` (simulating an upgrade) — `aart`'s entry updated in place, `monet`'s was untouched, no duplication.
- **Phase 4, live:** generated the canonical instructions text via `aart init-agent` against a throwaway scratch dir (never hand-written), appended it — wrapped in `<!-- BEGIN with-aart:agent --> / <!-- END with-aart:agent -->` markers, backed up first — to a synthetic `CLAUDE.md` already containing a `<!-- BEGIN with-monet:stig --> / <!-- END with-monet:stig -->` block; diffed the with-monet region before/after and confirmed byte-identical (never touched). Regenerated the instructions under `AART_TRUST_MODE=dev` (genuinely different text — a different `## Approval` section) and ran the documented replace-in-place logic a second time: exactly one `with-aart:agent` block afterward (no duplicate), its content updated to the new text, the with-monet block still byte-identical to its backup.
- **Phase 5, live — a real MCP protocol handshake, not a stub:** using the real `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` `Client`/`StdioClientTransport` (matching A54's own precedent, not a hand-rolled JSON-RPC framer), spoke a genuine `initialize` + `tools/list` + `tools/call` against exactly the `command`/`args` the scratch workspace's own generated `.mcp.json` names (`node <dist/bin.js> mcp`). Got back `serverInfo: {"name":"aart","version":"0.1.0"}`, a 16-tool catalog whose sorted names matched `AUTHORING.md` part (c)'s own documented list **exactly**, and a real `aart_find_blocks({query:"http"})` call returning five genuine, correctly-scored blocks (`http.download`, `http.health_check`, `http.request`, `browser.goto`, `web.read`) with the documented `next`-field guidance. `client.close()` returned cleanly; `pgrep -fl "dist/bin.js mcp"` afterward found no leaked process (this repo's own A53 history — a leaked headless Chromium hanging CI — made this worth checking explicitly rather than assuming).

**Docs.** `AUTHORING.md` part (c) rewritten to lead with the one-line `with-aart` agent-first install, with `aart init-agent`'s own mechanism (unchanged) kept as the documented "what happens under the hood / by hand" path directly beneath it, plus the new merge-safety behavior noted inline. Root `README.md` gained one "Where to go next" bullet pointing at `with-aart/`. `with-aart/README.md`, `with-aart/package.json` (`private: true` — not separately published; colocated in this monorepo like everything else pre-`1.0.0`), `with-aart/bootstrap/cli.mjs` (prints the one-line paste-in, mirroring `with-monet/bootstrap/cli.mjs`), and `with-aart/mcp/aart.json` (a reference template, explicitly marked "don't hand-write this") round out the tree.

**Gates, from clean.** `pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`: **225 files / 2191 tests, all green** (A54's own 225/2187 baseline + this session's 4 new `.mcp.json` merge-safety tests). One clean-gate build hit a real `tsc` error first (`McpConfig`, a named interface with no index signature, isn't structurally assignable to `Record<string, unknown>` even though a fresh object-literal spread is) — fixed by returning `{ ...generated }` instead of `generated`, then the full clean-gate sequence ran green. `lint:redaction`: clean, 201 reviewed suppressions, 0 new/kept/stale. `check:tsconfig-refs`: OK, same 5 non-blocking informational notes as every prior session. Re-run for stability, not just once: the full suite ran green twice back to back (2191/2191 both times); the two touched test files specifically ran green two further times back to back (`cli.test.ts` 57/57, `init-agent.test.ts` 12/12, both twice).

**What would differ on a real second machine, stated plainly.** (1) Phase 4's global-instructions write in this session targeted a synthetic scratch `CLAUDE.md`, never the real `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` on this machine — a real install would write there, still through the same back-up-then-append-or-replace-in-place logic just proven, but that exact file was deliberately never touched this session, per this session's own brief. (2) Phase 1's host-orientation and the "Host notes" section's Codex/opencode guidance are adapted by documented analogy from `with-monet`'s own equivalent notes, not independently re-verified against those hosts — `install.md` says this plainly rather than implying broader verification than occurred. (3) Phase 2 (getting `aart` itself) ran against this session's own from-clean build, not a genuine second `git clone` — A54's own fresh-clone proof already covers that path end to end and wasn't worth re-running a third time solely for this session's own scope. (4) The Phase 6 "star the repo" `gh` flow was not exercised (no repo-star action was actually requested or taken this session).

### A56 — D1 "remotes + push": git-style named remotes + one-command HTTP deploy (`aart push`/`aart_deploy`), a token-gated ingest surface on `aart server`, and ADR-2's `aart environment register` — closing the "no legal way to create a production-trust Environment, or to ship a bundle without `scp`" gaps

Founder-ratified design (John: "alright looks good," 2026-07-12; Stig ratified the analyst design memo plus two ADR rulings before dispatch). Builds a full deploy-over-HTTP surface on top of S12's existing bundle system (`bundle.ts`/`load.ts`) rather than replacing it — a bundle is still the one artifact this whole story moves around; this session adds a second way to MOVE one (POST, not `scp`) and a second dimension to WHERE it lands (a real named `Environment`, not only the synthetic `env_bundle` fallback).

**D-1/D-2 — envelope + ingest semantics.** `POST /bundles/ingest`/`POST /bundles/plan` (`packages/server/src/http/server.ts:381-441`) accept exactly `{ files: Record<relPath, string> }` — the shape `bundleToBundleLike` (now `real-context.ts`'s shared `resolveAndProduceBundle`, see D-8 below) already builds, no base64/tar/multipart, matching bundles being 100% JSON text. `readBundleFromDisk` (`packages/server/src/bundle/load.ts`) was refactored into a shared `readBundleFromSource` core fed a `BundleSource` abstraction (`diskSource` | the new `envelopeSource`) — `readBundleFromEnvelope` is the new in-memory counterpart, mirroring every disk-mode failure shape (missing entry, invalid JSON, schema failure, hash mismatch) exactly, one implementation not two. `Deployment` gains `promoted?: boolean` (`packages/types/src/store-records.ts`) — undefined/true = active (byte-identical to every pre-A56 row), false = "evidence recorded via ingest, awaiting promotion." `hydrateBundle` (`load.ts:377-460`) now resolves `manifest.targetEnvironment` (D-6, below) against a REAL, already-registered `Environment` (`resolveHydrationTarget`, exported) — throws with a remedy naming both `aart environment register` and `POST /environments` if it doesn't exist, never auto-vivifying — and keys the resulting `Deployment` `bundle:${wf}@${ver}:${environmentId}` (env-scoped, resolved id never name), stamping `promoted: resolvedTrustMode === "dev"`. A manifest with NO `targetEnvironment` (every pre-A56 bundle, or one produced without `--environment`) takes the OLD path completely unchanged: global key `bundle:${wf}@${ver}`, synthetic `env_bundle` auto-vivified, `promoted` left unset — verified by the full pre-existing `load.test.ts` suite passing with zero modifications to its own fixtures. `deploymentToBinding` (`packages/server/src/triggers/registry.ts:41-56`) gained the single chokepoint both real trigger-ingress paths (webhook/github/slack HTTP, the poll ticker) already funnel through: `if (deployment.promoted === false) return undefined;`. `promoteWorkflowVersionToEnvironment` (`packages/server/src/promotion.ts:109-152`) now stamps `promoted: true` explicitly on both its fresh-create and merge-existing branches — a LOCAL promotion is definitionally live, distinct from `PromotionRecord.promoted` (governance's own per-call ELIGIBILITY computation, never persisted, a same-named-but-unrelated concept the code comments are deliberately explicit about).

**D-3 — auth.** `checkDeployToken` (`packages/server/src/deploy-token.ts:30`) — `sha256` of BOTH operands then `crypto.timingSafeEqual` (never a raw compare; hashing first means the comparison can never throw on a length mismatch, so there's no length-based timing branch to leak either). `resolveDeployToken` (`packages/cli/src/secrets.ts`) is a DELIBERATELY separate function from `createRealSecretResolver` — the plain `AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN` env var, then `<root>/secrets.json`'s own key of that name; reusing the ref-stripping webhook resolver would have double-prefixed to `AART_SECRET_AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN`. `ServerConfig.deployToken` gates `POST /bundles/ingest`/`POST /bundles/plan`/`POST /environments` — unconditionally fail-closed when unset (no "auth disabled" state for this one surface); the three `/webhooks/*` routes keep their pre-existing, separate per-binding HMAC verification completely untouched.

**D-4/D-5 — remotes + plan.** `<root>/remotes.json` (`packages/cli/src/remote-config.ts`, sibling to `secrets.json`) — `{ "<name>": { url, environment, tokenRef? } }`; `aart remote add/list/remove` (`packages/cli/src/commands/remote.ts`) is pure CLI CRUD with no MCP-tool counterpart, same class as `aart trigger add`. `POST /bundles/plan` (`packages/server/src/bundle/plan.ts`'s `planBundleIngest`) reuses the ingest envelope-parse/hash-verify path, stops before `store.transact()` — zero writes, verified by a dedicated test asserting the store is byte-identical before/after.

**D-6/D-7 — manifest field + the sqlite migration precedent.** `BundleManifestSchema.targetEnvironment?: string` (`packages/server/src/bundle/bundle.ts:21-37`), schemaVersion stays `z.literal(1)` — purely additive both directions. `packages/store/src/adapters/sqlite/migrations.ts`'s `createSqliteAddDeploymentPromotedMigration` is this adapter's FIRST migration past `0001_init` — `ALTER TABLE deployments ADD COLUMN promoted INTEGER`, deliberately NOT folded into the baseline `SQLITE_SCHEMA_STATEMENTS` DDL, so a pre-existing row's column reads back SQL `NULL` -> `fromBoolOrNull` -> TS `undefined`, never `false` — proven against a REAL pre-populated-db fixture (`packages/store/src/adapters/sqlite/migrations.test.ts`), not just asserted.

**D-8 — the CLI/MCP plumbing, and where the real cross-package tension in this whole memo turned out to live.** `AartContext` gained two ports NOT CLI-only like `ServerPort` (`packages/mcp/src/types.ts`) — `ctx.bundler`/`ctx.remotes` — because MCP stdio never receives a `ServerPort` at all (`real-context.ts`'s own established note), so `aart_deploy` had no other route to real bundle production/remote resolution. Real implementations live in `@aart/mcp` (`real-context.ts`'s `resolveAndProduceBundle` — the resolveDeployment/bundleToBundleLike bridge EXTRACTED from `@aart/cli`'s `real-server-port.ts`, which now imports and calls this exact function instead of keeping a local copy; `stubs/deploy.ts`'s `createRemotesPort`, exported under both `createStubRemotesPort` and `createRealRemotesPort` since a plain JSON-file read has no expensive "real thing" to fake, unlike every other port in that file). This required adding `@aart/server` as a genuinely NEW dependency of `@aart/mcp` (package.json + tsconfig.json references + a `pnpm install` to create the workspace symlink — the build failed with `Cannot find module '@aart/server'` until that ran, a real gotcha worth flagging for whoever next adds an inter-package dependency in this monorepo). `deployToRemoteHandler` (`packages/mcp/src/handlers/deployment.ts`) is the ONE shared function both `aart push` (`packages/cli/src/commands/deployment.ts`'s `pushCommand`) and the MCP `aart_deploy` tool (`HANDLERS.aart_deploy`) call directly — same-function-reference, not just equivalent behavior, matching this codebase's established three-clients discipline.

**Deviations from the ratified memo — flagged explicitly, not silently resolved:**

1. **`packages/cli/src/deploy-client.ts`'s relationship to `deployToRemoteHandler` is NOT what a literal reading of D-8 might suggest.** The memo names `deploy-client.ts` as "a thin fetch-based POST helper" in the same breath as `deployToRemoteHandler`/`ctx.bundler`/`ctx.remotes`, which could read as "the shared handler calls into this file." It cannot: `@aart/mcp` cannot depend on `@aart/cli` (the dependency runs the other way), and `deployToRemoteHandler` is registered directly in `HANDLERS` (a fixed 2-arg `ToolHandler` signature, no room for an injected transport parameter without breaking that direct registration — verified by checking `handlers/index.ts`'s own type). Resolved: `deployToRemoteHandler` performs its own outbound POST via Node 22's global `fetch`, inline, in `packages/mcp/src/handlers/deployment.ts` — genuinely the ONE implementation both CLI and MCP route through. `deploy-client.ts` was still built exactly as named (a standalone, directly-tested `postBundleEnvelope` mirroring the identical shape) — a small, deliberate, independently-defined duplicate for CLI-side isolated testing, the same unavoidable-duplication class D-8's own `stubs/deploy.ts` already established for remotes.json/secrets.json reading (documented as such in both files' own header comments). If a tighter coupling was actually intended, this is the seam to revisit.
2. **D-3's "Set -> gates EVERY mutation POST route" was read NARROWLY — the three new deploy-surface routes only, not the ~20 pre-existing mutation routes (`/runs/trigger`, `/workflows/:id/approve`, `/corrections`, `/evals/suites`, ...) `http/server.ts` already exposed.** The memo's own D-3 bullet only ever names `/bundles/ingest`/`/bundles/plan` for the unset-behavior half of that sentence, and ADR-2 explicitly scopes `POST /environments` as the third gated route — nothing in the memo's TESTS list, DOCS CLOSURE list, or file inventory mentions threading this token through the dashboard's existing HTTP client or any pre-existing CLI command, which a broad reading would require (every one of those routes currently has zero auth of any kind, called by the dashboard/CLI/MCP with no token at all). Read literally-broad, this would have been a breaking change to every existing write action with no supporting plumbing anywhere else in the brief. `DEPLOY.md`'s "Ops limits" section now states this narrow scope explicitly ("the ONLY part of this API a bearer token protects") specifically so this interpretation is visible and correctable, not silently assumed.
3. **`plan.ts`'s `capabilityDiff` is a step-level `uses:` diff, not a resolved capability-TIER diff.** `@aart/server` has no live block registry (that lives in `@aart/blocks-core`/`@aart/llm`, assembled only in `@aart/mcp`'s `real-context.ts`) to resolve a `uses` id to its declared capabilities — multiple different `uses` values can share one capability (root AMENDMENTS.md A24's own finding: every `browser.*` block declares the same `browser` capability). The field is documented in `plan.ts` itself as a structural approximation, not a formal risk-tier verdict — still genuinely useful ("this version now uses `command.run`, which the active one didn't"), just not what "capability diff" might imply from the name alone.
4. **`plan.ts`'s `currentVersion`** (the version treated as "currently active" in a target environment, for the `capabilityDiff` comparison) **is a documented heuristic — the most-recently-CREATED `Deployment` row with `promoted !== false` — not a hard system invariant.** This system permits multiple `Deployment` rows per (workflowId, environmentId) (one per version ever promoted or hydrated there — verified directly against both `promoteWorkflowVersionToEnvironment`'s own existing-row lookup and this session's own env-scoped hydration keying), so "the current version" has no single authoritative answer already defined elsewhere in this codebase; this is `plan.ts`'s own reasonable fill for a preview field, not a memo-specified algorithm.
5. **`aart_deploy` (MCP tool name) vs `aart push` (CLI verb) — a deliberate asymmetry, ratified by ADR-1, not an inconsistency silently fixed here.** Flagged for visibility since it's a genuine oddity a future reader might "helpfully" rename one way or the other.

**Docs.** `AUTHORING.md` part (e) gained the `aart push` one-command path plus a rewrite of its own previously-documented "a bundle doesn't carry an environment name" gotcha (now fixed, not just worked around) and a note on the new env-scoped `deploymentId` shape; `DEPLOY.md` gained "Deploy token" and "Environment registration" sections (linked from its TOC) plus a correction to its "No authentication" Ops-limits bullet (now precise about the one exception); `TEST-DRIVE.md` part (f) gained an `aart push` callout, a produce-time-vs-runtime `--environment` disambiguation (two different mechanisms sharing a flag name — the kind of thing worth spelling out once, explicitly, rather than leaving to inference), and an `aart_deploy` bullet in "what to try next"; `docker-compose.yml`'s stale environment-scoping comment now names `aart environment register` alongside the pre-existing auto-vivification path, plus an `AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN` mention on the `server` service's `env_file` line; `.env.example` gained a documented `AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN` entry; root `README.md` gained a one-clause pointer. The dashboard's `ProductionPage.tsx` promoted-badge is explicitly OUT of scope (flagged as a residual in `DEPLOY.md`'s own Ops limits, not silently dropped) — no frontend code was touched this session.

**Tests.** Every item the design memo's own TESTS section named, implemented: manifest round-trip of `targetEnvironment` (present/absent/independent-of-`deployment`/hash-sensitive); `readBundleFromEnvelope` mirroring every `readBundleFromDisk` failure mode with matching error shapes; real-environment-vs-legacy-`env_bundle` fallback (including the two never colliding for the same workflow@version); all three idempotency scenarios (same tuple+hash no-op, same tuple+different-hash refuse, same workflow@version to different environments independent); `promoteWorkflowVersionToEnvironment` stamping `promoted:true` on both branches; `deploymentToBinding` skipping `promoted===false` while `undefined`/`true` fall through unaffected (regression-proven against every pre-existing fixture); the sqlite `0002_deployment_promoted` migration against a genuinely pre-populated db (plus `down()`/`up()` reversibility); the full `checkDeployToken` truth table including the timing-safe different-length path; `aart remote add/list/remove` round-trip; `aart_deploy` registered in all four trust modes with zero data-existence precondition; `POST /environments` register+list; the request-body size cap both over and under `MAX_BUNDLE_INGEST_BYTES` (10MB). `redaction-lint`: every new finding this session's own code introduced (20 new entries — 10 `stubs/deploy.ts`, 6 `handlers/deployment.ts`, 4 `real-context.ts`; 201 -> 223 applied-findings count, the +2 beyond +20 being two of those entries each matching a second, identically-shaped call site) individually reviewed and suppressed with a written, specific justification (A44 discipline) — including one requiring genuine care (`resolveTokenRef`'s resolved-token return value, justified by tracing that it only ever feeds an outbound `Authorization` header, verified never to appear in `deployToRemoteHandler`'s own returned result via a dedicated test) — plus 2 pre-existing suppressions in `context.ts` updated (not silently left stale) for the two new ports added to an already-reviewed DI-bundle return.

**Gates, from clean.** `pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`: **231 files / 2336 tests, all green** (A55's own 225/2191 baseline + this session's 6 new test files — `migrations.test.ts`, `deploy-token.test.ts`, `router.test.ts`, `remote-config.test.ts`, `deploy-client.test.ts`, `stubs/deploy.test.ts` — plus extensive additions to existing files). `check:tsconfig-refs`: OK, same 5 non-blocking informational notes as every prior session (`@aart/mcp`'s new production dependency on `@aart/server` is NOT among them — it's a real production dependency, not a devDependency-only build-time reference, so it never triggers this check's note). `lint:redaction`: clean, 223 reviewed suppressions (201 pre-session baseline + 22 net new this session, each individually reviewed and justified per A44 discipline — see this session's own final report for the full list). Re-run for stability, not just once: the full suite ran green twice back to back, byte-identical counts both times (231/231 files, 2336/2336 tests).

**Commit:** local only. No push (orchestrator verifies then pushes).

## 2026-07-12 — D1 fix pass

### A57 — D1 "remotes + push" fix pass: the deploy-token composition-root gap closed for real (a third occurrence of the A48/A53 bug class), a silent trustMode governance downgrade closed, promote brought inside the trust boundary, and eight smaller findings from a two-perspective review closed

Stig-ratified fix pass, John-approved: John ratified D1 2026-07-12 ("alright looks good"); this session implements Stig-ratified rulings on findings from a two-perspective review (briefed reviewer + cold auditor) plus a real-binary e2e test of branch `d1/remotes-and-push` (HEAD `37b9e3c`, A56's own final state). All three verifiers confirmed A56's underlying design sound — these are ten targeted fixes, not a redesign; nothing in A56's own D-1 through D-8 architecture was touched beyond what each fix needed. Ten commits, one per fix, local only — no push.

**FIX 1 — BLOCKER, closed.** `resolveDeployToken(root)` (`packages/cli/src/secrets.ts:104`, built in A56) had ZERO production callers: `real-server-port.ts`'s `startServer` built a real `secretResolver` (A45) but never resolved/threaded `deployToken`, so `ServerConfig.deployToken` was always `undefined` through a real `aart server` — `POST /bundles/ingest`, `POST /bundles/plan`, `POST /environments` refused EVERY request unconditionally, including a genuinely correct one, since A56 itself landed. Fixed at `packages/cli/src/real-server-port.ts:97`: `const deployToken = await resolveDeployToken(root);` inside `startServer`, threaded into the `startRealServer({...})` call — no `ServerPort` interface change, resolved internally exactly like `secretResolver` one line above. This is the THIRD occurrence of this exact bug class in this repo (a real, unit-tested mechanism built but never wired into the one production composition root that needed it) — A48 (capability-gating), A53 (a leaked headless Chromium's cleanup hook), now this. Regression test mirrors the existing A45 secretResolver-wiring pattern exactly (`packages/cli/src/real-server-port.test.ts`, new `describe("createRealServerPort — real deployToken wiring (AMENDMENTS.md A57 fix pass)")`): a REAL `aart server` process (via `cli.serverPort.startServer`), a correct Bearer token reaching past the gate to `requireBundleEnvelope`'s own 400 (never the "not configured" 401), a wrong token still 401ing with the differentiated remedy, and the pre-existing fail-closed-when-unset behavior reconfirmed unchanged.

**FIX 2 — MAJOR, closed.** `POST /environments` (`packages/server/src/http/server.ts`) cast the body and passed `trustMode` straight into `registerEnvironment` with no runtime check — an invalid value (a typo'd `"prod"`) persisted verbatim, and every real reader (`requiredGatesForEnvironment`, `normalizeEnvironmentTrustMode`) silently downgrades an unrecognized string to `"governed"`, with zero signal anywhere (not even in this route's own `200` response, which echoes the bad string back verbatim). Fixed with `requireValidTrustMode` (`server.ts:194`, called at `server.ts:525`) — a plain runtime check + 400 naming all four valid values, matching this file's own sibling `requireBundleEnvelope`'s established style (deliberately NOT a new Zod validation layer, per the ruling — this file's other POST handlers don't use one either). The vocabulary itself is sourced from `@aart/types`' `TrustModeSchema.options` (the one canonical definition `TrustMode` is already derived from) rather than a third hand-typed copy of the same four strings — `@aart/governance`'s own `normalizeEnvironmentTrustMode` doc comment already flags exactly this class of duplication as the cause of a past bug (root AMENDMENTS.md A42). **Deviation flagged**: the ruling offered an OR — "reject unknown top-level body fields OR validate trustMode strictness matching the CLI." Took the second option; the CLI sibling (`commands/environment.ts`) doesn't validate `config`/`secretSource` shape or reject extra fields either, so matching its actual strictness meant scoping this fix to `trustMode` alone. Tests (`server.test.ts`, `describe("POST /environments — ADR-2 ...")`): all four valid modes accepted; `"prod"` → 400 naming `dev`/`governed`/`strict`/`production` and echoing the rejected value; omitted `trustMode` still accepted (unchanged); the pre-existing "missing name" test unaffected.

**FIX 3 — MAJOR (trust boundary), closed.** `POST /workflows/:id/promote` was completely open — no token check at all, even with `AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN` configured. Ruling: promote flips `Deployment.promoted` `false`→`true`; D1 both tells operators it's fine to network-expose `aart server` and created that exact dormancy property (push-now-promote-later), so an unauthenticated promote defeats D1's own guarantee. Fixed with `requireDeployTokenIfConfigured` (`server.ts:148`, applied at `server.ts:377`) — a DISTINCT helper from `requireDeployToken`, not a flag on it, so the fail-closed (three A56 routes) vs conditional (promote) semantics stay explicit in the code: `deployToken` unset → stays open (unchanged pre-A56 behavior — a tokenless local/dev/TEST-DRIVE dashboard keeps working, never fail-closed) with ONE loud startup warning (`server.ts:232`, `logger.warn`, not per-request); configured → the same `checkDeployToken`/`extractBearerToken` check the other three routes use, with a promote-specific 401 remedy ("provide a valid token," not "set `AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN`," since one already is). **Closure, all landed in the same commit**: `@aart/dashboard`'s `createHttpApiClient` (`packages/dashboard/src/api-client.ts:203`) gained an optional `deployToken` parameter, attached as `Authorization: Bearer <token>` on `promoteWorkflow` ONLY (not threaded into every `postJson*` call — that's the one route the server conditionally gates); `deploy/serve-dashboard.mjs:116` resolves `AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN` from env (verified: this launcher has no root/secrets.json convention of its own — every other config value on that page, `AART_ROOT`/`AART_STORE`/`AART_SERVER_URL`, is env-var-only too, so this matches rather than invents a new resolution shape, per the ruling's own conditional); `docker-compose.yml`'s `dashboard` service gained `env_file: - .env` (server/worker already had it; dashboard didn't) so the var actually reaches the container. Tests: `server.test.ts`'s new `describe("POST /workflows/:id/promote — conditional deploy-token gating ...")` (configured+correct → 200 with a Deployment created; configured+wrong → 401 with the differentiated remedy, no Deployment; configured+absent header → 401; unconfigured → 200, unchanged); `api-client.test.ts`'s two new tests against a REAL token-gated `@aart/server` instance (not a mock) — `promoteWorkflow` with the token succeeds, without it throws `/401/`, proving the header is genuinely load-bearing.

**FIX 4, closed.** `packages/cli/src/deploy-client.ts` + `deploy-client.test.ts` deleted. That module's own header comment justified its existence with "this package cannot depend on that one [`@aart/mcp`]; the dependency runs the other way" — false: `@aart/cli` already depends on `@aart/mcp` (three-clients principle), so `pushCommand` could always call `deployToRemoteHandler` directly, and does. The "mirror" was dead code from the day it was written — grep-confirmed zero references anywhere outside the two deleted files and its own now-rewritten explanatory comment in `packages/mcp/src/handlers/deployment.ts`. **`packages/cli/src/index.ts` never exported anything from it** (checked before deleting), and **`packages/governance/src/redaction-lint-suppressions.ts` had zero entries for it** (grep-confirmed, both before and after — nothing to clean up, contrary to this fix's own initial "may REMOVE entries" expectation). Final grep proof (post-deletion): `grep -rn "deploy-client|postBundleEnvelope|PostBundleResult" packages --include="*.ts" --include="*.json"` (excluding dist/node_modules) → only the two explanatory comment lines in `deployment.ts` naming the deleted files by way of explaining why; `ls` on both deleted paths → `No such file or directory`; suppressions grep → zero matches.

**FIX 5, closed — plus a genuine verification finding worth recording.** `plan.ts`'s `findCurrentVersion` sorted candidate `Deployment` rows by `createdAt` only; two rows with the exact same millisecond-resolution timestamp made the "winner" depend on whatever order `store.deployments.list` happened to return them in — an ordering `AartStore`'s own interface never promises. Fixed with a secondary sort key on `id` (`plan.ts:103`, now exported) — `id` is a per-row random UUID (`ids.ts`'s `generateId`), guaranteed unique, so it fully resolves any tie deterministically in one step; there is no claim this recovers TRUE recency (once `createdAt` itself has collided, that's already unknowable from stored data alone), only that the SAME answer comes back every time. **Verification finding, not assumed**: the first regression test written (HTTP-level, through a real `createFsStore`-backed `POST /bundles/plan`) turned out NOT to distinguish pre-fix from post-fix behavior at all — empirically confirmed by stashing the fix and re-running, twice, both times passing regardless. Root cause: `createFsStore`'s own `KeyedJsonCollection.list()` (`adapters/fs/json-file.ts:151`) already sorts its returned rows alphabetically by id, which coincidentally matches this fix's own tie-break output independent of whether the fix exists — a real store backend's OWN incidental behavior was silently making the bug unreproducible through that one adapter. Replaced with a dedicated, backend-independent unit suite (`packages/server/src/bundle/plan.test.ts`, new file) calling the now-exported `findCurrentVersion` directly against a minimal store double whose `deployments.list()` returns rows in a deliberately adversarial order — verified this suite genuinely fails against the pre-fix comparator (not merely the missing export: re-tested with the export kept and only the comparator reverted, got clean value-level `AssertionError`s, "expected '1' to be '2'", on exactly the two tests exercising the tie-break) before finalizing it. The original HTTP-level test was kept as a narrower, honestly-recaptioned integration check (a currentVersion resolves at all, and is stable across repeated requests) rather than deleted or left over-claiming what it proves.

**FIX 6, closed.** `deployToRemoteHandler`'s (`packages/mcp/src/handlers/deployment.ts`) final success return spread the remote's parsed response body AFTER this handler's own `ok`/`remote`/`plan` — a later key always wins in an object literal, so a compromised or malicious remote replying `200 {"ok":false,"remote":"evil"}` could silently overwrite the caller-visible verdict. Fixed by reordering (`deployment.ts:293`): `responseBody` spread FIRST, canonical keys set after. Verified the vulnerability and the fix standalone (`node -e`, exact object literals from both orderings) before touching the real source, confirming the pre-fix shape really did yield `{"ok":false,"remote":"evil",...}`. `redaction-lint-suppressions.ts`'s matching entry updated in place (matched by exact source line, not line number, per that file's own documented discipline) — 0 net new/stale findings. Test: a mock remote returning `200 {"ok":false,"remote":"evil","plan":"not-a-boolean"}` — `deployToRemoteHandler`'s own `ok`/`remote`/`plan` all come back correct regardless.

**FIX 7, closed.** `aart remote add` accepted any URL scheme with zero signal that a `http://` (non-loopback) remote sends the Bearer deploy token cleartext. Added `cleartextTokenWarning` (`deployment.ts:221`, exported from `@aart/mcp`) — a warning, never a refusal (a private-network/VPN-only http deployment is a legitimate topology this code has no business unilaterally forbidding, matching `DEPLOY.md`'s own operator-owns-the-exposure-decision framing); skips `localhost`/`127.0.0.1`/`[::1]` (verified via Node's own `URL` parser that the IPv6 loopback literal's `.hostname` keeps its brackets — `"[::1]"`, not `"::1"` — a real gotcha the implementation and its tests both account for). **Deliberate scope decision, flagged**: wired into BOTH `aart remote add` (`commands/remote.ts:35`, explicitly named by the ruling) AND `deployToRemoteHandler`'s own push path (covering `aart push` AND `aart_deploy` in one place) rather than only the CLI's `pushCommand` — one implementation shared by both surfaces, matching the three-clients precedent this entire D1 feature was built around, and the ruling's own "on push" phrasing didn't specify CLI-only. **Test-design note**: `deployToRemoteHandler`'s own success-path tests always use a `localhost` fake server (the exempt branch), so the "warns" branch can't be exercised through a live non-loopback network round trip without real network access; covered instead by direct unit tests of `cleartextTokenWarning` (both branches, plus the IPv6-bracket edge case and a malformed-URL-doesn't-throw case) and CLI-level tests of `aart remote add` (which never touches the network, so both branches are covered with zero flakiness risk). A light assertion (`result.warning` is `undefined`) added to an existing localhost-based `deployToRemoteHandler` test proves the wiring doesn't spuriously fire for the common local/test case.

**FIX 8, closed.** `router.ts`'s `readBody` Content-Length pre-check branch rejected + `req.resume()`d with no `"error"` listener attached, unlike the accumulation branch below (which always has one). No reproducible crash found on this session's pinned Node (22.22.2) — confirmed, not assumed — but that's an undocumented implementation detail, not a contract to depend on. One-line fix (`router.ts:172`, `req.on("error", () => {})`, attached before `resume()`); no new observable behavior, so no new test — the existing `router.test.ts` coverage of this exact branch (a Content-Length-over-cap 413) is unaffected and still green.

**FIX 9, closed.** `triggerWorkflowHandler` (`deployment.ts`) treated ANY `Deployment` row as "deployed," including `promoted:false` ones (D1's own push-without-promote dormancy state) — `deploymentToBinding` (`@aart/server`'s `triggers/registry.ts`) already skips these for every REAL trigger path (webhook/github/slack HTTP, the poll ticker), so a pushed-but-unpromoted workflow reported as triggerable through this MCP tool while every other real trigger path treated it as dormant. Fixed by filtering to `promoted !== false` rows (`deployment.ts:101`) before the "is this deployed" check; when the ONLY rows are `promoted:false`, refuses with a remedy naming the promotion step in all three forms (`aart promote`, `POST /workflows/:id/promote`, the dashboard) rather than the generic "not deployed" message. Server's `POST /runs/trigger` (pre-existing manual-run semantics) deliberately untouched, per the ruling's own explicit scope. New suppression entry added (a new interpolated-remedy return line in an MCP-scoped file). Tests: refuses when every Deployment is `promoted:false`; still triggers fine when at least one Deployment is runnable even if a sibling for the same workflow is `promoted:false` (mixed case).

**FIX 10, closed — a tester finding, docs only.** `aart push`/`aart_deploy` resolve `--environment <name>` against a `Deployment` on the CALLER's OWN store (`resolveDeploymentForEnvironmentName`, `real-context.ts:522` — by design: the local `Deployment`'s `triggerConfig` is what the bundle ships with), but the bare "not found" error and `AUTHORING.md` both read as if only server-side registration mattered — a first-time user who registered the environment only on the remote server hit a confusing local refusal. The error now states BOTH facts explicitly (this is a check against THIS store; the exact local remedy command; explicitly distinguished from server-side registration, which is a different store entirely) rather than a bare "not found." `AUTHORING.md`'s "(e) Deploying to the server" section gained a numbered two-part callout spelling out the two separate registrations (local, before any network call; remote, before the remote accepts the push) as its own subsection, not folded into prose as before. `DEPLOY.md`'s "Deploy token" section gained an explicit gating-matrix table (fail-closed / conditionally-gated / always-open, matching FIX 3's own new tier) plus the trust-boundary reasoning for why promote isn't fail-closed like the other three; the "Ops limits" authentication bullet updated to match ("two exceptions, NOT the same shape"); `.env.example` gained a short pointer to the same matrix and the dashboard's own separate `AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN` requirement (a small, deliberate scope addition beyond the two files the ruling named, done because leaving it stale would have directly contradicted the docs this same fix was correcting elsewhere). New test (`real-context.test.ts`) asserts the improved error names the exact local remedy command AND explicitly distinguishes it from server-side registration, not just a loose substring match.

**Gates, from clean, run TWICE in full** (`pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`, the complete sequence repeated end to end, not just the test step): **231 files / 2357 tests, all green, byte-for-byte identical across both complete from-clean runs** (A56's own 231/2336 baseline + this fix pass's net +21 across FIX 1/2/3/5/6/7/9/10's new tests, minus FIX 4's 8 deleted deploy-client.test.ts tests — file count stays at 231 since FIX 4 deleted one file and FIX 5 added exactly one). `pnpm test` re-run a further TWO times beyond that (five consecutive full runs total across this verification) for extra stability confidence — 231/2357 every single time, zero flakiness, log swept for hidden failures (`grep -niE "error TS|FAIL|✗"`) — zero matches. `check:tsconfig-refs`: OK, the same 5 non-blocking informational notes as every prior session, both runs. `lint:redaction`: clean both runs — 225 reviewed suppressions (223 A56 baseline; FIX 6 modified one entry in place, net zero; FIX 7 and FIX 9 each added one new individually-justified entry, +2; 223+2=225), 0 kept/stale findings.

**Commits (local only, none pushed), one per fix:** `4a16ddb` (FIX 1), `d8c46bb` (FIX 2), `9199c6f` (FIX 3), `d9a9626` (FIX 4), `9873f65` (FIX 5), `426e7f1` (FIX 6), `257301b` (FIX 7), `eea047f` (FIX 8), `38a8a23` (FIX 9), `33ef5b0` (FIX 10, docs) — all on `d1/remotes-and-push`, all on top of A56's own final HEAD `37b9e3c`.

### A58 — D1 "remotes + push" fix pass 2: sqlite concurrent-startup migration race genuinely closed (with a deeper root cause than assumed), structured logging wired for real, dashboard error mapping fixed, DEPLOY.md corrected

John-approved, Stig-ratified fix pass on findings from the post-A57 e2e rerun (tester, real binaries): branch `d1/remotes-and-push`, HEAD `85c700a` (A57's own final state) going in. Four fixes, one commit each, local only — no push.

**FIX A — BLOCKER, closed.** Reproduced directly (tester): a fresh-root `aart server --store sqlite` + `aart worker --store sqlite` started concurrently crashed — run 1 `{"ok":false,"error":"duplicate column name: promoted"}`, run 2 (retried) `{"ok":false,"error":"database is locked"}` on both. Root cause turned out to be TWO independent gaps, not one — the second only surfaced once the first was fixed and re-verified, exactly why "verify yourself, do not trust the paraphrase" mattered here:

1. `openSqliteStore`'s migration application (`packages/store/src/adapters/sqlite/index.ts`) was a bare `new MigrationRunner(...).up()` with no cross-process coordination — `MigrationRunner` itself (`../../migrations/types.ts`) is S0-frozen and adapter-agnostic, deliberately unaware of sqlite locking. Two processes could both read watermark 0 and both attempt `0002_deployment_promoted`'s non-idempotent `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`. Fixed with `runMigrationsCoordinated` (`index.ts`): the whole read-watermark → apply-pending → write-watermark sequence now runs inside one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction, turning `MigrationRunner.up()`'s own internal `watermark.read()` — the first thing it does — into the "recheck inside the lock" half of double-checked locking; a losing process's `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` blocks until the winner commits, then sees the fully-applied watermark and skips everything already done.
2. Reordering/wrapping alone did NOT fully fix it on first verification — re-running the mandatory test against the fix from (1) alone still failed, now with `"database is locked"` thrown from `openSqliteDb` (`db.ts`) itself, BEFORE migrations ever start. Root cause: `db.ts` set `PRAGMA busy_timeout` THIRD, after `PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL` — switching a brand-new file to WAL mode is itself a one-time, lock-sensitive operation, so a losing connection had zero retry cushion before its own `busy_timeout` was even active. Reordered `busy_timeout` first — still not sufficient: verified directly (a real two-process race, both isolated in a standalone probe and inside the real adapter) that `PRAGMA busy_timeout`'s own internal retry does not reliably cover the `journal_mode = WAL` statement regardless of ordering — it reproducibly threw `SQLITE_BUSY` (errcode 5) immediately despite `busy_timeout` already being configured on both connections. Added `db.ts`'s `withSqliteBusyRetry` — a bounded, application-level catch/sleep/retry — applied to every bootstrap statement in `openSqliteDb` (now `async`, was sync; two call sites updated, both already inside async functions, both in this same package — not part of the public export surface) and reused by `runMigrationsCoordinated`'s own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` acquisition.
3. Belt-and-braces (as briefed): `0002`'s `up()` (`migrations.ts`) now tolerates a `"duplicate column name"` failure as already-applied (matched on message text — verified `errcode`/`errstr` for this specific failure are the generic `SQLITE_ERROR`/`"SQL logic error"` shared by every schema-mismatch error this driver can throw, so message-substring matching is the only option, same discipline `server.ts`'s `bundleErrorStatus` already uses) — a backstop for schema/watermark drift outside this adapter's own tracking, not a substitute for fix (1).
4. `docker-compose.yml`: `worker` now `depends_on: server: condition: service_healthy` (mirrors `dashboard`'s own pre-existing mechanism) — no longer load-bearing for correctness, kept for deterministic startup order. `DEPLOY.md`'s systemd section notes `After=`/`Requires=` ordering (or staggering) for the same reason.

**Proving test, and the revert-proof it was actually asked to be** (`packages/store/src/e2e/migration-race.e2e.test.ts` + `migration-race-worker.mjs`, 8 iterations): spawns two GENUINELY SEPARATE OS processes (`child_process.spawn`, this repo's own established `src/e2e/*-worker.mjs` convention) against the same fresh path. Explicitly NOT `Promise.all`/`node:worker_threads` on two in-process connections — tried both during development and both falsely fail: `node:sqlite`'s `DatabaseSync` is fully synchronous, so a `busy_timeout` retry-wait on one in-process connection is a genuinely blocking call that stalls the entire JS thread, including whatever pending continuation on the OTHER in-process connection needs the event loop free to reach its own `COMMIT` — this starves it instead of letting it run concurrently, reproduced directly (`probe_interleave2.mjs`-style harness) with a deterministic false failure at ~2000ms every time; a `node:worker_threads` probe (two real OS threads) confirmed real concurrency behaves correctly, but genuine child processes are the closer match to the real bug (two real `aart server`/`aart worker` OS processes) and were used for the checked-in test. Revert-proof performed exactly as instructed: `git stash push` on just the four source files (`db.ts`/`index.ts`/`migrations.ts`/`sqlite-store.test.ts`, keeping the new test), rebuilt, reran 3 times — reliably reproduced BOTH documented crash messages on the very first iteration every time (`"database is locked"` once, `"duplicate column name: promoted"` twice); `git stash pop` restored the fix, rebuilt, reran clean 6 consecutive times. The belt-and-braces sub-fix (item 3) got its own independent unit test (`migrations.test.ts`) and its own independent revert-proof (stashed `migrations.ts` alone, confirmed the new test fails with exactly `"duplicate column name: promoted"`, restored, confirmed clean).

**FIX B, closed.** Dead `LogSink` — the FOURTH occurrence of the "a real, unit-tested mechanism built but never wired into the one production composition root that needed it" bug class in this repo (A48, A53, A57's own FIX 1, now this). `@aart/store`'s `createLogger` defaults to a silent `noopSink`; `ServerConfig`/`WorkerConfig.logSink` have existed since S2 — but nothing under `packages/cli/src` ever passed one through, so a real `aart server`/`aart worker`'s entire structured-logging surface (including A57's own FIX-3 tokenless-promote startup warning) fired into the void, despite `DEPLOY.md` documenting "structured JSON logs to stdout" as out-of-the-box. Tester confirmed: a tokenless `aart server` startup log contained only Node's sqlite experimental warning. Fixed at `packages/cli/src/real-server-port.ts:114` (`startServer`'s `startRealServer({...})` call) and `:126` (`startWorker`'s `startRealWorker({...})` call): both now pass `logSink: consoleJsonSink`, unconditionally, default-on — no new flag/env var (checked for an existing log-level/format convention first; none exists, matching the ruling's own conditional).

Test through the real CLI composition root (`real-server-port.test.ts`, new describe block, mirrors this file's own A57 deploy-token-wiring pattern exactly): spies on the real `console.error` global (there is no config surface to inject a substitute sink through `cli.serverPort.startServer`'s own public options — `@aart/mcp`'s `ServerPort` type exposes only `{port?, environment?}`), starts a real tokenless server via `cli.serverPort.startServer`, asserts the tokenless-promote warning is genuinely observable as a structured JSON line (`level`/`msg`/`service`/`component`/`time`, not just "some text landed on stderr"). Revert-proofed: stashed just `real-server-port.ts` (kept the test), reran — failed with zero captured console output (`"saw: []"`); restored, reran clean.

**Deviation flagged, not silent — no equivalent worker-side full-`startWorker()` wiring test.** `ServerPort.startWorker`'s public interface (`packages/mcp/src/types.ts`) deliberately exposes no `healthPort` override — every one of this repo's own 12 direct `startWorker` call sites (`packages/server/src/worker/worker.test.ts`) passes `healthPort: 0` specifically to avoid binding the real default port (8787) in a test; going through `cli.serverPort.startWorker(...)` as written cannot avoid that bind (the restriction is baked into `createRealServerPort`'s own implementation, not just the TS type — adding a pass-through would be a new, unrelated `ServerPort` interface expansion, out of this fix's stated "no new config surface" scope). Introducing the first fixed-port-binding test in this codebase, purely to mirror the server-side proof, was judged a worse trade (real CI-flakiness risk) than relying on the code-level symmetry with the already-proven server-side change (identical one-line `logSink: consoleJsonSink` addition, same `createServerLogger`/`consoleJsonSink` mechanism).

**FIX C, closed.** Tester transcript: promote via the dashboard without a token, against a token-gated real server → the upstream `401` collapsed into an HTML `"500 Internal Server Error"` page, the real message buried in a `<pre>` — `Router.handle`'s generic catch (`packages/dashboard/src/http/router.ts`) always returned HTML 500 regardless of the real error. Fixed with a new `HttpError` class (`http/router.ts`, co-located with `sendJson`/`sendHtml` — generic infra, deliberately NOT importing from the domain-specific `api-client.ts` one layer up) carrying a real status. `api-client.ts`'s real `createHttpApiClient` now throws `HttpError` (not bare `Error`) at every non-2xx site (`getJson`, `postJsonOrThrow`, `getRun`, `getWorkflow`, `updateCorrectionRunOutput`, `createEvalExampleFromCorrection`, `createIssueForCorrection`, `clearRunFlag`) instead of only baking the status into the message text. `Router.handle`'s catch-all now checks `url.pathname.startsWith("/api/")`: preserves `err.status` verbatim for an `HttpError` (any status, not a hardcoded allowlist — "at minimum 401/403/404/409" from the ruling is met and generalized), falls back to JSON 500 (never HTML) for a genuinely unexpected error; non-`/api/*` routes (the static-file/SPA-fallback path) are unaffected. Every route `buildDashboardRouter` registers is `/api/*` or `/health` (post-A47 SPA architecture — no server-rendered HTML page routes exist on this `Router` at all), so this is effectively the router's universal JSON contract now; scoped explicitly by pathname anyway, to stay precise and defend any future non-API route addition.

Tests: `router.test.ts` gets 4 new isolated unit tests (an `HttpError` preserved as JSON; a plain `Error` still 500 but JSON not HTML; 403/404/409 preserved identically; a non-`/api/*` route stays HTML, unaffected — the pre-existing `/boom` test's own sibling). `server.test.ts` gets the tester's exact end-to-end repro — a REAL token-gated `@aart/server` + a REAL `startDashboard` (not `createFakeApiClient`, which never goes through HTTP/`HttpError` at all): promote via the dashboard's own `/api/workflows/:id/promote` with no token on the dashboard's client returns `401` JSON (not 500 HTML), and a follow-up `/api/health` request proves the dashboard stayed up. Revert-proofed: stashed both source files (kept all 5 new tests), reran — all 5 failed exactly as expected (401 assertions saw 500; one test's own `new HttpError(...)` construction threw `"is not a constructor"` against the reverted module — itself proof the class didn't exist pre-fix); restored, reran clean (99/99 across the whole package).

**FIX D, closed — docs only, no code.** Three corrections to `DEPLOY.md`, each verified against source before writing, one caught and corrected mid-draft:

1. **Store choice: fs vs sqlite** — the table's unqualified `"**Yes** — this is what it's for"` for sqlite's multi-process safety is now `"**Yes** — including a coordinated concurrent first startup (AMENDMENTS.md A58, see below)"`, with a new paragraph beneath the table stating precisely what "safe" means (concurrent FIRST startup now coordinated via FIX A; still single-writer-at-a-time at the SQLite level, which `job_queue`'s own ADR-05 claim leasing already assumes and correctly handles). `docker-compose.yml`'s own header comment (same unqualified claim) now points at this corrected section.
2. **Ops limits' logging bullet** — was `"What you get out of the box: structured JSON logs to stdout (wire `consoleJsonSink`)"`, worded as if an operator had to do the wiring. Now: `"**What you get out of the box, no wiring required (AMENDMENTS.md A58):** `aart server` and `aart worker` (both real composition roots, `packages/cli/src/real-server-port.ts`) unconditionally wire `consoleJsonSink` — one JSON-stringified line per log call, shaped `{level, msg, time, ...context}` (`service`/`component` and request-scoped fields like `runId` where relevant) — `debug`/`info` lines to stdout, `warn`/`error` lines to stderr. There is no flag to turn this off and no level filter — every level is always emitted"` — distinguished explicitly from `@aart/store`'s own `createLogger()` LIBRARY default, which is still a no-op (that part was already true and stays true — only the CLI's own composition-root behavior changed).
3. **`aart trigger add`'s real behavior**, previously undocumented anywhere. Verified directly (`packages/cli/src/commands/deployment.ts`'s `triggerAddCommand`, `packages/mcp/src/handlers/deployment.ts`'s `deployWorkflowHandler`) that it attaches to whichever `Deployment` row `deployments.list({workflowId}).at(-1)` returns — NOT reliably "most recent": the `fs` adapter sorts alphabetically by the deployment's random `id` (`KeyedJsonCollection.list()`), unrelated to creation time; the `sqlite` adapter's query has no `ORDER BY` at all (unspecified row order). **Caught and corrected during drafting, not shipped wrong:** an earlier draft of this section's "workaround" claimed "deploy, then immediately `trigger add`, one environment at a time" was reliable for every environment in sequence — verified against `deployWorkflowHandler`'s actual `id: newId("deploy")` behavior that every `aart deploy` call creates a BRAND NEW row, never updating one in place, so a workflow deployed to a second environment already has a prior row in the table by the time `trigger add` runs again — the SAME list-order ambiguity applies, the workaround does NOT generalize. Documented honestly instead: the first-ever-deployment case is the only deterministic one; for a second or later deployment, the section now tells the operator to verify which row actually changed (`GET /deployments` — grep-confirmed real route, `server.ts:571` — or the dashboard's Deployments view) rather than trust a false workaround. A real `--environment` flag on `trigger add` is explicitly out of scope here (D2 backlog, per the ruling) — not implemented.

Scoped to `DEPLOY.md` only, matching the ruling's own explicit framing ("make DEPLOY.md true") — `AUTHORING.md`'s own `aart trigger add` example (part (d), single-environment context, not misleading there) was left untouched, not silently expanded.

**Deviation flagged, not silent — the briefing's own file list for FIX B's composition roots.** The task named `packages/cli/src/real-server-port.ts, real-context.ts, commands/process.ts`. `packages/cli/src/real-context.ts` does not exist — the actual CLI composition root that constructs `createRealServerPort(...)` is `cli-context.ts` (there IS a same-named but unrelated `packages/mcp/src/real-context.ts`, likely the source of the mix-up). Checked `cli-context.ts` directly: it only threads `store`/`engine`/`root` into `createRealServerPort`, nothing logSink-related to wire there. Checked `commands/process.ts` directly too: it calls `cli.serverPort.startServer({port, environment})`/`startWorker({workerId})` — `ServerPort`'s own public interface (verified, `packages/mcp/src/types.ts`) has no `logSink` parameter at all, by design, so there is nothing for this file to pass through either; the fix is entirely internal to `real-server-port.ts`, matching "no new config surface."

**Gates, from clean, run TWICE in full** (`pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`): **232 files / 2365 tests, all green, byte-for-byte identical across both complete from-clean runs** (A57's own 231/2357 baseline + this pass's net +1 file / +8 tests — FIX A: +1 file/+2 tests (`migration-race.e2e.test.ts` + the belt-and-braces unit test); FIX B: +1 test; FIX C: +5 tests; FIX D: docs only, 0 tests). `pnpm test` re-run a further TWO times beyond the two full-clean runs (five consecutive full runs total) — 232/2365 every time, zero flakiness; log swept for hidden failures (`grep -niE "error TS|FAIL|✗"`) both full runs — zero matches. Given FIX A's own new test spawns genuinely concurrent OS processes (the highest flakiness-risk addition in this pass), it was ALSO run in isolation 5 additional times beyond the runs already inside the five full-suite passes (11 total observed passes of that one test across this verification) — clean every time. `check:tsconfig-refs`: OK, the same 5 non-blocking informational notes as every prior session, both runs. `lint:redaction`: clean both runs — 225 reviewed suppressions, UNCHANGED from A57's own final count (this fix pass introduced no new suppression-worthy code — no raw secret/credential handling touched).

**Commits (local only, none pushed), one per fix, all on `d1/remotes-and-push` on top of A57's own final HEAD `85c700a`:** `2374f3e` (FIX A), `2ef97dc` (FIX B), `7698930` (FIX C), `08a1247` (FIX D, docs).
