## 2026-07-12 — D2a security hardening

### A59 — D2a: unified conditional auth middleware closing the "every mutation route except promote is unauthenticated" gap a focused security review found, plus the localhost-default breaking-change bind, token rotation, token-derived attribution's mechanical half, and the dashboard's own closure

John-ratified plan (D2+V1) and D2a design, including the localhost-default-bind breaking-change call, 2026-07-12 ("go"). Branch `d2a/auth-middleware` off `main`@`81d46ac` (clean, CI green going in). Six commits, each a coherent unit, local only — no push. Every file:line reference in the briefing was verified against `main`@`81d46ac` before implementation began and matched exactly — no drift found.

**Context.** D1 (A56) shipped git-style remotes + `aart push` over HTTP, gating three deploy-surface routes (fail-closed) plus `POST /workflows/:id/promote` (conditionally, A57 fix pass) with a shared bearer token. A focused security review found the remaining ~17 mutation routes this server exposes were, by construction, exactly as unauthenticated as promote had been before A57 — network-reachable, capable of triggering runs/deciding approvals/mutating governance state, with `AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN` playing no role at all. D2a closes that.

**PART 1 — unified conditional auth middleware.** `RouteOptions` (`packages/server/src/http/router.ts`) gains `auth?: (ctx: RouteContext) => boolean | Promise<boolean>`, run inside `Router.handle` BEFORE `readBody` — an unauthenticated caller can no longer force this process to buffer/parse a request body at all (closing a pre-existing gap for the three ALREADY-gated fail-closed routes too, whose inline checks used to run after the body was already read). Framework-free by design: the type has zero reference to `ServerConfig`/`deploy-token.ts`; the actual policy lives in `server.ts`'s own `requireDeployToken`/`requireDeployTokenIfConfigured` closures over `config`, passed in as plain functions — mirrors `maxBodyBytes`'s own established shape (a value this file has no opinion about the source of). `requireDeployTokenIfConfigured` (`server.ts:167`, previously hardcoded to promote's own wording) gains an `actionLabel` param defaulting to a route-neutral phrase, so each route's 401 remedy names what it actually gates; the promote call site passes `"promote a workflow version"`, preserving A57's exact original wording byte-for-byte. Applied via `{ auth: (ctx) => requireDeployTokenIfConfigured(config, ctx, "<label>") }` to all 17 routes the brief's own verified line-number list named (`/runs/trigger`, `/approvals/:id/decision`, `/workflows/:id/approve`, `/block-promotion`, `/unblock-promotion`, `/mark-needs-review`, `/clear-needs-review`, `/trigger-improvement`, `/corrections`, `/corrections/:key/update-run-output`, `/corrections/:key/create-eval-example`, `/corrections/:key/create-issue`, `/evals/suites`, `/evals/runs`, `/runs/:runId/resume`, `/runs/:runId/signal`, `/runs/:runId/flag/clear`); the three fail-closed routes (`/bundles/ingest`, `/bundles/plan`, `/environments`) and promote itself migrated from an inline `if (!require...) return;` first line to the SAME `auth` option, fixing their auth-before-body gap for free. The startup warning (`server.ts`, fires once when `deployToken` unset) reworded from naming promote alone to describing the widened scope generically, still containing the literal substring `"UNAUTHENTICATED"` the existing A58 logSink-wiring test greps for.

**The completeness test** (`server.test.ts`, `describe("auth-gate completeness...")`) enumerates every registered route via a new `Router.getRoutes()`/`ServerHandle.getRoutes()` read-only accessor and asserts each POST route either carries an explicit `auth` value or is named in a small, hardcoded, reviewed open-allowlist (the three `/webhooks/*` routes — separate per-binding HMAC verification, untouched). A second test asserts every allowlist entry corresponds to a real registered route (closing the "vacuous allowlist entry" gap the other direction). **Proven to actually catch a regression, as required**: temporarily removed `/runs/trigger`'s `auth` option, ran the suite — failed with `AssertionError: POST /runs/trigger is NOT on the open-allowlist and has no explicit auth option ...: expected undefined to be defined` — restored the file from a pre-edit backup, `diff` confirmed byte-identical, reran clean.

A shared table-driven suite (`describe("uniform conditional auth gate...")`, 17 rows, one `it` per route) proves the 4-case pattern for every newly-gated route: rather than hardcoding an assumed status per route, each test captures the route's own natural baseline response (deployToken unset) and asserts a correct-token response is byte-identical to it, while wrong/missing-token responses are `401` — proving auth wraps around existing behavior without altering it. Each row is also cross-checked against the real router registration (`handle.getRoutes()`), so a typo'd path in the test table itself, or a route that silently lost its `auth` option, cannot make the test pass for the wrong reason.

**PART 2 — auth-before-body + global body cap.** `Router.handle`'s `readBody` call changed from `matched.maxBodyBytes` (routes without an explicit cap were completely unbounded) to `matched.maxBodyBytes ?? DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES`. `DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES = 1_048_576` (1MB) added to `config.ts` alongside `MAX_BUNDLE_INGEST_BYTES` — control-plane bodies are small JSON payloads by construction; the two bundle routes keep their own, much larger, explicit 10MB cap, which still overrides the default (`??` only falls back on `undefined`). This default applies globally, including to the three `/webhooks/*` routes (unrelated to the auth question — a legitimate, separate hygiene improvement for third-party-supplied payloads; verified no existing webhook test sends a body anywhere near 1MB). **Deviation from a literal reading of the brief's "keep router.ts framework-free... imports NOTHING from... config.ts"**: `router.ts` DOES import `DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES` from `../config.js` (Part 2's own instruction explicitly says so: "Add DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES to config.ts... `readBody(req, matched.maxBodyBytes ?? DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES)`" at router.ts). Resolved by reading "framework-free" as scoped to Part 1's AUTH mechanism specifically (no `deploy-token.ts` import, no `ServerConfig` type reference, the `auth` field stays fully generic) rather than a blanket ban on any config.ts reference — `MAX_BUNDLE_INGEST_BYTES` already flows from config.ts into a route registration the identical way; a shared numeric constant is not domain/business logic the way "what a valid deploy token is" would be. No cycle: `config.ts` has no import of `http/router.ts`, directly or transitively.

`router.test.ts`'s former "no maxBodyBytes = unbounded" test (asserting a 2MB body succeeds on an uncapped route) is now FALSE — recalibrated to a normal-sized (10KB) body proving what's still true, plus a NEW test proving the opposite: the identical 2MB body that used to succeed now rejects `413`.

**PART 3 — localhost-default bind + `--host`/`AART_HOST` (BREAKING, ships atomically with the compose/systemd migration).** `ServerHttpConfig.host?: string` + `DEFAULT_HTTP_HOST = "127.0.0.1"` (`config.ts`); `server.ts`'s `server.listen(port, ...)` (previously no host argument -> Node's own every-interface default) becomes `server.listen(port, config.host ?? DEFAULT_HTTP_HOST, ...)`. `ServerPort.startServer`'s public config type (`packages/mcp/src/types.ts`) gains `host?: string`; `createRealServerPort` (`real-server-port.ts`) threads it straight through; `commands/process.ts`'s `serverCommand` parses `--host`/`AART_HOST` with the SAME flag > env > default precedence `--environment`/`AART_ENVIRONMENT` already established; `cli.ts`'s `USAGE` string updated. `docker-compose.yml`'s `server` service command gains `--host 0.0.0.0` (required, not optional — a loopback-bound container is unreachable via the `dashboard` service's own Docker-network-alias `AART_SERVER_URL` AND via the published `8080:8080` port; the healthcheck below it runs `curl` inside the SAME container's namespace, so it would keep passing and silently mask exactly that regression) with an explanatory comment; the "Bare `docker run`" doc example in `DEPLOY.md` gets the identical addition. `DEPLOY.md` gains a new "Network binding" section (linked from the TOC) spelling out who must set `--host` on upgrade (docker-compose already does; Path B/systemd needs a manual `Environment=AART_HOST=0.0.0.0` add to `ExecStart`'s unit; a genuinely single-host deployment needs no change).

`worker/health.ts` investigated per the brief's own explicit instruction ("VERIFY don't assume") — confirmed it has the identical all-interfaces bind (`server.listen(port, cb)`, no host arg) — and left DELIBERATELY UNCHANGED, not silently: it's read-only (`{status, claimedRuns, uptime, version}`, zero mutation capability, categorically lower risk than the ~20 write routes this whole sub-phase is about), and is BY DESIGN cross-container-reachable today (`docker-compose.yml`'s `AART_WORKER_URLS: http://worker:8787`, feeding the dashboard's already-shipped worker-health page) — locking it to loopback would silently break that feature unless `docker-compose.yml` also gained a matching override, additional breaking-change surface the ratified plan didn't ask for. Flagged as a reasonable fast-follow.

**PART 4 — token rotation.** `checkAnyDeployToken(configuredTokens, provided)` (`deploy-token.ts`) — a thin wrapper checking `provided` against every candidate via the SAME unmodified `checkDeployToken` (constant-time comparison untouched); `ServerConfig.deployTokenNext?: string`; both `requireDeployToken` and `requireDeployTokenIfConfigured` call `checkAnyDeployToken([config.deployToken, config.deployTokenNext], provided)`. `secrets.ts`'s `resolveDeployTokenNext(root)` mirrors `resolveDeployToken`'s own resolution mechanism (`AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN_NEXT` env, then `<root>/secrets.json`'s own key) exactly; `real-server-port.ts` resolves and threads it the same way/timing as `deployToken`. `deployTokenNext` unset (the default) is byte-identical to the pre-rotation single-token check — verified by a dedicated test. `.env.example`/`DEPLOY.md` document the rotation workflow (publish `AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN_NEXT`, both accepted, migrate callers, promote to `AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN`, clear `_NEXT`).

**PART 5 — token-derived attribution ("mechanical half"; named per-token identities deferred, stated explicitly, not silently).** `RouteContext.authenticated?: { label: string }` (`router.ts`); `requireDeployTokenIfConfigured` stamps `ctx.authenticated = { label: "deploy-token" }` on success via a PROVIDED, matching token (never the "unconfigured, proceed" branch, which has no caller identity). **Deviation from the brief's offered OR, stated explicitly**: the brief allowed a configurable `ServerConfig.deployTokenLabel` OR hardcoding "deploy-token" as a flagged fast-follow — hardcoded. Reasoning: this is the mechanical half specifically BECAUSE named per-token identities are out of scope; a single global label-string config doesn't add real attribution value (it doesn't distinguish WHICH of possibly several callers used the token, which is what rotation's two-valid-tokens state would make meaningfully ambiguous to begin with) — implementing it would have added composition-root wiring + a mandatory CLI-entry test (per this repo's own STANDING IMPERATIVE) for near-zero payoff today. `requireDeployToken` (the fail-closed sibling) deliberately does NOT set `ctx.authenticated` — none of its three routes consume it.

`POST /approvals/:id/decision` threads `ctx.authenticated?.label` into `decideApprovalTask`'s new `authenticatedAs?: string` input field via an EXPLICIT ALLOWLIST construction (`{ status: decision.status, reviewer: decision.reviewer, decision: decision.decision, trustMode: decision.trustMode, authenticatedAs: ctx.authenticated?.label }`), never a blind `{ ...decision, authenticatedAs: ... }` spread — a client including `authenticatedAs` in its own request body cannot self-attribute a decision (verified by a dedicated test sending exactly that payload against an unconfigured server and confirming it's ignored). Persisted onto a new, additive `ApprovalTask.authenticatedAs?: string` (`packages/types/src/approval.ts`), same pattern as A56's own `Deployment.promoted` — including the SAME follow-through: a third sqlite migration (`0003_approval_task_authenticated_as`, `packages/store/src/adapters/sqlite/migrations.ts`), nullable `ALTER TABLE approval_tasks ADD COLUMN authenticated_as TEXT`, not folded into the baseline DDL, with the identical "duplicate column name" tolerance A58's belt-and-braces fix established for `0002`. `SqliteApprovalStore`'s row<->`ApprovalTask` mapping and INSERT/ON CONFLICT UPDATE updated; the fs adapter needs no change (schema-free JSON collection). The existing three `0002`-specific tests in `migrations.test.ts` were RESCOPED to an explicit `[0001, 0002]` migration list (rather than `ALL_SQLITE_MIGRATIONS`, which now also includes `0003`) so they stay about `0002`'s own behavior instead of coupling to whatever the total happens to be — the "down() reverts 0002" test specifically needed this, since `MigrationRunner.down()` reverts exactly the LAST-applied migration; against the full 3-migration list it would have reverted `0003`, not `0002`, silently testing the wrong thing. A new, identically-shaped `0003` describe block (pre-populated-db upgrade, tolerance, down/up round-trip) mirrors `0002`'s own three tests exactly. Every other hardcoded "2 migrations total" assertion repo-wide (`sqlite-store.test.ts` ×2, `migration-race.e2e.test.ts` + `migration-race-worker.mjs`) updated to 3; the e2e worker now also checks the new column survives the concurrent-startup race, not just watermark/`promoted`. `conformance.ts` gains an `authenticatedAs` set/omitted round-trip test, run against both adapters.

`POST /workflows/:id/approve` gets two closures in the SAME change: (a) the unvalidated `trustMode` cast fixed — the identical bug shape A57 FIX 2 closed for `POST /environments` (a typo'd value silently persists, every real reader downgrades an unrecognized string to `"governed"` with zero signal) — via the same `requireValidTrustMode` helper, with a sibling test mirroring `/environments`' own invalid-trustMode test; (b) LOG-ONLY attribution (`logger.info` with `{workflowId, version, action, authenticatedAs}`) — `approveOrDeprecateWorkflow` has no actor param today and its persisted shape is deliberately NOT extended (out of scope, stated explicitly in the brief), so logging is the mechanism available without touching that function's signature.

**PART 6 — dashboard closure.** `api-client.ts`'s `createHttpApiClient` generalizes `promoteAuthHeaders` (A57's own promote-only name) to `writeAuthHeaders`, applied to all 15 additional write methods this client exposes — 12 via the existing `postJson`/`postJsonOrThrow` `extraHeaders` param, 3 (`updateCorrectionRunOutput`, `createEvalExampleFromCorrection`, `createIssueForCorrection` — raw `fetch`, not `postJson*`) via a `headers: { ...writeAuthHeaders }` structural addition. `createHttpApiClient`'s own public signature is UNCHANGED — `deploy/serve-dashboard.mjs` (which already resolves `AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN` into this client per A57) needs no update; confirmed by reading it directly, not assumed. Tests: the three existing unauthenticated "round trip over real HTTP" tests re-run verbatim in shape against a TOKEN-GATED server with a token-carrying client (proving every route, not just `promoteWorkflow`, now attaches and needs the header), plus a compact data-driven negative test proving all 15 newly-gated methods reject `401` without a token.

**Docs closure.** `DEPLOY.md`'s "Deploy token" section: the former inline "Gating matrix" bolded paragraph promoted to a real `### Gating matrix` heading (now linkable) with the table rewritten for D2a's uniform-coverage scope — the "conditionally gated" tier now lists all ~17 routes instead of promote alone, with a new paragraph explaining "conditionally gated is the norm now, not the exception." A "Token rotation" note and a "Token-derived attribution" note added to the same section. The "No authentication" Ops-limits bullet rewritten — its own pre-D2a claim that this deploy kit's examples "bind to all interfaces by default" is now false, and its own claim that "every OTHER write route... remains exactly as open" is now false; rewritten to state precisely what changed (loopback-default bind; near-uniform token gating) and what's STILL true (no login/per-user identity, a shared token is not a logged-in-operator concept). `AUTHORING.md` §(e) (264-395) checked directly for stale token-gating cross-references — none found; its one `AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN` mention (the fail-closed ingest gate) is unaffected by this session, left untouched.

**Tests, summarized** (all described above, in context): the completeness test + its own actually-demonstrated red/green proof; the 17-row uniform-gate table; the default-body-cap opposite-behavior test; the composition-root host-binding test through `cli.serverPort.startServer` (default loopback, explicit host honored) PLUS a full CLI-entry test (`cli.test.ts`) proving `--host`/`AART_HOST` reaches the real bind via `run() -> serverCommand -> cli.serverPort.startServer`, using a thin `ServerPort` wrapper to capture the bound address before `serverCommand`'s own non-blocking mode closes the handle (there is no other window to observe a live socket through `run()`'s own return value alone); the token-rotation suite (NEXT accepted, primary still accepted, neither accepted -> 401, unset-NEXT unchanged); the `/workflows/:id/approve` trustMode-validation + attribution-field tests (including the self-attribution-is-ignored case); the sqlite migration's own three-test pattern plus the repo-wide "2 -> 3 migrations" ripple; the dashboard's positive+negative uniform-gate coverage. Existing tests at `server.test.ts`'s deploy-surface/promote-gating describe blocks kept passing UNMODIFIED (verified — migrating the auth mechanism's location doesn't change any existing assertion); `server.test.ts`'s own "GET routes stay open" test (unaffected by `deployToken`) stays true, untouched.

**Gates, from clean, run in full THREE times (two required, one extra given this session's scope)** (`pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`): **232 files / 2418 tests, all green, byte-for-byte identical across all three complete from-clean runs** (A58's own 232/2365 baseline + this session's net +53 tests, 0 new files — every addition landed inside an existing test file). `check:tsconfig-refs`: OK, the same 5 non-blocking informational notes as every prior session, all runs. `lint:redaction`: clean all runs — 225 reviewed suppressions, UNCHANGED from A58's own final count (this session introduced zero new suppression-worthy call sites — verified directly: the one new `store.approvals.put(updated)` call site in `approvals.ts` keeps its exact pre-existing line text, so its existing A47 suppression entry still matches; the new `logger.info(...)` attribution line doesn't match the lint's `console.*`-only pattern at all). Given `migration-race.e2e.test.ts` spawns genuinely concurrent OS processes (the highest flakiness-risk addition in this session, inherited from A58's own concurrency-race infrastructure applied to a third migration), it was ALSO run in isolation 3 additional times beyond the three full-suite passes — clean every time (24 further concurrent-race iterations, 48 further process spawns, zero flakiness).

**`DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES` final value: 1MB (1,048,576 bytes)**, as specified — sanity-checked against this repo's own real workflow-input fixtures (trigger payloads, approval decisions, correction records, eval suite definitions) used throughout the test suite; none approaches even a fraction of 1MB, so no adjustment was warranted.

**`worker/health.ts` host-bind decision:** investigated, confirmed the same all-interfaces default, deliberately left UNCHANGED — full reasoning under PART 3 above.

**Deviations flagged, not silent:** (1) Part 2's `router.ts` DOES import `config.ts` (`DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES`) — reconciles the brief's own two directives, reasoning under PART 2 above. (2) Part 5's `deployTokenLabel` hardcoded rather than made configurable — the brief's own offered fallback, reasoning under PART 5 above.

**Every new `ServerConfig` field's composition-root wiring + from-CLI-entry test, per this repo's own STANDING IMPERATIVE:**
- `host?: string` — wired in `real-server-port.ts` (`startServer`'s `startRealServer({..., host: config.host})`) and `commands/process.ts` (`--host`/`AART_HOST` parsing); tested through `cli.serverPort.startServer` directly (`real-server-port.test.ts`, default loopback + explicit host) AND through the full `run(["server", "--host", ...])` CLI entry (`cli.test.ts`, all three precedence cases).
- `deployTokenNext?: string` — wired in `real-server-port.ts` (`resolveDeployTokenNext(root)` resolved and threaded the same way as `deployToken`); no CLI flag (env-var/secrets.json-only, matching `deployToken`'s own established resolution shape, no new flag asked for) — tested through `cli.serverPort.startServer` directly (`real-server-port.test.ts`, all four rotation cases).
- `deployTokenLabel?: string` — NOT added (see deviation (2) above); no wiring/test obligation since the field doesn't exist.

## 2026-07-12 — D2a fix pass

### A60 — D2a fix pass: webhook body-cap regression closed (GitHub deliveries were being silently, permanently dropped), a `deployTokenNext`-without-primary hole closed on the fail-closed tier, startup "listening" log lines added for both `aart server` and `aart worker`, and three smaller polish findings closed

John-ratified fix pass, 2026-07-12, on findings from the post-A59 three-perspective verification of branch `d2a/auth-middleware` (security: SHIP; cold auditor: pass-with-reservations; e2e tester: PASS-with-notes). Branch `d2a/auth-middleware`, HEAD `0858768` (A59's own final state) going in. Six fixes plus one self-caught typecheck correction, one commit each, local only — no push.

**Context.** A59 (immediately above) added the unified conditional auth middleware, a global `DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES` (1MB) default, and the localhost-default bind. Verification found one MAJOR reliability regression (the webhook body cap), one MINOR security/doc contradiction (`deployTokenNext`), and four smaller polish items. Targeted fixes only, as briefed — no redesign.

**FIX 1 — MAJOR, closed.** A59's global `DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES` (1MB, `config.ts`) silently applied to the three `/webhooks/*` routes too — they passed no `maxBodyBytes` of their own, so `Router.handle`'s `matched.maxBodyBytes ?? DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES` fallback caught them. GitHub webhook deliveries run up to GitHub's own documented ceiling — verified directly against `https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/webhook-events-and-payloads`: *"Payloads are capped at 25 MB."* (confirmed unchanged across the GitHub Enterprise Cloud/Server doc versions checked too) — and GitHub does not retry a delivery it can't make, so a >1MB delivery arriving under A59's new default would now 413 before HMAC/intake ever ran and be PERMANENTLY, SILENTLY dropped, never redelivered; pre-A59 these three routes were fully unbounded. Added `MAX_WEBHOOK_INGEST_BYTES = 26_214_400` (25 MiB — `config.ts:174`, right next to `MAX_BUNDLE_INGEST_BYTES`) — 25 MiB rather than 25 MB decimal (25,000,000 bytes) specifically so it exceeds GitHub's stated ceiling under EITHER unit convention. A hardcoded constant, matching `MAX_BUNDLE_INGEST_BYTES`'s own established precedent, not a configurable `AART_WEBHOOK_MAX_BYTES` env knob — no strong reason surfaced during implementation to deviate from that precedent for this sibling constant. Applied via `{ maxBodyBytes: MAX_WEBHOOK_INGEST_BYTES }` on all three webhook route registrations (`server.ts:333`, `:351`, `:365` — generic, github, slack). Still strictly safer than pre-A59 `main`'s fully unbounded read; just no longer silently dropping legitimate large deliveries the way the 1MB global default did.

Test (`server.test.ts:158`, `:186`): a real, HMAC-signed ~2MB webhook body — over the old 1MB default, comfortably under the new cap — now reaches HMAC verification and trigger intake, returning `200`/`{kind: "started"}` (previously would 413 before HMAC ever ran, proven as the exact pre-A60 regression shape); a 27MB body still 413s. Both added to the existing `describe("webhook ingress...")` block rather than tripled across all three routes — mirrors how `/bundles/plan` doesn't repeat `/bundles/ingest`'s own size-cap test (one shared mechanism, proven once against a representative route).

Docs: DEPLOY.md's gating-matrix "Open, always" row (`~line 367` pre-fix) said the three `/webhooks/*` routes are "completely untouched" — still true for the deploy-token/HMAC auth question specifically (verified: the auth mechanism genuinely is untouched by this fix), but read unqualified it now overclaims given the new body cap; reworded to scope "untouched" precisely to auth and point at the new cap documentation. **Deviation flagged, not silent:** the brief called this "reconcile the gating-matrix + ops-limits body-cap wording," implying pre-existing ops-limits body-cap wording to reconcile — verified directly (grepped DEPLOY.md for "cap"/"MB"/"byte"/"buffer" before writing anything) that no such bullet existed; A59's own DEPLOY.md closure never added one. A NEW Ops-limits bullet ("Request body size caps," `DEPLOY.md:736`) was added instead, documenting all three tiers (1MB/10MB/25MiB) — see the exact wording below.

**FIX 2 — MINOR, closed.** `requireDeployToken` (`server.ts`, the fail-closed tier: `/bundles/ingest`, `/bundles/plan`, `/environments`) called `checkAnyDeployToken([config.deployToken, config.deployTokenNext], provided)` with NO guard that `deployToken` was actually set — unlike its conditional sibling `requireDeployTokenIfConfigured`, which already guards `if (!config.deployToken) return true`. Setting ONLY `AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN_NEXT` (no primary) let a caller holding that NEXT value authenticate against a fail-closed route — contradicting both the 401 remedy ("no AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN configured") and the function's own pre-existing doc comment ("cannot substitute for the primary token being configured at all"); that comment was aspirational, not actually enforced, until this fix. Fixed with an explicit `if (!config.deployToken)` early-return guard (`server.ts:142`) that refuses unconditionally before `deployTokenNext` is ever consulted — mirrors `requireDeployTokenIfConfigured`'s own guard exactly, same remedy text preserved byte-for-byte (verified: every pre-existing test asserting either remedy string still passes unmodified). `checkAnyDeployToken` itself untouched (a pure helper, as briefed); `requireDeployTokenIfConfigured`'s existing guard checked directly and confirmed already-correct pre-fix — no change needed there.

**Verified the guard closes a real hole, not a hypothetical one, exactly as required:** wrote the fail-closed-tier test FIRST and ran it against the UNMODIFIED pre-fix code — failed exactly as the brief predicted (`expected 401, got 200`: a caller supplying only the `deployTokenNext` value authenticated against `/bundles/ingest` with no primary token configured at all). Applied the guard, reran — passed. The conditional-tier companion test (`/runs/trigger`, comparing byte-identical to the fully-unconfigured baseline) already PASSED pre-fix, confirming `requireDeployTokenIfConfigured` was never the problem — a non-regression check, not a second hole. Both tests: `server.test.ts:991`, `:1011`.

**FIX 3, closed.** Neither `aart server` nor `aart worker`'s health listener logged anything on bind — mechanically silent, so an operator couldn't tell a loopback-only bind from an all-interfaces one at the moment it mattered, only discoverable later via a failed remote connection — a real gap given A59's own breaking-change loopback-default bind. `startServer` (`server.ts:826`) now logs `logger.info("aart server listening", { host, port: boundPort })` immediately after `server.listen`'s callback has genuinely fired (`boundPort` only resolves once it has) — `host` is the SAME `config.host ?? DEFAULT_HTTP_HOST` value already threaded into `.listen()` a few lines above, `boundPort` the REAL bound port (not the `port: 0` request itself). `startWorker` (`worker.ts:168`) now logs `logger.info("aart worker health listening", { host: healthHost, port: health.port })` right after `startHealthServer` resolves, reading `healthHost` off `health.server.address()` — mirrors `startServer`'s own `.address()` idiom — rather than assuming a literal `"0.0.0.0"` (Node's dual-stack default can bind `::`). Deliberately still all-interfaces (A59 PART 3's own ruling, unchanged by this fix) — this only makes that visible, it doesn't change the bind. `worker/health.ts` itself was left untouched (still zero logging dependency) — the line lives in `worker.ts`, which already holds a component-scoped `logger` and already exposes `health.server` for the address, the smaller, non-invasive change achieving the identical observable line without threading a new `Logger` dependency through `health.ts`'s public signature or its one call site's neighbors.

Tests: the server line proven through the REAL CLI composition root (`real-server-port.test.ts:409`, `:427` — mirrors A58's own console-spy technique exactly: default-loopback host and an explicit `--host` both proven, plus `service`/`component` shape asserted). The worker line proven directly against `@aart/server`'s own `startWorker`, with `healthPort: 0` and an injected `logSink` array-capture (`worker.test.ts:92`), rather than through `cli.serverPort.startWorker` — **consistent with, not a departure from, A58's own already-documented deviation for this exact subsystem**: `ServerPort.startWorker`'s public interface (re-verified directly, `packages/mcp/src/types.ts`) still exposes no `healthPort` override, so a full composition-root test here would bind the real default health port (8787) — the identical flakiness risk A58 already flagged and chose to avoid. `@aart/server`'s own `logSink` config IS directly pluggable at this layer (unlike the CLI's `ServerPort` surface, which has no such seam — the actual reason A58 needed console-spying at all), making a captured-lines array both a more precise assertion and lower-risk than reproducing that technique one layer down.

**Self-caught bug, fixed before the final gates counted as valid:** the FIRST attempt at gate run 1's `pnpm run typecheck:tests` caught an implicit-`any` in the new `real-server-port.test.ts` helper — `ReturnType<typeof vi.spyOn>` used as a standalone parameter type erased `.mock.calls`' element typing through `vi.spyOn`'s overloaded signature, leaving a destructured parameter implicitly `any`. Retyped the helper to accept `unknown[][]` directly instead of destructuring through the spy's own return type. No behavior change (the same 16/16 `real-server-port.test.ts` tests still pass). Given its own commit (`f265831`), not folded into FIX 3's commit, since it was only discovered after that commit had already landed — per this repo's own commit discipline (new commits over amends).

**FIX 4, closed — wording/comment only, no behavior change.** `deploy/serve-dashboard.mjs`'s tokenless-startup log line (and its adjacent comment) still said "promote requests will be sent unauthenticated" — accurate at A57 (when promote was the only gated write), stale since A59 widened server-side gating to nearly every mutation route. `createHttpApiClient` already attaches the token to every write call, not just promote (A59 PART 6) — only the WORDING was wrong. Updated both the `console.log` line (`serve-dashboard.mjs:148`, now "every dashboard write action -- not just promote -- will be sent unauthenticated") and its adjacent comment block, which described the identical stale "promote alone" scope and would otherwise have sat two lines above the newly-corrected wording, freshly contradicting it.

**FIX 5, closed — docs only.** DEPLOY.md's "Network binding" section documented `aart server`'s new loopback default in detail but never mentioned `aart worker`'s own `GET /health` listener (default 8787) is unaffected and still binds every interface — deliberate (A59 PART 3), not an oversight, but previously stated only in AMENDMENTS.md's own A59 text, not in DEPLOY.md itself. Added a one-line callout (`DEPLOY.md:536`) so an operator reading only DEPLOY.md doesn't over-generalize "D2a made `aart` bind loopback-only" to the worker too. **Also corrected in passing**: verified directly (`commands/process.ts`, `cli.ts` — zero matches for `health-port`/`healthPort`/`HEALTH_PORT`) that `aart worker` has no `--health-port` CLI flag today, so the callout doesn't point an operator at a knob that doesn't exist — the health port is only configurable at the `@aart/server`-package `WorkerConfig.healthPort` level, not through the CLI.

**FIX 6, closed — comment only, no behavior change.** `approvals.ts` (assignment now at `:93`, was `:86` before this fix's own comment pushed it down) assigns `authenticatedAs` unconditionally from `input.authenticatedAs`, so a tokenless re-decision of a previously token-decided task clears it to `undefined`. Intended — each decision is a fresh attribution, matching `ApprovalTask.authenticatedAs`'s own doc comment in `@aart/types` ("which credential authenticated... this task," describing the CURRENT decision, not a cumulative history) — but previously unstated at the call site itself, leaving a future reader likely to "fix" it into a merge/preserve. A seven-line intent comment added directly above the assignment (`approvals.ts:86-92`), ending with an explicit "do not fix this into ... ?? task.authenticatedAs" warning.

**Deviations flagged, not silent:**
1. FIX 1's brief said "reconcile the gating-matrix + ops-limits body-cap wording" — no pre-existing ops-limits body-cap wording was found to reconcile (verified by direct grep before writing). A new bullet was added instead of editing a nonexistent one. See FIX 1 above.
2. FIX 3's worker-side proving test goes through `@aart/server`'s own `startWorker`, not the full CLI composition root — consistent with A58's own already-documented, re-verified-still-true deviation for this exact subsystem (the `ServerPort.startWorker` healthPort-binding-risk reasoning is identical). See FIX 3 above.
3. FIX 5 also corrected a related, not-explicitly-briefed inaccuracy in passing (no `--health-port` CLI flag exists) — kept in scope since it sits immediately adjacent to the one line the fix touches and leaving it would have shipped a fresh internal contradiction two lines away.

**The exact new webhook body-cap DEPLOY.md wording** (Ops limits, new bullet, verbatim):

> - **Request body size caps (AMENDMENTS.md A59/A60).** Every route on
>   `aart server` has a hard cap on request body size — `Router.handle`'s own
>   `readBody` (`packages/server/src/http/router.ts`) rejects an over-cap
>   body `413` before JSON-parsing (or, for a gated route, before the request
>   is even authenticated) ever runs, via a `Content-Length` pre-check when
>   the header is present and honest, plus a running-total check during
>   accumulation otherwise (catches chunked transfer-encoding, or a client
>   that lies about/omits the header) — never a truly unbounded read. Three
>   tiers, by route:
>   1. **1MB** (`DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES`) — every control-plane route that
>      doesn't specify its own cap: trigger a run, decide an approval, record
>      a correction, create/run an eval suite, and similar small JSON
>      payloads.
>   2. **10MB** (`MAX_BUNDLE_INGEST_BYTES`) — `POST /bundles/ingest`/`POST
>      /bundles/plan`, sized for a real workflow closure bundle (100% JSON
>      text).
>   3. **25 MiB / 26,214,400 bytes** (`MAX_WEBHOOK_INGEST_BYTES`, AMENDMENTS.md
>      A60) — the three `/webhooks/*` routes, sized to (and slightly past)
>      GitHub's own documented ~25MB webhook payload ceiling. These are
>      EXTERNAL, operator-uncontrolled payloads, not small control-plane
>      JSON — GitHub does not retry a delivery it can't make, so this bound
>      has to cover the largest delivery GitHub could actually send, not a
>      typical one; a body between 1MB and this cap that would have 413'd
>      under tier 1 now reaches HMAC verification/intake normally.
>   If you front any route with your own reverse proxy, make sure it doesn't
>   impose a SMALLER cap than the tier that route actually needs.

Plus the gating-matrix "Open, always" row's cell rewritten in place (`DEPLOY.md:367`) — see git diff for the exact before/after.

**Gates, from clean, run TWICE in full** (`pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`): **232 files / 2425 tests, all green, byte-for-byte identical across both complete from-clean runs** (A59's own 232/2418 baseline + this pass's net +7 tests, 0 new files — FIX 1: +2; FIX 2: +2; FIX 3: +3, 2 server-side + 1 worker-side; FIXes 4-6: docs/comments only, 0 tests). The FIRST attempt at what would have been gate run 1 caught the FIX-3 typecheck bug described above (`typecheck:tests` failed, exit 2, before `lint:redaction`/`test` ever ran) — fixed, then the FULL clean-gate sequence was re-run from `pnpm run clean` onward and passed; that re-run is "run 1" below, matching this repo's own established discipline of never counting a partial/failed attempt toward the "run twice" requirement. `check:tsconfig-refs`: OK, the same 5 non-blocking informational notes as every prior session, both runs. `lint:redaction`: clean both runs — 225 reviewed suppressions, UNCHANGED from A59's own final count (this pass introduced zero new suppression-worthy call sites — every new log line goes through `logger.info(...)`, not a raw `console.*` call, the same reasoning that already exempted A59's own attribution-logging line; `serve-dashboard.mjs`'s one touched `console.log` call site is a pre-existing, already-reviewed call whose STRING content changed, not a new call site). Both full runs' logs swept for hidden failures (`grep -niE "error TS|FAIL|✗|UnhandledRejection|ELIFECYCLE"`) — zero matches either run; diffed both full run logs against each other (normalizing only PIDs/timestamps) — identical except the frontend `vite build` step's own reported duration (132ms vs 135ms), an unavoidable timing variance. The three touched/new test files (`server.test.ts`, `worker.test.ts`, `real-server-port.test.ts`) were additionally re-run together in isolation 3 further times beyond the two full-suite passes — 126/126 every time, zero flakiness. None of this pass's own additions involve the genuine cross-process concurrency A58's own migration-race test does (the highest-timing-sensitivity new addition here is a real HTTP fetch carrying a ~2MB body, plus ordinary port bind/close cycles), so this level of repetition was judged sufficient rather than the heavier concurrent-iteration protocol A58/A59 reserved specifically for that one test.

**`MAX_WEBHOOK_INGEST_BYTES` final value: 25 MiB (26,214,400 bytes)**, as the briefing's own suggested number and as specified above — confirmed directly against GitHub's own documentation (`docs.github.com/en/webhooks/webhook-events-and-payloads`: *"Payloads are capped at 25 MB"*) before choosing it, specifically to exceed that stated ceiling under either the decimal-MB or binary-MiB reading of "25 MB."

**Every new/changed refusal carries its remedy, verified directly:** FIX 1's `413` (an over-cap webhook body) reuses `readBody`'s existing `BodyTooLargeError` message plus `Router.handle`'s existing "split the payload into something smaller, or remove unused content, and retry" remedy, unchanged mechanism — just a larger threshold now applies to these three routes specifically. FIX 2's `401` (`deployTokenNext` supplied with no primary configured) resolves to the SAME "no AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN configured" remedy `requireDeployToken` already gives for the fully-unconfigured case — byte-identical text, asserted directly by the FIX 2 test itself (`server.test.ts:1003`) — never a new or different message implying a half-configured state exists.

**No `ServerConfig`/`WorkerConfig` field was added by this pass** (FIX 1 added a constant, not a config field; FIX 2/3 are behavior/logging fixes to existing fields) — the STANDING IMPERATIVE (every new config field needs composition-root wiring + a from-CLI-entry test) does not apply here; noted explicitly rather than silently skipped.

**Commits (local only, none pushed), one per fix plus one self-caught correction, all on `d2a/auth-middleware` on top of A59's own final HEAD `0858768`:** `d8164d0` (FIX 1), `219e826` (FIX 2), `342437a` (FIX 3), `f265831` (FIX 3's own typecheck correction), `d3b3ece` (FIX 4), `8fe9a67` (FIX 5), `530de0b` (FIX 6).

## 2026-07-12 — V1 Slice 1: the event log foundation

### A61 — V1 Slice 1: the real event log — a 17th `AartStore` member (`EventLogStore`), a 4th sqlite migration, ~20 real write sites across CLI/MCP/server, `GET /events`, and a self-caught `workflow.approved` gap closed in the same change

John-ratified, 2026-07-12, on the D2+V1 wave and the "build the real event log" fork. Built in an isolated git worktree off `main`@`3f62a73`, branch `v1/event-log`. This is the activity-feed + live-updates spine: every real place a workflow/run/approval/deployment/eval fact becomes true now appends a durable, best-effort `EventLogEntry`, readable back via `GET /events`.

**PART 1 — `EventLogStore`, the 17th `AartStore` member.** New `packages/types/src/event-log.ts`: `EventLogEntrySchema` (zod) + `EventLogEntry` — `{ id, type, occurredAt, summary }` required, `{ workflowId?, workflowVersion?, runId?, deploymentId?, environmentId?, approvalTaskId?, actor? }` all optional and independent. `type` deliberately kept a plain `z.string()`, not a closed enum — this session's own taxonomy (below) is the value set it ships with, not a schema-level constraint a Wave-2 addition (`deployment.pushed`, explicitly deferred below) would need another amendment to extend.

`packages/store/src/types.ts`: new `EventLogStore { append(entry): Promise<void>; list(filter?: {since?, limit?}): Promise<EventLogEntry[]> }`, added as `events` on `AartStore` — the header's own "16 members" counted-fact comment (and its two sibling references to the same count, on the `job_queue`/`idempotencyLedger` "not one of the 16" notes and the interface's own closing comment) updated to 17 throughout; `conformance.ts`'s `jobQueue` describe label and `adapters/sqlite/schema.ts` + `stores/simple-stores.ts`'s own identical "not one of AartStore's 16 members" comments swept to 17 too, for internal consistency (a small superset of what the brief asked for verbatim — trivial, zero-risk comment edits, flagged here rather than silently done).

fs adapter (`adapters/fs/events.ts`, new `FsEventLogStore`): mirrors `signals.ts`'s pattern exactly — one file per entry (`<occurredAt>__<id>.json`), scan-based `list()` with newest-first sort + `since`/`limit` applied on top, and — the load-bearing part of "mirror signals.ts" — **deliberately NOT staged by `transact()`**, the same non-atomic-gap precedent `SignalStore` established (constructor takes only `dir`, no `staging` param). `AartStore.transact()`'s own doc comment (types.ts) extended with a new paragraph naming `tx.events` as a second non-staged member, alongside the existing `tx.signals` one, with the reasoning: every real write site in this session appends its event OUTSIDE any `store.transact()` call, so there is no caller today relying on an event append rolling back with a failed transaction.

sqlite adapter: new migration `0004_events_table` (`adapters/sqlite/migrations.ts`) — this adapter's FIRST migration that adds a whole TABLE rather than a column on an existing one. `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`/`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` are naturally idempotent (unlike `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`, which SQLite gives no `IF NOT EXISTS` for — 0002/0003's own reason for their try/catch "duplicate column name" tolerance), so 0004 does NOT need that dance. `down()` mirrors `0001_init`'s own `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS` idiom, not 0002/0003's `DROP COLUMN`. `events` is NOT added to `SQLITE_SCHEMA_STATEMENTS` (schema.ts) — same "0001's baseline DDL stays exactly as it always was" precedent 0002/0003 established, so a fresh 0001→0004 sequence and an upgraded pre-existing database converge on one identical shape. `stores/events.ts` (new `SqliteEventLogStore`): unlike the fs adapter, this member IS staged/transactional (mirrors `SqliteSignalStore`'s own documented reasoning — sqlite has a REAL `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`, so there's no fs-specific gap to preserve here; only the fs side needed the SignalStore-style carve-out). Optional-field row mapping uses this codebase's own established `row.field ?? undefined` idiom (verified directly against `ApprovalTaskRow.authenticated_as` -> `authenticatedAs`, not invented), so an omitted correlation field round-trips as genuinely absent under `toEqual`, never a stray populated value.

`conformance.ts`: new `events` describe block — append+list round-trip (all fields set, and all correlation fields omitted), newest-first ordering independent of append order, `since` filtering, `limit` (newest-N), and `since`+`limit` combined (limit applies AFTER the since filter). Runs against both adapters automatically (the shared suite's own existing wiring, no adapter-specific test file changes needed).

`packages/store/src/event-log.ts` (new): the shared write path every real call site in PART 2 goes through — `recordEvent(store, input, now?)` (best-effort, try/catch-wrapped `store.events.append`, silently swallowed on failure, deliberately no logging — mirrors `run-lifecycle.ts`'s own `runOnRunTerminal` precedent for an identically-scoped best-effort hook) and `recordRunTerminalEvent(store, runId, now?)` (re-reads the just-persisted `RunRecord` by id — `onRunTerminal`'s own signature only carries a `runId` — and maps `completed`/`failed`/`cancelled` to the matching event type; a defensive outer try/catch of its own, since the `store.runs.get` read itself sits outside `recordEvent`'s try/catch). Exported from `@aart/store`'s index.

**Migration-count ripple, every site found and updated:** `adapters/sqlite/sqlite-store.test.ts` (watermark assertion + comment, two places), `adapters/sqlite/migrations.test.ts` (see the trap fix below, plus a new `0004_events_table` describe block), `e2e/migration-race.e2e.test.ts` + `e2e/migration-race-worker.mjs` (watermark 3→4, new `hasEventsTable` check via `PRAGMA table_info(events)` — empty-not-error on a nonexistent table, so a non-empty result is a genuine existence proof — plus a real `store.events.append` call mirroring the existing `workflows.put` one).

**The trap this session's own brief warned about, hit and closed for real:** `migrations.test.ts`'s pre-existing "down() reverts 0003" test drove `openSqliteStore(dbPath)` (the default — now runs 0001→0004) into a runner built from `ALL_SQLITE_MIGRATIONS` sitting at watermark 4. `MigrationRunner.down()` reverts only the migration currently AT the watermark (its own documented contract, the exact shape A58 first caught) — unfixed, this test would have silently started reverting 0004 (drop the `events` TABLE) instead of 0003 (drop the `authenticated_as` COLUMN) it actually means to exercise, while the assertion `expect(await runner.currentVersion()).toBe(3)` would itself have failed loudly first (caught in this session's own store-package test run before any silent-wrong-revert could hide). Rescoped to an explicit `[0001, 0002, 0003]` migration list (a new `migrationsThrough0003` helper), mirroring the "down() reverts 0002" test's own established pattern one describe block up — and its two sibling tests in the same 0003 describe block, which were ALSO (less severely — they were about `up()`, not `down()`) scoped to `ALL_SQLITE_MIGRATIONS`, rescoped identically for consistency even though only the `down()` test would have produced a wrong-not-just-failing result.

**PART 2 — event-write instrumentation, every site.** Principle applied uniformly: the event write is colocated with the real domain write it documents, using whatever conditionality that write already has — no new dedup/guard logic invented beyond selecting which taxonomy value applies to the outcome. Every write goes through `recordEvent`/`recordRunTerminalEvent` (never `store.events.append` directly), so the best-effort contract is enforced once, not re-implemented at each site.

- `workflow.version_registered` — `registerWorkflowHandler` (`packages/mcp/src/handlers/authoring.ts`).
- `workflow.validated` (gate==="validate" only) + `workflow.gate_passed`/`workflow.gate_failed` (status-dependent, any gate) — `applyGateResult` (`packages/mcp/src/handlers/governance.ts`), the shared path `validateWorkflowHandler`/`runWorkflowHandler`'s readiness writer/`runEvalHandler`'s evals writer/`applyVersionReviewDecision` (humanReview/riskReview, the mcp `aart_approve` path) all route through — one instrumentation point covers all five gates for every MCP/CLI-reachable writer.
- `workflow.gate_passed`/`workflow.gate_failed` (humanReview/riskReview) + `approval.decided` — `decideApprovalTask` (`packages/server/src/approvals.ts`), this package's OWN copy of the identical writes, required by package layering (`@aart/server` cannot import `@aart/mcp` — confirmed directly via package.json before writing this, not assumed).
- `workflow.approved` — `promoteWorkflowHandler` (mcp/governance.ts, gate-based reconciliation) + `approveOrDeprecateWorkflow`'s `"approve"` action (server/workflow-actions.ts) + **`applyGateResult` and `decideApprovalTask` themselves** (see the self-caught gap below — NOT in the original write-site list, added after this session's own test caught the omission). `workflow.deprecated` — `approveOrDeprecateWorkflow`'s `"deprecate"` action.
- `run.started` — `runWorkflowHandler` (mcp/handlers/execution.ts). `triggerWorkflowHandler` (mcp/handlers/deployment.ts) needs NO separate instrumentation — it calls `runWorkflowHandler` internally (three-clients-same-function), so instrumenting the callee alone covers both; verified by reading the call graph directly rather than instrumenting both and risking a double-emit.
- `run.completed`/`run.failed`/`run.cancelled` — see RISK 1 below.
- `approval.requested` — `requestApprovalHandler` (mcp/governance.ts).
- `approval.decided` — ALSO `approveHandler` (mcp/governance.ts) — see the self-caught gap below, same story as `workflow.approved`.
- `correction.recorded` — `recordCorrectionHandler` (mcp/governance.ts).
- `eval.suite_created` — `createEvalSuite`; `eval.run_completed` — `runEvalSuiteForWorkflow` (both `packages/server/src/evals.ts` — NOT an mcp file; verified directly via grep before assuming otherwise, since the taxonomy names read like mcp handlers).
- `deployment.created` — `deployWorkflowHandler` (mcp/handlers/deployment.ts, local deploy only).
- `deployment.promoted` — `promoteWorkflowVersionToEnvironment` (server/promotion.ts).
- `deployment.pushed` — deliberately NOT added. `deployToRemoteHandler` (mcp/handlers/deployment.ts) was never touched — confirmed via `git diff` against `main` after the fact: only the import line and `deployWorkflowHandler`'s own body changed in that file.

**Two self-caught gaps, fixed in the same change, not left for a fix pass:** `applyGateResult` and `decideApprovalTask` both recompute and write `approval` UNCONDITIONALLY on every call (not just when a caller explicitly "promotes") — whichever gate happens to be the LAST one a trust mode requires can flip a workflow from `draft` to `approved` right there, as a side effect of a human's `aart_approve`/dashboard decision or an evidence-based gate writer, with no separate `promoteWorkflowHandler`/`approveOrDeprecateWorkflow` call ever happening. Neither was in this session's own briefed write-site list (which named only the two explicit-promotion functions for `workflow.approved`). This session's OWN `governance.test.ts` addition ("emits approval.decided, workflow.gate_passed, and workflow.approved for a workflow-version review decision that completes approval") caught the missing event before it shipped — the test failed against the as-briefed implementation, the gap was diagnosed, and both `applyGateResult` and its server-side twin `decideApprovalTask` were fixed in this same commit, each gated identically (`approval !== workflow.approval && approval === "approved"`). Relatedly, `approveHandler` (mcp) is a SECOND real `approval.decided` entry point never named in the brief — verified directly that CLI's `aart approve` (`commands/governance.ts`) and the MCP tool `aart_approve` both dispatch to it directly, never through `decideApprovalTask` (which backs only the dashboard's separate HTTP route) — omitting it would have silently dropped every CLI/MCP-decided approval from the event log. Both gaps are exactly the missed-composition-root failure shape this repo's own standing imperative (five prior instances: A48/A53/A57/A58) exists to catch, just surfacing for an event-taxonomy completeness question rather than a config-field wiring one.

**RISK 1 — run.completed/failed/cancelled, the three real sites, closed with the composition-root enumeration the brief demanded be done FIRST.** Grepped every `createEngine(` call site across the whole workspace before writing any hook code: exactly **two** real composition roots exist — `packages/mcp/src/real-context.ts`'s `createRealEngine` and `packages/familiarity-evals/src/real-checks.ts`'s `createRealRunSuccessFn`. (`packages/cli`/`packages/server` never independently construct one — confirmed by tracing `cli-context.ts` -> `createRealAartContextWithEngine` -> the SAME `createRealEngine` instance threaded into `createRealServerPort` -> `createRealEngineBoundary`, one `Engine`, shared by every real consumer.) Both composition roots already had `onRunTerminal: (runId) => closeBrowserSession(runId)` wired from a prior session; both now ALSO call the new `recordRunTerminalEvent(store, runId)` — ordered FIRST, `closeBrowserSession` second, so a browser-cleanup failure (already swallowed either way by `run-lifecycle.ts`'s own outer `runOnRunTerminal` catch) can never suppress the event write by throwing before it runs, while `recordRunTerminalEvent` itself never throws (its own internal try/catch) so this ordering costs `closeBrowserSession` nothing.

Two cheap-but-load-bearing verifications the brief asked for, done BEFORE writing the hook, both confirmed by reading the code directly:
1. **Does `cancelRun` share `onRunTerminal`?** Yes — `run-lifecycle.ts`'s `cancelRun` calls the exact same `runOnRunTerminal(config, redacted.runId)` helper `finalizeTerminal` does, at its own terminal-write call site (`:350`). No separate instrumentation needed for explicit cancellation; proven by a dedicated test (`engine.cancelRun(...)` called directly, asserting `run.cancelled` appears).
2. **Does CLI's `registerCommand` call `registerWorkflowHandler` directly?** Yes — `packages/cli/src/commands/authoring.ts:54`: `const result = await registerWorkflowHandler(cli.aart, { workflow: source })`, the literal same function object the MCP tool dispatches to. Same file: `runCommand` calls `runWorkflowHandler` directly too, confirming one instrumentation point in `execution.ts` covers `aart run` and `aart_run_workflow` both.

Coverage proof, mirroring A48's own four-entry-point discipline, one test each (all new):
- **CLI `aart run` / MCP `aart_run_workflow`** (same function, `real-context.test.ts`): a completed run emits `run.completed`; a `flow.fail` run emits `run.failed`, not `run.completed`; `engine.cancelRun(...)` emits `run.cancelled` (proving point 1 above empirically, not just by code-reading).
- **Trigger-fired via server + worker-claimed** (`real-context.test.ts`, new describe block): `createRealEngineBoundary(store, createRealEngine(...))` — the EXACT composition `@aart/cli`'s `real-server-port.ts` performs, not a re-implemented approximation — `startRun` then `executeClaimedRun` emits `run.completed`.
- **Worker-reclaimed** (`packages/server/src/worker/worker.test.ts`, extending the existing reclaim-exhausted test): `runReclaimSweep` writes `store.runs.put` directly on lease-expiry-past-budget, bypassing `@aart/engine` entirely — the `onRunTerminal` fix above cannot see this path at all, so it needed its OWN explicit `recordEvent` call (`packages/server/src/worker/reclaim.ts`) — proven by the extended test asserting `run.failed` after the sweep exhausts the bounded reclaim count.
- `packages/familiarity-evals/src/real-checks.ts`'s second composition root got its own new test file (`real-checks.test.ts` — none existed before; `run-success.test.ts` only covers the lightweight reference checker, never this real-engine one) proving `run.completed`/`run.failed` independently, since this Engine instance is never reachable through the mcp/cli/server stack at all.

**PART 3 — `GET /events`.** Registered in `http/server.ts`'s existing GET block, immediately after `/rejected-triggers`, alongside `/deployments`/`/approvals` — `store.events.list({ since: ctx.query.get("since") ?? undefined, limit })`, `limit` parsed defensively (`Number.isFinite` guard against a garbage query value, since `Array.prototype.slice`/sqlite `LIMIT` both do something confusing with a bare `NaN`) but otherwise unvalidated, matching this route family's own established loose-parsing idiom (`status`/`workflowId`/etc.'s `?? undefined` pattern, no schema validation layer). Deliberately UNGATED — no `auth` route option, matching every sibling GET route's default-open posture (only `/runs`+`/runs/:id` are being gated, a parallel, different-routes change per this session's file-ownership boundary, D2b/Dev B's to make). Test proves this explicitly: a server started WITH a configured `deployToken` still serves `GET /events` with no `Authorization` header.

**Gates, from clean, run TWICE in full** (`pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`): **234 files / 2474 tests, all green, byte-for-byte identical across both complete from-clean runs** (A60's own 232/2425 baseline + this session's net +49 tests, +2 new test files — `packages/familiarity-evals/src/real-checks.test.ts`, none previously existed for that module; `packages/types/src/event-log.test.ts`, the new schema's own round-trip suite). `check:tsconfig-refs`: OK, the same 5 non-blocking informational notes as every prior session, both runs. `lint:redaction`: clean both runs — **225 reviewed suppressions, UNCHANGED from A60's own final count** — this session introduced zero new suppression-worthy call sites (every event write goes through `recordEvent`/`store.events.append`, never a raw `console.*` call the lint's pattern matches at all). Both full run logs swept for hidden failures (`grep -niE "error TS|FAIL|✗|UnhandledRejection|ELIFECYCLE"`) — zero matches either run. Given `migration-race.e2e.test.ts` spawns genuinely concurrent OS processes (this repo's own highest flakiness-risk test class, A58's precedent) and this session added a 4th migration + a new `hasEventsTable` check to it, it was ALSO run in isolation 3 additional times beyond the two full-suite passes — clean every time (24 further concurrent-race iterations, 48 further process spawns, zero flakiness).

**Every event summary verified to never embed a resolved secret value** (this session's own explicit verification requirement): every `summary:` string constructed across all ~20 write sites interpolates only identifiers (workflow/run/deployment/environment/task ids, names, statuses, counts, reviewer names) — never a correction's `observed`/`corrected` payload, a resumed wait's payload, or anything reaching through `ctx.resolveSecret`/`secrets.*`. Confirmed structurally by the unchanged 225-suppression `lint:redaction` count above, not just by inspection.

**File-ownership boundary respected, confirmed directly via `git diff --stat main...HEAD`** after the fact, not just by discipline while writing: zero diff against `packages/mcp/src/tools/*`, `packages/mcp/src/handlers/index.ts`, and `packages/cli/src/cli.ts`; `packages/mcp/src/handlers/deployment.ts`'s own diff touches only the import line and `deployWorkflowHandler`'s body — `deployToRemoteHandler` untouched.

The two self-caught `workflow.approved`/`approval.decided` gaps above (`applyGateResult`, `decideApprovalTask`, `approveHandler`) — additions beyond the brief's own write-site list, kept in scope since leaving them out would have shipped a known, test-demonstrated silent gap in the exact bug class this session's own standing imperative names.

The "16 members" -> "17 members" comment sweep touched two files beyond `packages/store/src/types.ts` (`conformance.ts`'s `jobQueue` describe label, `schema.ts`/`stores/simple-stores.ts`'s own duplicate "not one of the 16" comments) — trivial, zero-behavior-change text edits for internal consistency within a package this session already owns; `packages/server/src/triggers/intake.ts`'s own unrelated "16 members" mention (about a completely different concern, the trigger dedupe-key ledger) was deliberately left untouched — out of scope, not silently missed.
