## 2026-07-12 — D2b Wave 1 (remote reads)

### A62 — D2b Wave 1: the READ half of "see a deployed server" — a shared `fetchFromRemote` client, four new `aart_remote_*` MCP tools + CLI mirrors, `GET /runs`+`GET /runs/:id` conditionally deploy-token-gated, dashboard reads carry the token, full redaction-lint closure on the new surface

**NUMBERING COORDINATION NOTE, read this first if merging:** this branch (`d2b/remote-reads`) forked from `main`@`3f62a73` (A60's own final state) at the same time a concurrent sibling branch (Dev A, building the V1 event log) forked from the identical commit. Both branches independently see A60 as the last entry and both may have picked `A61` as "the next free number" — check for a collision at merge time and renumber whichever entry lands second, rather than silently overwriting one.

**Context.** John-ratified 2026-07-12, alongside the D2+V1 wave and the "gate the run-read routes" fork specifically. D2a (A59/A60) closed the write-mutation trust-boundary gap; D1 (A56/A57) shipped `aart push`/`aart_deploy` — an authoring agent can PUSH a workflow to a remote `aart server`, but until this session had no way to SEE what actually happened once it got there: no visibility into what's live, why, or how a deployed run went. D2b's product framing: "let an authoring agent SEE a deployed server so it can debug/improve deployed workflows." This session is Wave 1 — the READ half. The WRITE-against-remote half (an `aart_remote_approve` tool, approving a workflow version directly against a remote's own approval surface) is explicitly DEFERRED to Wave 2, not built here.

**Verified foundation, confirmed directly before writing any code (not assumed from the brief alone):** `RemotesPort` (`packages/mcp/src/types.ts` ~322-327) gives `list`/`get`/`resolveToken` over `remotes.json`, already wired on `AartContext` since D1. `deployToRemoteHandler` (`packages/mcp/src/handlers/deployment.ts`) had its own inline authed `fetch` — the shape every new tool needed, just never factored out. D2b needed ZERO new server routes — every one of the six real routes these tools read (`GET /workflows/:id`, `/deployments`, `/runs`, `/runs/:id`, `/approvals`, `/environments`) already existed and already served real data.

**PART 1 — the shared remote client (new `packages/mcp/src/remote-client.ts`).** `fetchFromRemote(remoteEntry, path, {method?, body?, token?})` — mirrors `deployToRemoteHandler`'s own pre-existing inline fetch byte-for-byte (same header construction, same "parse JSON, tolerate a malformed/absent body," same "never throw — a network failure is a returned result" discipline). A plain exported function, not a `ctx` port — no stub-swap value, matching the `cleartextTokenWarning` precedent (a small pure directly-exported shared function, not a DI seam). Attaches the resolved bearer token on EVERY call, GET and POST alike — harmless against a remote whose routes are still open, and forward-compatible with the run-read gating Part 3 adds. Three more small shared helpers moved/added alongside it: `isRecord` (moved from `deployment.ts`), `describeUnreachableRemote`/`remoteNotFoundError` (extracted verbatim from `deployToRemoteHandler`'s own pre-existing inline strings, now shared so the four new tools give byte-identical remedies for the identical failure modes), `remoteErrorMessage` (generalizes the inline `responseBody["error"]` extraction). `deployToRemoteHandler` itself migrated onto `fetchFromRemote` — verified byte-for-byte behavior preservation: all 30 pre-existing tests in `deployment.test.ts` pass UNMODIFIED, including the A57 fix-pass spread-ordering test (`response.body` spread FIRST, `ok`/`remote`/`plan` set AFTER — an untrusted remote must never override this handler's own canonical verdict) and the exact `"Could not reach remote..."`/`"Remote ... refused..."` error strings those tests assert against.

**PART 2 — four new READ tools (new `packages/mcp/src/handlers/remote-observability.ts`), uniform `aart_remote_*` prefix:**

- **`aart_remote_status {workflowId, remote?}`** — local-vs-remote drift, one named remote or every configured remote (`ctx.remotes.list()`) when omitted. Compares local `ctx.store.workflows.getLatest(workflowId)` against remote `GET /workflows/:id` + `GET /deployments` (filtered CLIENT-SIDE — `GET /deployments` ignores query params entirely, confirmed directly against `server.ts`). Diffs gates/approval FIELD-BY-FIELD when version strings match (`diffApprovalAndGates`, `remote-observability.ts:56-66`) — a remote can show the SAME version with DIFFERENT gate state (re-approved locally after the same version was already pushed), and a bare version-equality check would hide that. An unreachable remote gets its OWN `{remote, reachable:false, error}` row (`statusForOneRemote`, `remote-observability.ts:81-127`) — proven directly: a multi-remote test with one unreachable (`http://localhost:1`) and one live remote asserts the live one's row is unaffected.
- **`aart_remote_why {remote, workflowId}`** (both required) — what's live and why. Resolves the remote's own configured environment NAME to an id via `GET /environments` (`remote-observability.ts:211-215`), scopes `GET /deployments` to `(workflowId, environmentId)` client-side, then calls the REAL `findCurrentVersion` (not a re-derived heuristic — see Part 1's own deviation below) to determine which version is actually live. Gates/approval come from `GET /workflows/:id?version=<live>`; who-approved comes from `GET /approvals` decoded via `ctx.governance.decodeWorkflowVersionApprovalSubject` (the SAME decode `/approvals/:id/decision` itself uses) filtered to `(workflowId, liveVersion)`, surfacing `{gate, reviewer, authenticatedAs, decidedAt, status}` — an explicit ALLOWLIST that deliberately excludes `ApprovalTask.decision` (arbitrary human/agent-authored payload). `whoPushed`/`whoPromoted` are NOT tracked by this system anywhere — reported `null` with an explicit `attributionNote`, never guessed.
- **`aart_remote_runs {remote, status?}`** — compact run summaries (`runId`/`workflowId`/`workflowVersion`/`status`/`startedAt`/`updatedAt`/`headline`), NEVER full traces — `status` maps directly to `GET /runs`'s own server-side `?status=` filter. `headline` (`deriveHeadline`, `remote-observability.ts:352-358`) is a CHEAP LOCAL derivation from `status`/`error` (truncated to 140 chars for a failed run) — deliberately NOT a `ctx.evidence.modelFacingReport` render; that seam is reserved for the next tool, and rendering N full reports for a list call would defeat "compact summary."
- **`aart_remote_run {remote, runId, format?}`** — one run's full evidence report. **THE REDACTION SEAM.** `GET /runs/:id` returns a raw `RunRecord`; this handler routes it through `ctx.evidence.modelFacingReport(body.run)`/`markdownReport(body.run)` — the EXACT SAME real chokepoint (`real-context.ts`'s `createReportRenderers(redactRecord)`) `aart_get_report`'s own `getReportHandler` (`execution.ts:99-106`) already uses for a LOCAL run, never a second way to render one. Verified directly (`remote-observability.test.ts`, "routes the remote-fetched RunRecord through ctx.evidence.modelFacingReport" — the test computes the expected report independently via the SAME `ctx.evidence.modelFacingReport(run)` call and deep-equals it against the tool's own return, PLUS asserts the returned `report` carries none of `RunRecord`'s own distinguishing raw fields — `trace`/`runId`/`snapshot` — which a raw pass-through would have kept and a real `ModelFacingReport` render never does). **Per the brief's own explicit instruction, this is a CONSISTENCY/inheritance move, not new protection** — `@aart/evidence`'s own `redact.ts` doc comment already establishes the render-time scrub is defense-in-depth (resolved secret VALUES are never persisted onto a `RunRecord` in the first place); reading a remote run through this seam inherits the blocking `lint:redaction` CI gate the local path already had, it does not add a NEW guarantee beyond it — `tools/definitions.ts`'s own description for this tool is worded to avoid overclaiming this (states what it does — "the same rendering aart_get_report gives" — never "this redacts secrets").

CLI parity (built, not deferred, per the brief): `aart remote-status/remote-why/remote-runs/remote-run` — hyphenated (bare `aart run`/`runs`/`status` are already taken; `cli.ts`'s own `case "run"` dispatches to the LOCAL `runCommand`, and `aart remote <subcommand>` is already reserved for `remotes.json` CRUD with zero network calls, a meaningfully different operation). Thin wrappers over the same handler functions (three-clients precedent, new `packages/cli/src/commands/remote-observability.ts`).

**PART 3 — run-read gating (John's own ratified fork).** `GET /runs` and `GET /runs/:id` (`packages/server/src/http/server.ts`) now carry `{ auth: (ctx) => requireDeployTokenIfConfigured(config, ctx, "read run data") }` — the SAME conditional mechanism (unconfigured stays open, configured requires the valid Bearer) every mutation route already used; the ONLY two GET routes on this server carrying an `auth` option, proven directly (a new server.test.ts test fetches every other GET route with `deployToken` configured and asserts none of them 401). Rationale: D2b makes a run's full trace/inputs/outputs agent-discoverable from a REMOTE caller for the first time (`aart_remote_run`) — the same "who can reach this" trust-boundary reasoning D2a's own write-gating already applied to mutation now applies to this specific read. `fetchFromRemote` (Part 1) already attaches a resolved token on every call including GETs, so `aart_remote_runs`/`aart_remote_run` keep working against a gated server with no changes of their own.

Because these two routes can now 401, the DASHBOARD's own `listRuns`/`getRun` (`packages/dashboard/src/api-client.ts`) needed the same token attached — checked directly: `getJson` had NO `extraHeaders` param at all (unlike its `postJson`/`postJsonOrThrow` siblings, which have carried one since A57) — added one, applied to both methods (`getRun`'s own raw `fetch` too, since it bypasses `getJson` for its 404-means-undefined convention). The token variable itself was renamed `writeAuthHeaders` -> `deployAuthHeaders` (A59's own name, when it covered writes only) since it now also covers two reads — same value/construction, name-only change, both "renamed from" doc comments deliberately preserve the OLD name for historical clarity.

**PART 4 — MCP registration closure (22 -> 26 tools).** All four tools added to the full trio: `TOOL_NAMES`/`TOOL_TIERS` (extended tier)/`NEXT_TABLE` (one success/failure prose pair each) in `response.ts`; `DESCRIPTIONS`/`inputSchemas` in `definitions.ts`; `HANDLERS` in `handlers/index.ts`; a new `REMOTE_GATED_TOOLS` set in `tools/server.ts` (`isToolRegistered`, mirroring `ENVIRONMENT_GATED_TOOLS`/`EVAL_SUITE_GATED_TOOLS`'s own list-construction filtering shape) — the four tools appear only once `ctx.remotes.list()` is non-empty. `aart_deploy` deliberately NOT added to this gate (unlike the four reads, which need an existing remote to read FROM, `aart_deploy` is how a caller configures their FIRST remote's content — gating it on one already existing would be circular; this was already true pre-D2b and is now stated explicitly in `tools/server.ts`'s own module doc comment, which previously claimed ALL 7 non-Environment/EvalSuite-gated extended tools had "no data-existence precondition," no longer true now that 4 of the other 7 do).

**Deviations, flagged, not silent:**

1. **`findCurrentVersion` (`packages/server/src/bundle/plan.ts`) was exported from its own file but never re-exported through `@aart/server`'s public `index.ts` barrel** — the brief's own "already a transitive dep" claim is true for the PACKAGE, not for this specific function's reachability: that package's `package.json` `exports` map declares only `"."`, and `moduleResolution: NodeNext` (`tsconfig.base.json`) enforces that strictly — a deep `@aart/server/dist/bundle/plan.js` subpath import is rejected at both typecheck and runtime, with no precedent anywhere in this repo for bypassing a package's declared export surface (verified: zero deep-import matches across every `packages/*/src/*.ts` file). Given the brief's own explicit, load-bearing instruction ("do NOT re-derive a heuristic" — re-deriving `findCurrentVersion`'s tie-break locally would have recreated exactly the drift risk that instruction exists to prevent), the smallest correct fix was a single additive re-export line in `packages/server/src/index.ts` (`export { findCurrentVersion } from "./bundle/plan.js";`) rather than a local reimplementation — a narrow exception to this session's own "packages/server ONLY the /runs + /runs/:id auth gating" file-ownership boundary, purely additive (zero behavior change to anything already exported), and does not touch any file Dev A owns.
2. **`aart_remote_why` needs `findCurrentVersion`'s winning ROW (for `bundleHash`/`pushedAt`), not just the version string it returns** — `pickLiveDeploymentRow` (`remote-observability.ts:191-193`) mirrors `findCurrentVersion`'s own tie-break EXACTLY (same comparator: most-recently-created active row, `id` as the secondary sort key), narrowed to rows already matching the version `findCurrentVersion` picked. This is a second-order "which of possibly-several rows sharing that exact version" selection (only reachable if the same version was pushed more than once into the same environment) — not a re-derivation of "which VERSION is live" itself, which always goes through the real function first.
3. **AUTHORING.md's `--failed` shorthand from the brief's own prose became `--status failed`** — the brief's informal "`aart remote-runs --failed`" phrasing has no literal CLI-flag equivalent in `aart_remote_runs`'s own schema (`status?: RunStatus`, a 6-value enum, no boolean shortcut) — implemented/documented as `aart remote-runs <remote> --status failed`, matching this codebase's own established `--status <value>` convention elsewhere (no existing CLI surface has an ad hoc boolean shortcut for one enum value) rather than inventing a new one-off flag shape.
4. **A genuine, pre-existing, UNRELATED doc staleness noticed in passing, deliberately NOT fixed here:** `AUTHORING.md` part (c)'s "you should see 16 `aart_*` tools" checkpoint (verified against a truly fresh project — zero Environments/EvalSuites/remotes) is stale by one tool even before this session — `aart_deploy` (D1, A56) registers unconditionally and should already make that count 17, but the enumerated 16-name list predates `aart_deploy`'s own addition (that paragraph's own "Merge-safe as of AMENDMENTS.md A55" marker is BEFORE A56). D2b's own remote-gated tools don't change this specific count (they stay absent with zero remotes, by design) — the staleness is real but orthogonal to this session's scope, so left uncorrected rather than silently expanded into a re-verification this session didn't budget for; flagged via a spawned follow-up task instead.

**Tests, summarized (233 files / 2477 tests total, net +44 over this session's own 233/2433 starting point after Parts 1-3's code landed and closure-test counts were fixed):**
- `packages/mcp/src/handlers/remote-observability.test.ts` (new, 25 tests) — against a REAL `@aart/server` instance standing in for the remote (not hand-mocked; these tools read six different real routes with real response shapes their handlers actually parse). Covers every branch enumerated in Part 2 above, plus a real bug this test file's own construction surfaced: the local `writeRemote` test helper (mirrored from `deployment.test.ts`'s own, which never needed two remotes in one root) was silently OVERWRITING `remotes.json` on a second call instead of merging — caught by the multi-remote `aart_remote_status` test, fixed to merge.
- `packages/mcp/src/tools/server.test.ts` (+4) — `REMOTE_GATED_TOOLS` availability: absent with 0 remotes, present with >=1, `isToolRegistered`/`listTools` agreement both ways, `aart_deploy`'s own deliberate non-gating. Also fixed 1 pre-existing test (`CORE_MINUS_APPROVE`'s exclude list) that would otherwise have misclassified the 4 new extended tools as core.
- `packages/server/src/http/server.test.ts` (+5) — `GET /runs`/`GET /runs/:id`: 401 without token when configured, 200 with a correct Bearer, 401 with a wrong one, 200 unconfigured (byte-identical to pre-D2b), and every OTHER GET route proven to stay open even when `deployToken` is configured (the "only these two" claim, proven, not just asserted in a comment).
- `packages/dashboard/src/api-client.test.ts` (+3) — `listRuns`/`getRun` round-trip against a token-gated real server (proves `getJson`'s new `extraHeaders` is genuinely wired, not just accepted and ignored), `getRun`'s 404-vs-401 distinction holds under gating, and the negative (no token -> 401) case — mirroring D2a's own established "positive round-trip + data-driven negative" pattern for the two newly-gated reads.
- `packages/cli/src/cli.test.ts` (+7) — thin-wrapper smoke coverage for all four new commands against a real remote server (5 tests); caught and fixed a 6th test that wrongly assumed uniform `ok:false` across all four commands, split into 2 corrected tests (`aart_remote_status` deliberately returns `ok:true` with a per-row failure for an unconfigured remote, since it can iterate multiple remotes and one bad name shouldn't hide the others' real status — the OTHER three fail the whole call, since they operate on exactly one named remote).
- Response-affordance/tool-definition closure tests (`response.test.ts`, `tools/definitions.test.ts`) updated from the pre-D2b 22-tool/12-extended counts to 26/16 — generic per-tool sweeps (`computeNext` for every tool x 2 outcomes; description-length floor; input-schema presence) needed no new per-tool entries, only the hardcoded totals.

**Redaction-lint closure (the brief's own explicit requirement — "every new finding individually justified").** Migrating `deployToRemoteHandler` onto `fetchFromRemote` left 5 EXISTING suppression entries stale (exact snippet text changed) — re-reviewed against the new exact source lines (not just re-pointed), same underlying reasoning confirmed to still hold in every case. ~27 new entries added, covering: `remote-observability.ts`'s 25 non-trivial returns, documented as four recurring, individually-applied categories (Workflow/Deployment governance/config metadata; `RunRecord` fields already redacted at the REMOTE's own write time, per the same "engine redacts before persist" invariant this file already relies on for local runs; an explicit `ApprovalTask` field allowlist excluding `decision`; opaque remote-error passthrough) with THE REDACTION SEAM entry (`aart_remote_run`'s own `return result;`) spelled out in full, citing `execution.ts`'s own already-reviewed `getReportHandler` entry as the identical precedent; `remote-client.ts`'s 7 transport-layer helper returns, including an explicit reasoning note for WHY `fetchFromRemote`'s own raw-body return is safe despite carrying an arbitrary remote response (it's the transport layer, not a tool result — every REAL call site is independently reviewed, matching how `@aart/store`'s own reads are excluded from this lint's default scan for the identical "redaction is the CALLER's responsibility" reason); `tools/server.ts`'s one new boolean gate check. One small code change alongside the doc entries: `remoteErrorMessage`'s one data-carrying branch (`return body["error"]`) was reformatted onto its own line — it was folded onto its `if` guard's own line, which happens to make the lint's line-anchored `/^\s*return\b/` pattern miss it entirely; moved so it gets the same individually-reviewed treatment as every other data-carrying return in this new surface, not an accidental pass by pattern luck.

**Gates, from clean, run TWICE in full** (`pnpm run clean && check:tsconfig-refs && build && typecheck && typecheck:tests && lint:redaction && test`): **233 files / 2477 tests, all green, byte-for-byte identical across both complete from-clean runs** (this session's own 232/2433 pre-test-batch baseline + this session's net +1 file / +44 tests). `check:tsconfig-refs`: OK, the same 5 non-blocking informational notes as every prior session, both runs. `lint:redaction`: clean both runs — 261 reviewed suppressions (up from A58's 225 baseline: +36 net across this session's own fix-then-add sequence, see the redaction-lint closure paragraph above for the breakdown). Both full runs' logs swept for hidden failures (`grep -niE "error TS|FAIL|✗|UnhandledRejection|ELIFECYCLE"`) — zero matches either run; test-file/test counts identical between both runs (233/233, 2477/2477). ## 2026-07-13 — Wave 1 fix pass

### A63 — Wave 1 (D2b + V1) fix pass: GET /flagged-runs closed as a gating-intent MAJOR (it stayed open after GET /runs/GET /runs/:id were gated, defeating the point), GET /events documented + given a real ?limit= default/clamp, EventLogStore adapter conformance (negative-limit, occurredAt/id ordering), recordEvent's best-effort contract genuinely closed, plus doc/UX polish

John-approved, Stig-ratified fix pass on findings from Wave 1's three-perspective verification (cold auditor MAJOR + reviewer + real-binary e2e) of the merged `wave1/integration` branch (V1 event log, AMENDMENTS A61, + D2b remote reads, AMENDMENTS A62). Branch `wave1/integration`, HEAD `ddbf557` (the V1+D2b merge) going in. Targeted fixes only — no redesign, no Wave-2 features (no `deployment.pushed`, no approve-against-remote). Seven numbered fixes plus one confirm-only item, one commit each except where two fixes landed in the same files (noted below).

**FIX 1 — MAJOR, closed (completes John's own ratified "gate the run-read routes" intent).** `GET /runs`/`GET /runs/:id` were gated (D2b, AMENDMENTS.md A62) specifically because a run's full trace/inputs/outputs can carry secret-adjacent content — but `GET /flagged-runs` (`packages/server/src/http/server.ts:856-860`, → `listFlaggedRuns`, `packages/server/src/flags.ts:42`) returns the SAME full `RunRecord[]` shape, filtered server-side to failed+uncleared-flag runs, and stayed OPEN: an unauthenticated caller could still read every reclaim-exhausted/poison run's full trace through this one route alone, regardless of the other two being gated — the exact gap the cold auditor's MAJOR finding named. Fixed: `{ auth: (ctx) => requireDeployTokenIfConfigured(config, ctx, "read run data") }` added to its registration, byte-identical conditional semantics to its two siblings.

`GET /waiting-runs` (`server.ts:846`) was evaluated for the identical gating, per the fix's own instruction to decide by reading the actual return shape, not by name: `WaitStore.list()` (`packages/store/src/types.ts:82-95`) returns `Array<{runId, stepId, wait, createdAt}>` — read directly against `WaitConditionSchema`'s full 7-member discriminated union (`packages/types/src/wait.ts`), confirmed NONE of the seven variants (`approval`/`signal`/`timer`/`webhook`/`external_job`/`queue`/`manual`) carries anything beyond condition metadata (`taskId`/`name`/`correlationId`/`resumeAt`/`event`/`provider`/`jobId`/`queue`/`timeout`/`schemaVersion`) — no trace/inputs/outputs anywhere in the type. Left OPEN, with that evidence recorded inline at the route (`server.ts`) and in `DEPLOY.md`'s gating matrix note.

Dashboard closure: `listFlaggedRunsViaApi` (`packages/dashboard/src/api-client.ts:262-270`) called `getJson` with no `extraHeaders` at all — would have 401'd against a token-configured real server the moment an operator set `AART_DEPLOY_TOKEN`, exactly the shape D2a/D2b's own precedent already fixed for `listRuns`/`getRun`. Now threads `deployAuthHeaders` identically.

Tests (`packages/server/src/http/server.test.ts:1209-1290`): a new `describe("GET /flagged-runs — conditional deploy-token gating"...)` block mirrors the existing `GET /runs`/`GET /runs/:id` one exactly — 401 no header, 200 correct Bearer (real flagged/trace-bearing data returned), 401 wrong Bearer, 200 unconfigured; a new `describe("GET /waiting-runs — evaluated..., deliberately left open"...)` block proves it stays open even token-configured; `/flagged-runs` removed from the pre-existing "every OTHER GET route stays open" list (`server.test.ts:1140`, since it's no longer one — this existing test caught the regression immediately: ran RED the moment the route was gated, confirming the test suite's own value before being updated to match the new, intended invariant). `packages/dashboard/src/api-client.test.ts:504-519`: `listFlaggedRunsViaApi` positive (token-gated server, real data) + negative (no token, 401) cases added to the existing "newly-gated READ methods" describe block (renamed two → three).

**FIX 2 — closed, docs only, no code.** `GET /events` (V1 event log, AMENDMENTS.md A61) was entirely absent from `DEPLOY.md`'s gating-matrix "Open, always" row — both reviewer and tester flagged this as a real gap, not deliberate silence. Ruling (Stig): leave `/events` OPEN — `EventLogEntrySchema` (`packages/types/src/event-log.ts:41-56`) carries only run-lifecycle metadata (workflow id/version, status, timing, correlation ids) — no `trace`/`inputs`/`outputs` field exists on the type at all — the same open-always sensitivity tier as the already-open `/deployments`, and it's the activity-feed source the dashboard reads live. Added to the table + a dedicated note (verbatim, `DEPLOY.md`, "Gating matrix" section):

> **`GET /events` is open, not by omission (D2b/V1 fix pass, AMENDMENTS.md A63 FIX 2 — previously absent from this table entirely, a genuine doc gap, not a deliberate silence).** It exposes run-lifecycle METADATA only — workflow id/version, status, timing, and the ids involved (`runId`, `deploymentId`, `environmentId`, ...) — never a step's trace/inputs/outputs (`EventLogEntrySchema`, `@aart/types`' `event-log.ts`, has no such field). It sits in the same open-always sensitivity tier as `/deployments`/`/approvals`, not the gated tier `GET /runs`/`GET /runs/:id`/`GET /flagged-runs` occupy, and is the activity-feed source the dashboard reads live.

Every other place in `DEPLOY.md` that enumerated the gated-read tier as an exhaustive "two routes" (the matrix table's own cell, its explanatory note, and two mentions in "Ops limits") updated to three, now that FIX 1 added `GET /flagged-runs` — stale counts are exactly the doc-drift class root AMENDMENTS.md A58 FIX D ("make DEPLOY.md true") exists to close.

**FIX 3 — closed, two layers.** Route layer (`packages/server/src/http/server.ts`, new `parseEventsLimit`, `:303-329`; `DEFAULT_EVENTS_LIMIT`/`MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT`, `packages/server/src/config.ts:193-208`): `GET /events`'s `?limit=` parsing was `Number.isFinite(Number(limitParam))`, which admits negative and fractional values through untouched, and had no default/max at all — an absent `limit` meant "serialize the entire append-only log," unauthenticated (this route is deliberately open, FIX 2 above). Now: non-integer or negative values fall back to the default (100, chosen the same way `DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES` picked "generous headroom for the common case," not derived from an external constraint) rather than 400ing (matching this route family's existing loose `?status=`/`?workflowId=` idiom); a valid value clamps to `MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT` (1000); `0` is honored as an explicit "give me zero," distinct from absent.

Store layer (`packages/store/src/adapters/fs/events.ts:48-70`, `packages/store/src/adapters/sqlite/stores/events.ts:70-103`): the two adapters DIVERGED on a negative limit reaching them directly (not just through the now-fixed route) — fs's `Array.prototype.slice(0, -N)` means "drop the last N" (so a negative limit returned MORE rows, not fewer); sqlite's `LIMIT -N` means "unlimited" (SQLite's own documented behavior) — different wrong answers, not the same one. Both now independently guard: `limit < 0` → treated as `0` (the safe direction — matches FIX 2's "never expand exposure beyond what's asked" reasoning — never "unlimited," never slice's "drop the last N" meaning), agreeing with each other.

**FIX 4 — closed.** Neither `EventLogStore` adapter had a total order on equal `occurredAt` (ms precision — a tight burst, e.g. `aart_approve`'s own 3-event emission, `governance.ts`, can land on the same millisecond). fs (`events.ts:52`) fell back to `Array.prototype.sort`'s stability, i.e. whatever order `readdir()` happened to return the tied files in (filesystem-dependent, not a real order this store's contract promises); sqlite (`stores/events.ts:83`, pre-fix) had `ORDER BY occurred_at DESC` with no secondary key, an unspecified tie order. Both now break ties DESC on `id` as a secondary sort/`ORDER BY` key — mirroring the `(createdAt || id)` discipline already established elsewhere in this codebase (`pickLiveDeploymentRow`/`findCurrentVersion`, per AMENDMENTS.md A62's own deviation note).

**FIX 3+4 conformance proof** (`packages/store/src/conformance.ts:541-580`, the shared `events` describe block, runs automatically against both adapters): two new cases — a negative-limit case (append 3, `list({limit: -1})`, expect `[]`) and an equal-`occurredAt` tiebreak case (three fixed-id entries appended out of order, expect one specific `id`-DESC order back). **Verified directly that both FAIL against the pre-fix adapters before either fix landed** — `git stash push` of just the two adapter files (keeping the new tests), reran: 4 failures (2 tests × 2 adapters) — fs's negative-limit case returned 2 items (`slice(0,-1)`), sqlite's returned all 3 (`LIMIT -1` = unlimited), neither the fixed `[]`; both adapters' tiebreak case returned append order `[b, c, a]` instead of the fixed `[c, b, a]` id-DESC order. `git stash pop` restored the fix, reran clean — 116/116 across both conformance suites (fs + sqlite).

Route-level proof for FIX 3's default (`server.test.ts:739-774`): the only way a black-box HTTP test can prove "absent `?limit=` no longer means unlimited" is to seed MORE than the chosen default and confirm the response is actually capped — 105 events seeded directly via `store.events.append` (bypasses HTTP for setup; fast, ~50ms total), `GET /events` with no `?limit=` at all returns exactly 100. Plus: 5 malformed/negative values (`-5`, `abc`, `1.5`, `NaN`, `Infinity`) all fall back to the default (2 seeded events, all 2 returned, no 400); `?limit=0` explicitly returns `[]`. The upper clamp (`MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT`, 1000) is code-reviewed (`Math.min(n, MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT)`, a trivial one-liner) but NOT independently HTTP-proven at its exact boundary — seeding 1000+ real store rows for one assertion was judged disproportionate; flagged here rather than silently skipped.

**FIX 5 — closed.** `recordEvent`'s own doc comment (`packages/store/src/event-log.ts`) promises "never fails the primary operation" — every one of the ~21 real `await recordEvent(...)` call sites across mcp/server/cli relies on this, none wraps it in its own try/catch. But the `entry` object (including `now().toISOString()`, a caller-supplied, uncontrolled function) was constructed OUTSIDE the try/catch that only wrapped `store.events.append(entry)`. An `async function` body converts ANY synchronous throw into a rejected promise regardless of an internal try/catch elsewhere in the body — so a throwing `now()`/clock rejected the returned promise instead of being absorbed like every other failure mode, silently contradicting the contract. Fixed by moving entry construction inside the try block (`event-log.ts:52-63`) — `recordRunTerminalEvent` already wraps ITSELF for the identical shape of gap (its own `store.runs.get` read sits outside `recordEvent`'s try/catch); `recordEvent` now does the same for its own construction step.

New `packages/store/src/event-log.test.ts` (this module had no dedicated unit test file before — only exercised transitively through real call sites): 9 tests — normal append/id-generation/field-omission behavior, the pre-existing "a throwing append is swallowed" contract, the new "a throwing `now()` does NOT reject" case (`:65-71`, this fix's own proof) plus a "doesn't leak bad state into a later healthy call" case (`:74-81`), and `recordRunTerminalEvent`'s terminal-mapping/non-terminal-no-op/outer-try-catch coverage. **Verified directly that the two `now()`-throwing cases FAIL against the pre-fix code** — `git stash push` of just `event-log.ts` (keeping the tests), reran: both failed with `"promise rejected 'Error: clock broke' instead of resolving"`; `git stash pop` restored the fix, reran clean, 9/9. (One authoring bug caught and fixed along the way, not shipped: the test helper's first draft passed `recordEvent` the UNWRAPPED `events` sub-object instead of `{events}` — `Pick<AartStore, "events">`'s own required shape — which incidentally made every "healthy" case fail too, for the wrong reason (a `TypeError` inside `recordEvent`'s own try/catch, silently swallowed by the very contract under test, masking the helper bug rather than revealing it); caught by running the suite BEFORE assuming the tests were correct, fixed before this file was ever committed.)

**FIX 6 — closed, doc/description only, no behavior change.** `aart_remote_run --format model` (default) returns the bounded model-facing report (headline/gates/failures/artifact refs — verified against `redaction-lint-suppressions.ts:218`'s own "bounded, rendered fields" language); `--format markdown` additionally embeds the run's ENTIRE step trace verbatim. Verified directly before writing the doc fix: `renderers/markdown.ts:69-77`'s "## Full trace" section is `JSON.stringify(model.fullTrace, null, 2)`, and `report-model.ts:96`'s own `fullTrace: run.trace` confirms it's the raw `StepTrace[]` (real per-step inputs/outputs), not a summary — the code comments were already honest about this, but `AUTHORING.md`'s "Debugging a deployed workflow" section never surfaced it to a reader, and its own example commands never showed `--format` being used at all. Two sentences added (`AUTHORING.md`, after the "Redaction, stated precisely" paragraph) distinguishing the two formats' real size, so a user isn't surprised by markdown's own.

**FIX 7 — closed, optional/low-priority.** `aart --help`/`-h`/`help` fell through to `run()`'s own `default:` case (`packages/cli/src/cli.ts`) — the correct USAGE block WAS present (embedded in the JSON result's `usage` field) but wrapped in a false `{"ok":false,"error":"Unknown command..."}`, exit 1. Judged cheap enough to fix, not contorting the dispatch: `cli.ts`'s `run()` gained a `case "--help"/"-h"/"help"` (`:230-245`) returning `{ok:true, usage: USAGE}` (defense in depth for a direct `run()` caller); `bin.ts` (the real entry point) gained a pre-dispatch short-circuit (`:12-25`) mirroring its own PRE-EXISTING zero-arg special case exactly — plain USAGE to stdout, real `process.exit(0)`, bypassing `run()`/context construction entirely. **Verified end-to-end against the real built binary** (`packages/cli/dist/bin.js`, post-`pnpm run build`): `--help`/`-h`/`help` now print plain USAGE text at exit 0; `frobnicate` (genuinely unknown) and bare `aart` (zero args) are BYTE-IDENTICAL to their pre-fix behavior — no regression on either pre-existing path. `packages/cli/src/cli.test.ts:1319-1327`: `it.each` over the three aliases, proving `run()` itself no longer misclassifies them.

**CONFIRMED, no code change (governance.ts comment only).** The validate gate emitting TWO events (`workflow.validated` + `workflow.gate_passed`/`gate_failed`) on one call is intended taxonomy, not a duplicate-emission bug — distinct concepts ("a validation ran" vs "the validate gate passed/failed" in general, the way every other gate's outcome is reported). One-line comment added at the emission site (`packages/mcp/src/handlers/governance.ts:92-101`) so a future reader doesn't mistake this for a bug; `decideApprovalTask` (`packages/server/src/approvals.ts`) confirmed to have no equivalent dual-emission at all (it only ever decides humanReview/riskReview, never validate), so no second comment needed there.

**Deviations flagged, not silent:**
1. FIX 1 and FIX 3's route-layer half landed in the same files (`server.ts`/`config.ts`/`server.test.ts`/`api-client.ts`/`api-client.test.ts`) and share one commit — both touch `GET /events`'/`GET /flagged-runs`'s immediate neighborhood in the same route-registration block; splitting the diff by fix rather than by file was judged not worth the risk of a hand-split patch. Likewise FIX 3's store-layer half and FIX 4 share one commit (both touch the exact same two adapter `list()` methods plus the same conformance describe block) — the task's own RETURN spec already groups "FIX 3+4" together for this reason.
2. FIX 3's upper-bound clamp (`MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT` = 1000) is not independently HTTP-proven at its exact boundary — see FIX 3+4's own paragraph above.
3. FIX 7's full fix touches `bin.ts` in addition to `cli.ts`'s dispatch — the brief's own phrasing scoped this to "cli.ts's dispatch," but achieving the literally-requested behavior (plain stdout text, exit 0) for the REAL `aart` binary requires the entry-point file, not the dispatch function alone (`run()` is a pure function with no access to `process.stdout`/`process.exit`, callable from tests). Judged in scope: the `bin.ts` change is a 12-line mirror of an already-proven, pre-existing pattern in the same file (its own zero-arg case), not a new mechanism.

**Gates, from clean, run TWICE in full** (`pnpm run clean && pnpm run check:tsconfig-refs && pnpm run build && pnpm run typecheck && pnpm run typecheck:tests && pnpm run lint:redaction && pnpm test`): **236 files / 2552 tests, all green, byte-for-byte identical across both complete from-clean runs.** `check:tsconfig-refs`: OK, the same 5 non-blocking informational notes as every prior session, both runs. `lint:redaction`: clean both runs — **261 reviewed suppressions, UNCHANGED from A62's own final count** — this fix pass introduced zero new suppression-worthy call sites. Both full run logs swept for hidden failures (`grep -niE "error TS|FAIL|✗|UnhandledRejection|ELIFECYCLE"`) — zero matches either run. `migration-race.e2e.test.ts` (this repo's own highest flakiness-risk test class, spawns genuinely concurrent OS processes) — this session's changes touch the same adapters' `events` table read path that test's own `hasEventsTable` check + `store.events.append` call exercise, so it was run 3 additional times in isolation beyond the two full-suite passes (5 total observed passes) — clean every time, zero flakiness.

**Commits (local only, none pushed), all on `wave1/integration` on top of `ddbf557`:** `9e5df00` (FIX 1 + FIX 3 route layer), `0f5e75f` (FIX 3 store layer + FIX 4), `143725f` (FIX 2, docs), `4101722` (FIX 5), `2bed27c` (FIX 6, docs), `65ae263` (FIX 7), `efcdbff` (CONFIRM, comment only).

## 2026-07-13 — V2 Wave 2A: the activity feed, live

### A64 — V2 Wave 2A: the activity-feed dashboard view + live updates — `ApiClient.listEvents`, `GET /api/events` (backfill) + `GET /api/events/stream` (SSE broadcast), and `ActivityFeedPage.tsx`; parity id-routes for approvals/corrections/evals/deployments deliberately deferred, one with a genuine blocker found and flagged

John-ratified 2026-07-12 alongside the D2+V1 wave, "Wave 2 please" 2026-07-13. Branch `v2/activity-feed`, forked from `main`@`4100873` (the wave1/integration merge — D2b remote reads + V1 event log foundation, AMENDMENTS.md A61-A63). Product framing: "agent chat on the left, dashboard on the right, following live" — the visible payoff of the wave's VISIBILITY SYMMETRY intent. File-ownership boundary held throughout (two concurrent Wave-2 sibling sessions run in parallel on `aart watch`/packages/cli and approve-against-remote/packages/mcp): every file this session touched is under `packages/dashboard/**`, plus one narrow, flagged exception in `packages/governance/src/redaction-lint-suppressions.ts` (below) and this file.

**Foundation consumed, not built here:** V1's event log (AMENDMENTS.md A61, fix-passed by A63) already appends every lifecycle transition to the 17th `AartStore` member and serves it at the real server's `GET /events?since=&limit=` (open/unauthenticated — A63 FIX 2 — `{ events: EventLogEntry[] }`, newest-first). This session makes that log VISIBLE and LIVE in the dashboard; it adds no new event types and touches no write site.

**PART 1 — `ApiClient.listEvents(since?, limit?)` (`packages/dashboard/src/api-client.ts`).** Mirrors `listRuns`'s dual-implementation shape exactly. `createHttpApiClient`: GETs the real server's `/events`, attaching `deployAuthHeaders` on the same terms as `listRuns`/`getRun`/`listFlaggedRunsViaApi` even though `GET /events` stays open regardless of `deployToken` configuration (A63 FIX 2) — harmless, and keeps this client uniform against a future session that gates `/events` too. `createFakeApiClient`: `store.events.list({since, limit})`, a plain passthrough — deliberately NOT re-deriving `@aart/server`'s own `parseEventsLimit` default/clamp policy locally, since that policy exists to defend an unauthenticated HTTP-exposed route, not a same-process local store read (the same reasoning every other `list*` method on this fake client already follows — none of them impose an artificial cap either). Tests: fake-client store round-trip (ordering, since-is-inclusive, limit) plus two real-`@aart/server` tests (read-through, and — the one genuinely worth asserting explicitly — succeeding with NO `deployToken` configured against a token-gated server, proving this route really does stay open).

**PART 2 — dashboard routes (`packages/dashboard/src/server.ts`).** `GET /api/events` → `api.listEvents(since, limit)` → a bare `EventLogEntry[]` (matching every sibling list route's response shape in this file — `/api/environments`, `/api/deployments`, `/api/approvals`, `/api/corrections` — none wrap in an envelope object; the real server's own `{ events }` shape is unwrapped one layer down, inside `listEvents` itself). `GET /api/events/stream`: a read-only Server-Sent-Events broadcast of events NEW since the connection opened — writes SSE headers + `flushHeaders()` + an initial `:ok\n\n` comment, then a `setInterval` (default 1500ms, new `DashboardConfig.eventsStreamPollIntervalMs`, `config.ts` — overridable mainly so tests don't need multi-second real-time waits) polls `api.listEvents(cursor)` and writes each newly-observed event as `data: ${JSON.stringify(event)}\n\n`, oldest-of-the-batch first (so sequential client-side prepends land newest-on-top); `req.on("close")` clears the interval and ends the response. Never touches the real server's own ticker — purely additive reads through the existing `ApiClient` boundary, same single-instance-constraint respected as everything else in this file.

**The cursor mechanism, and a genuine self-review course-correction worth recording in full (not just the tidy final answer):** `EventLogStore.list`'s own `since` filter is INCLUSIVE (`occurredAt >= since`, both adapters), so a plain "occurredAt > cursor" comparison would silently drop same-millisecond ties (e.g. `aart_approve`'s own 3-event emission, A63 FIX 4) — `sentIdsAtCursor` tracks ids already sent at the exact cursor timestamp to handle that. The FIRST shipped design derived the starting cursor from a one-off async `listEvents(undefined, 1)` "seed" read before the first recurring tick, deliberately NOT marking that seed's own batch as already-sent (so a concurrent append racing the seed's own read wouldn't be silently swallowed). That design was committed, then questioned during self-review: is "seed doesn't mark-as-sent" actually sufficient under a real concurrent-append race, or just usually-fine? Investigated directly rather than assumed either way — reintroduced a fully-marking seed and ran the concurrent-append test (passed, but only because the store was still empty when that particular run's seed read fired — inconclusive); widened the race window with a temporary 80ms artificial delay inside the seed path to force the test's append to reliably land inside the seed's own in-flight read, then reran against the ACTUAL shipped design — still passed, confirming the asymmetric guard was already correct (the first recurring tick's own inclusive `since: cursor` query re-observes anything at that exact timestamp, and since `sentIdsAtCursor` was never populated by the seed, it gets broadcast). Not a bug fix, then — but simplified anyway: that correctness depended on a non-obvious two-kinds-of-ticks asymmetry a future reader would have to re-derive by the same case analysis. Replaced with one mechanism — `cursor` is anchored to `clock.nowIso()` captured SYNCHRONOUSLY right after the connection opens, no `await` between opening the stream and setting it, so no async read and therefore no race window exists AT ALL, provably by construction rather than by case analysis. One unified `tick()`, no `seed` parameter. All 115 backend tests green against the final version, including the exact concurrent-append scenario both designs were checked against (`server.test.ts`, "GET /api/events/stream opens... and broadcasts a newly-appended event... through the real composition path").

No redaction wrapper on either route — `EventLogEntry` carries only run-lifecycle metadata (id/type/occurredAt/summary + correlation ids), never trace/inputs/outputs (no such field exists on the schema at all), the identical reasoning A63 FIX 2 already applied to the real server's own `GET /events`. One redaction-lint suppression added (`packages/governance/src/redaction-lint-suppressions.ts` — a narrow, flagged exception to this session's own dashboard-only file-ownership boundary, purely additive, same precedent A62's own Deviation 1 set for an equivalent single-line necessary reach outside its nominal scope) for the poll tick's own `console.error`: individually justified — `err` is a read failure, never event/record data, and `EventLogEntry` has no secret-bearing field for this call site to have bypassed redaction on regardless.

Tests against a REAL `@aart/server` instance + real `startDashboard(createHttpApiClient(...))` over real HTTP (the standing imperative, and this file's own established "prove the router wiring, not a mock" bar): `GET /api/events` backfill; `GET /api/events/stream` opens and broadcasts a newly-appended event within a couple 30ms poll intervals (interval overridden for test speed only, production default unchanged at 1500ms); disconnect cleanup (destroying the client connection never crashes the dashboard, proven behaviorally — the process keeps serving fresh requests immediately after, past several poll intervals' worth of real time).

**PART 3 — `ActivityFeedPage.tsx` (`packages/dashboard/frontend/src/pages/`).** Backfills once on mount (`GET /api/events?limit=50`), then opens a same-origin `EventSource('/api/events/stream')` and prepends incoming events, deduping by id (a just-backfilled event and the stream's own first delivery can legitimately overlap — both routes independently read the same log). Each row: an icon+color by event-type FAMILY (green = `run.completed`/`workflow.approved`; red = `run.failed`/`workflow.gate_failed`; blue = `approval.*`/`workflow.gate_passed`/`validated`; gray = everything else), a relative timestamp, and a link built from `runId` first, `workflowId` as fallback (a workflow-version-level gate/approval event with no run yet — `event-log.ts`'s own documented case), unlinked otherwise. `classifyEvent`'s fallback (unrecognized type → gray) matters for real: `EventLogEntry.type` is deliberately not a closed enum (`event-log.ts`'s own header comment), so a future write site's new type string must still render sensibly, not break.

`router.tsx`: new `activity` `parseRoute` case, mirroring `waiting-runs`/`flagged-runs`/`approvals`'s own flat shape. `App.tsx`: new nav entry (`Rss` icon — `Activity` is already this sidebar's own Evaluation Suites entry), placed FIRST in the nav list — **a judgment call, not explicitly specified by the brief**, made given this slice's own repeated framing as the wave's visible payoff; trivially reorderable if wrong, flagged here rather than silently decided. New `MainContent` switch case.

Tests (`ActivityFeedPage.test.tsx`): backfill render (newest-first as returned, plus an explicit assertion that the constructed `EventSource` URL is the bare same-origin-relative `/api/events/stream` — this package's own "the SPA never knows the real server's address" invariant, header comment, applies to the stream exactly as much as to every `fetch()` call) and live-prepend-with-dedupe (redelivering the same id twice yields exactly one row — proven directly, not just implied by the implementation). jsdom has no `EventSource` implementation (verified directly — neither jsdom nor plain Node 22 expose a global one) — a minimal `MockEventSource` stand-in, stubbed via `vi.stubGlobal` the same way `test-support/mock-fetch.ts` already stands in for `global.fetch`.

Manually verified end-to-end in a real browser (a running dashboard, `createFakeApiClient` over a seeded fs store, one example of every documented event type plus a live append 4s after boot): correct colors/icons per family, correct relative timestamps, correct links — spot-checked via the rendered DOM's actual `href` attributes (`document.querySelectorAll('a')`), not just visual inspection, after an initial visual misread of which row a click landed on turned out to be tester error, not a code bug — the live event appeared at the top with the "Live" indicator lit, with no page reload.

**PARITY ID-ROUTES (V1 Slice 6) — deliberately deferred, not silently dropped.** The brief's own explicit fallback ("ship the feed even if you run low… note which parity routes you deferred") was exercised. Evaluated each of the four before deciding, not deferred by default:

- **Approvals — a genuine blocker found, not just deprioritized.** `GET /api/approvals` (`server.ts`) hardcodes `api.listApprovals("pending")` — it can never return an already-decided task. An `approval.decided` activity-feed event, by definition, points at a task that is no longer pending — building `/approvals/:id` today would mean shipping a link that reliably 404s for exactly the events it exists to serve. Flagged as a separate task (`task_fd445f45`, spawned this session) rather than fixed inline: fixing it means deciding between an optional `?status=` query param (preserving `ApprovalsPage.tsx`'s current pending-only default) and/or a new single-entity `GET /api/approvals/:id` + `ApiClient.getApproval`, a real design choice outside this slice's own scope.
- **Corrections/evals/deployments — not blocked (their list routes already return everything unconditionally, no status gate), but deferred anyway on time/risk budget.** Even at the brief's own "minimum" bar (route to the existing list page, highlight/scroll to the matching row — no new backend route needed), this touches three more existing, already-tested page files (`CorrectionsPage.tsx`, `EvalsPage.tsx`, `ProductionPage.tsx`) each needing its own re-verification, plus router/App wiring, plus widening `ActivityFeedPage.tsx`'s own `linkForEvent`. Judged better done as its own focused pass than squeezed into this session's tail end alongside the two-clean-full-gate-run requirement — the core feed (this slice's own explicit priority) is what got the full attention instead.

`linkForEvent` (`ActivityFeedPage.tsx`) links `runId`-then-`workflowId` only, today — the two routes that genuinely, robustly resolve; correlation-id-only events (deployment/eval/correction-only) render unlinked rather than pointing at a route that doesn't exist.

**Deviations flagged, not silent:**
1. Nav entry position (App.tsx, Activity Feed placed first) — a judgment call, see PART 3 above.
2. `packages/governance/src/redaction-lint-suppressions.ts` touched — a narrow, additive, individually-justified exception to this session's own dashboard-only file-ownership boundary (PART 2 above), required to keep `lint:redaction` green; no other file in that package touched.
3. `DashboardConfig.eventsStreamPollIntervalMs` is new public surface on a package whose own doc comments elsewhere (`config.ts`) mirror `@aart/server`'s "documented tunables with defaults" convention — added for the identical reason `clock`/`workerUrls` are already injectable there (test determinism), not exposed as a CLI/env-var knob anywhere; production behavior is unchanged (default 1500ms, exactly the ratified design's own specified interval).
4. `createFakeApiClient.listEvents` (the local/embedded `aart dev` topology) has no artificial page-size cap, while the real HTTP route defaults to 100 and hard-clamps at 1000 (`@aart/server`'s `parseEventsLimit`) — a deliberate, not accidental, asymmetry: the cap is a defense for an unauthenticated network-exposed route, not a same-process direct store read, and matches this fake client's own existing behavior for every other `list*` method (none of which impose a cap either). `ActivityFeedPage.tsx`'s own backfill fetch always sends an explicit `?limit=50` regardless, so this asymmetry has no practical effect on the shipped page.
5. One stale, already-resolved flagged task from a prior session (`task_75e1f39f`, "Add GET /events to DEPLOY.md's open-routes table") was found and dismissed in passing, with direct evidence (`DEPLOY.md:386` already carries A63 FIX 2's note) — not this session's own finding to claim credit for, noted here only because dismissing another session's flagged item deserves a paper trail.
